# Saints Row: The Third — Renderer Core / Render Pipeline Specification

**Prepared by:** SPEC TEAM (cleanroom reverse-engineering process)
**Phase:** 2, renderer-core pass (direct user request: "document the rendering of the game")
**Scope:** The Direct3D 9 device/renderer *core* — device creation, the engine's own render-command submission architecture, device-pointer identity, the vertex-declaration/skinning hand-off, the mesh draw-range chain's final consumer, and shader/texture binding — as distinct from the already-documented per-format specs (`.fxo_pc` shader wrapper, PEG textures, vertex/mesh layout, `.cefct_pc` VFX data). Primary target is `SaintsRowTheThird.exe` (Direct3D 9), matching every other spec in this project; the Direct3D 11 executable (`SaintsRowTheThird_DX11.exe`) is cross-checked only where a single, cheap check settles something (§13).
**Method:** Ghidra headless disassembly and decompilation of a disposable project copy (`tools/gp_render1`, robocopied from `tools/ghidra_projects`), anchored on two already-known facts from prior sessions (`HANDOFF.md` §27.6): the device-wrapper global `DAT_03171ac4` and its `+0x68`/`+0x6c` vertex/index-buffer creation slots, and the packed-handle draw-range decoders `FUN_00e72d00`/`FUN_00e72c60`/`FUN_00e73e80`. New anchors this pass: a whole-binary indirect-call census for the specific byte offsets of fourteen named `IDirect3DDevice9` methods (using the corrected `MOV reg,[base+disp]` → `CALL reg` idiom per `HANDOFF.md` §5/§27.4, not the naive `CALL [reg+disp]` form that undercounts), followed by manual disassembly/decompilation of every hit. The Direct3D 11 side reuses the already-imported, already-analysed project at `tools/gp_fxo_dx11` read-only, per `HANDOFF.md` §27.6.
**Cleanroom compliance:** No decompiled source lines, pseudocode, or internal-only variable/function names are quoted verbatim anywhere below — only function/data **addresses** (`FUN_00xxxxxx`, `DAT_0xxxxxxx`) are cited as evidence anchors, exactly as in every other spec in this project. Direct3D 9 and Direct3D 9Ex API names (`IDirect3DDevice9`, `IDirect3D9`, `CreateDevice`, `CreateDeviceEx`, `Reset`, `ResetEx`, `Present`, `BeginScene`, `EndScene`, `Clear`, `DrawPrimitive`, `DrawIndexedPrimitive`, `SetRenderState`, `SetSamplerState`, `SetTexture`, `SetVertexShader`, `SetPixelShader`, `SetVertexShaderConstantF`, `SetStreamSource`, `SetIndices`, `CreateVertexDeclaration`, `D3DPRESENT_PARAMETERS` and its member names, `D3DFMT_X8R8G8B8`, `D3DFMT_D24S8`, `D3DSWAPEFFECT_DISCARD`) are Microsoft's own public, documented COM interface — safe to cite freely, exactly like "Direct3D 9" itself and like the `CTAB`/D3D9-shader-bytecode citations in `spec-fxo-format.md`. A handful of short literal debug/log strings embedded in the binary (e.g. a thread name, a few boot-log lines) are cited verbatim as **data evidence** in the same spirit as this project's existing practice of citing filename literals, FourCCs, and Lua hook-name strings (`spec-fxo-format.md`'s `rfg-skybox-meteors.fxo_pc`, `spec-lua-bindings.md`'s `garage_populate`) — they are shipped string constants, not expressions of a proprietary algorithm, and no source code accompanies them.

**Confidence key** (as in prior specs): **CONFIRMED — disassembly**, **CONFIRMED — empirical**, **HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred**, **HYPOTHESIS — unconfirmed**, **OPEN / UNKNOWN**. **Clarification added 2026-09-25 (§17):** "CONFIRMED — empirical" is used in two senses across this document that should be read as distinct — validated against shipped/runtime game data (the sense used in most other specs), versus a static claim about the binary's own bytes that was independently re-checked byte-for-byte against the raw executable file rather than taken on a single Ghidra decompile's word (the sense in §3.6 and §6.4, both of which the orchestrator independently re-verified this way before this document was accepted). Both are genuinely empirical, but readers should not assume "empirical" always means "checked against live/save-file data" in this particular spec.

**Headline correction to the state this pass started from** (`HANDOFF.md` §27.6 described `DAT_03171ac4` as "almost certainly the top-level D3D9 device abstraction/wrapper"): it is more precise than that. `DAT_03171ac4`'s vtable-slot offsets match the **public `IDirect3DDevice9` COM vtable exactly** at every slot checked (§4.1) — it is a bare global holding the real device interface pointer, set directly from `CreateDevice`/`CreateDeviceEx`'s own output parameter (§3.5), not a custom-shaped wrapper class. The genuine engine-side "wrapper" the project's general lesson (§5/§27.4) predicted does exist, but it isn't a vtable reshuffle — it is a **cross-thread deferred command buffer**: most per-draw D3D9 calls (state changes, binds, both `Draw*` calls) are recorded by the game/logic thread into a growable opcode stream and physically executed later, on a dedicated thread, by a ~74-entry function-pointer dispatch table (§6). This is *why* naive vtable-slot scans undercounted so badly in earlier sessions: the real call sites are not inline in "normal" code at all, they sit in 74 tiny dedicated opcode-handler stubs reached only through one indirect, runtime-opcode-keyed table lookup.

---

## 1. Overview

Saints Row: The Third's PC renderer core, on the Direct3D 9 executable, has three structural layers, from bottom to top:

1. **The real Direct3D 9 (or 9Ex) device**, created once at startup (§3) and held directly in a small set of global pointer variables whose vtable-slot arithmetic matches the public COM interface layout exactly (§4).
2. **A deferred render-command buffer** ("RL" — almost certainly *Render Library*, per literal boot-log strings, §3.6/§6.1): the game/logic thread *records* abstract commands (opcode + fixed-size argument block) into a growable buffer; a dedicated thread named `rl_command_thread` (§6.1) *replays* each buffer strictly in order by reading its opcode and dispatching through a ~74-entry jump table (§6.4), each entry a tiny stub that reissues the real, public D3D9 call with the recorded arguments (§7).
3. **Resource creation and one-shot setup calls** (vertex/index buffers, textures, the vertex declaration, `Reset`) that go **directly** to the device pointers from step 1, bypassing the command buffer entirely — these are not per-frame, so they don't need deferred replay (§8).

This document covers all three layers to the depth reached this pass, plus the already-known vertex-declaration/skinning hand-off and mesh draw-range chain (cross-referenced, not re-derived) tied explicitly into where they sit relative to this architecture. Camera/view/projection matrix upload and the exact catalogue of render passes are only partially reached (§11–§12, honestly scoped as OPEN/partial) — this is flagged in the task brief as expected follow-up territory and treated that way here.

- Per-vertex attribute layout, the "Mesh" sub-block, and the vertex-declaration element table's on-disk source: `spec-vertex-format.md`, `spec-geometry-format.md`.
- VFX/particle **data** tables and their runtime consumer (emitter step/spawn, not the GPU draw call itself): `spec-effects-format.md`.
- Mesh group/draw-range array parsing and GPU upload (`FUN_00e71310`, `FUN_00482e00`/`FUN_00482f00`, the packed 16:16 draw-range handle decoders): `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.9 — cross-referenced in §9 below, not duplicated.
- Vehicle/cutscene camera **data** (`vehicle_cameras.xtbl`, `vehicle_group_cameras.xtbl`, cutscene camera sampler channels): `spec-tables-ui-controls.md`, `spec-cutscene-camera-format.md` — cross-referenced in §11, the runtime matrix-upload side is only partially connected to that data this pass.
- `.czn_pc` object-placement/property-stream interior remains **PARKED** per standing project rule; not opened during this investigation, and nothing found here bears on it.

## 3. Device Creation

### 3.1 Anchor and call site

`FUN_0047ba00` is the device-creation routine. It is called from `FUN_0047c150` (§3.6), which is itself the render-library bring-up routine called once during startup. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

### 3.2 API/library sequence

In order:

1. `LoadLibraryA` on the literal module name `d3d9.dll`, then `GetProcAddress` for the literal export name `Direct3DCreate9Ex`.
2. A capability/tier check (`FUN_00e98df0`) gates whether the Ex path is attempted; when it is not available or not selected, the code falls back to the **statically imported** `Direct3DCreate9` (the ordinary, non-Ex entry point, present in the import table rather than resolved dynamically).
3. Either call yields an `IDirect3D9`-shaped (Ex or non-Ex) interface pointer, stored in a per-instance field of the caller's object (not a documented global; the field is object-relative, offset `+0x194` by index arithmetic seen in the destructor `FUN_00477730`, §3.7).

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly** for the LoadLibrary/GetProcAddress/Direct3DCreate9Ex/Direct3DCreate9 sequence and the Ex-preferred-with-fallback shape; **HIGH CONFIDENCE** — not fully pinned to the exact register/argument choreography selecting one path over the other, because Ghidra's decompilation of this specific function carries several `unaff_*` pseudo-registers (a sign the decompiler lost track of part of the calling convention here) — the *existence* of the fallback and the *order tried* are solid, the *exact selection condition*'s wiring is not fully traced this pass.]

### 3.3 Adapter enumeration

Once the `IDirect3D9`/`IDirect3D9Ex` object exists, the code enumerates display adapters and modes entirely through the **public IDirect3D9 vtable**, matched byte-for-byte against Microsoft's own documented layout:

| Call | Vtable byte offset | Public method | Used for |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GetAdapterCount` | `0x10` | slot 4 | outer adapter loop bound |
| `GetAdapterModeCount` | `0x18` | slot 6 | inner display-mode loop bound |
| `EnumAdapterModes` | `0x1C` | slot 7 | collecting each mode's width/height |
| `CheckDeviceMultiSampleType` | `0x2C` | slot 11 | probing up to 64 candidate MSAA sample counts against `D3DFMT_X8R8G8B8` |
| `GetAdapterMonitor` | `0x3C` | slot 15 | resolving the adapter's monitor for windowed placement |

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]** Every one of these offsets matches the public `IDirect3D9` COM vtable's documented slot position exactly (slot index × 4 bytes) — a strong, cleanroom-safe cross-check, since the layout itself is Microsoft's published ABI, not this game's IP.

### 3.4 `D3DPRESENT_PARAMETERS`

The code builds a presentation-parameters block on the stack before calling `CreateDevice`/`CreateDeviceEx`. Matching field-by-field against the public struct's documented member order:

| Struct index | Field | Observed value | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | `BackBufferFormat` | `0x16` (22) | `D3DFMT_X8R8G8B8` |
| 3 | `BackBufferCount` | `2` | two back buffers (triple-buffered with the front buffer) |
| 4 | `MultiSampleType` | `0` | `D3DMULTISAMPLE_NONE` |
| 6 | `SwapEffect` | `1` | `D3DSWAPEFFECT_DISCARD` |
| 8 | `Windowed` | boolean, sourced from a caller-supplied settings block | windowed vs. fullscreen |
| 9 | `EnableAutoDepthStencil` | `1` (`TRUE`) | engine always requests an auto depth/stencil surface |
| 10 | `AutoDepthStencilFormat` | `0x4B` (75) | `D3DFMT_D24S8` |

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly — every value matches a real, named public D3D9 format/enum constant exactly, at the exact struct offset the public documentation gives it; this internal consistency across seven independent fields is itself strong corroboration.]** The `Windowed` flag (and several sibling flags read from the same caller-supplied block, at byte offsets `+0x11`/`+0x12`/`+0x13`/`+0x15`/`+0x16`/`+0x23` relative to that block) come from a settings/config structure passed into this routine by its caller — **OPEN**: the exact source of that structure (a graphics-options table, a command-line override, or a saved-settings blob) was not traced this pass; it is plausibly related to the graphics-option `.xtbl` tables already catalogued in `spec-tables-environment.md`/`spec-tables-ui-controls.md`, but that link is not confirmed.

### 3.5 `CreateDevice` / `CreateDeviceEx` and the resulting device pointers

The code branches on whether an adapter-monitor-group value is present: absent, it calls the plain `IDirect3D9::CreateDevice` (vtable `+0x40`, slot 16); present, it calls `IDirect3D9Ex::CreateDeviceEx` (vtable `+0x50`, slot 20) on a **separate** `IDirect3D9Ex`-shaped pointer. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for both vtable slots matching their public, documented positions.]**

On success (return value `>= 0`), **two** globals are assigned directly from the call's own results:

```
DAT_03171ac4 = <device pointer>       ; the base IDirect3DDevice9-shaped interface
DAT_03171ac8 = <second pointer>       ; only non-null on the Ex path
```

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly, at the exact call site — this is the device's construction, not a later copy.]** Immediately after, the code calls the public `D3DPERF_SetOptions` (a documented PIX/D3DPERF instrumentation API, safe to cite by name) with argument `1`.

### 3.6 Bring-up order and literal boot-log evidence

`FUN_0047c150` performs the renderer bring-up in this exact order, each step immediately preceded by a distinct literal debug string logged through `FUN_00e48f40`/`FUN_00e48f50` (a matched enter/exit log-scope pair):

1. Literal string **`"RL: create d3d device"`**, then a call to `FUN_0047ba00` (§3.1–§3.5).
2. Literal string **`"RL: initialize thread"`**, then a call to `FUN_00475090` (§6.1) — which itself spawns the deferred-command replay thread.
3. Literal string **`"RL: init d3d mempool"`**, then a call to `FUN_00475720` — allocation of the memory pool the deferred command buffers are carved from.

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly + empirical (the literal strings sit immediately before their matching call in program order, in all three cases).]** The `"RL:"` prefix is consistent across every one of these lines and also appears in a fourth, standalone string, **`"RL: init command"`**, logged inside `FUN_00e49250` (the renderer-library init routine documented from the shader-loading side in `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.2) — strong, if informal, evidence that "RL" is the engine's own name for this whole subsystem (most plausibly *Render Library*). This is cited as data evidence only (a shipped string constant), not as an assertion about internal source structure beyond what the string itself says.

### 3.7 Device teardown

`FUN_00477730` is the render-device teardown/destructor. Among other cleanup (releasing render targets, depth/stencil surfaces, textures, and several small object pools), it releases the `IDirect3D9`/`IDirect3D9Ex` object at its own `+0x194`-indexed field and explicitly zeroes `DAT_03171ac4` to `0` after releasing the device object reached through the same object's `+0x674`-chained pointer. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

## 4. The Device-Pointer Globals

Three distinct globals hold device-shaped COM pointers, used in different parts of the renderer. All three were checked against the public `IDirect3DDevice9`/`IDirect3DDevice9Ex` vtable layout and match it exactly at every slot exercised.

### 4.1 `DAT_03171ac4` — the base device pointer

Set directly by `CreateDevice`/`CreateDeviceEx` (§3.5); zeroed on teardown (§3.7). Confirmed call-site vtable offsets, cross-checked against the public interface's documented slot numbers:

| Offset | Public method | Slot | Confirmed at |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x40` | `Reset` | 16 | `FUN_0047c800` (device reset/resize path, §5) |
| `0x5C` | `CreateTexture` | 23 | `FUN_00472710` (texture creation helper) |
| `0x68` | `CreateVertexBuffer` | 26 | `FUN_00482f00` (already known, `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.9.2) |
| `0x6C` | `CreateIndexBuffer` | 27 | `FUN_00482f00` (same function, index-buffer branch) |
| `0x8C` | `ColorFill` | 35 | `FUN_00472710`/`FUN_00472f40` |
| `0x90` | `CreateOffscreenPlainSurface` | 36 | `FUN_00472f40`/`FUN_00472710` (texture-load fallback path) |
| `0x158` | `CreateVertexDeclaration` | 86 | `FUN_00476ca0` (already known, §8) |

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly, all rows.]** `DAT_03171ac4` has exactly **14 static references in the whole image** (an unusually small, fully-enumerable set for a variable this central — every one was individually decompiled this pass); none of the 14 is a per-draw state-set or draw call, confirming that this global is used for **resource creation and device-level lifecycle calls only**, never for the deferred per-frame command stream (§6).

### 4.2 `DAT_03171ac8` — the Ex-specific device pointer

Set in the same `CreateDevice`/`CreateDeviceEx` success path (§3.5), but **only non-null when the Ex device-creation branch was taken**. Its one confirmed call site uses vtable offset `0x210` (slot 132), which is **not** a valid offset on the base `IDirect3DDevice9` (which ends at slot 118, offset `0x1D8`) — it lands exactly on `IDirect3DDevice9Ex::ResetEx`, the 14th method `IDirect3DDevice9Ex` adds beyond the base interface. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly — the offset match to a documented Ex-only method, at a position past the base interface's own end, is a strong structural proof that `DAT_03171ac8` genuinely is the Ex-flavoured interface pointer to the same device, not an unrelated object.]** `FUN_0047c800` (§5) branches on whether `DAT_03171ac8` is null to decide whether to call the plain `Reset` (on `DAT_03171ac4`) or `ResetEx` (on `DAT_03171ac8`) — exactly the pattern the public API requires (an `IDirect3DDevice9Ex` object must be reset with `ResetEx`, not `Reset`).

### 4.3 `DAT_03171b68` — the replay-thread's local device pointer

A **third**, separately-named global, set **not** at device creation but inside the deferred-command replay driver itself (§6.3): `DAT_03171b68 = <device pointer>` is the very first statement of `FUN_0049dde0`'s per-buffer-descriptor setup, executed on the dedicated `rl_command_thread`. Every one of the ~74 opcode-handler stubs (§7) that need the device reads it from `DAT_03171b68`, never from `DAT_03171ac4` directly. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]** **HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred:** this almost certainly holds the *same runtime pointer value* as `DAT_03171ac4` (there is only ever one device), kept as a separate global purely so the render-command-buffer module (a distinct compiled unit from the device-creation code) has its own copy without a cross-module dependency; this was not proven by tracing an explicit `DAT_03171b68 = DAT_03171ac4`-shaped assignment, so it is not marked CONFIRMED.

### 4.4 `DAT_0351f8f8` — a render-context object, not a fourth device alias, and NOT device-vtable-shaped

A fourth global, `DAT_0351f8f8`, is dereferenced with the *same* vtable-call idiom at offsets matching `GetRenderTarget` (`0x98`, slot 38) and `Clear` (`0xAC`, slot 43) — but it is also used with plain (non-virtual) field access at offset `+0xC0`, and its `+0x50`-relative field is used by two of the `Draw*` opcode handlers themselves to set a status/dirty bit (`+0x4`, bit `0x80`) on a device-lost condition (§7.3) rather than to issue a device call. **DOWNGRADED 2026-09-25 following an external advisory review (§17):** the pass's original "vtable mirrors several D3D9 device slots" framing is not just unconfirmed but appears actively **contradicted** by this document's own §16.1 data — `DAT_0351f8f8`'s vtable slot `+0x28` is called there with **zero arguments**, but that offset on the real public `IDirect3DDevice9` vtable is `GetDeviceCaps` (one argument); under the `__thiscall` callee-cleanup convention this project's disassembly consistently uses elsewhere, a genuine `IDirect3DDevice9`-shaped call at that slot with the wrong argument count would corrupt the stack. This is the same small-displacement-offset coincidence already on record as a general failure mode (`HANDOFF.md` §27.4) — the `0x98`/`0xAC` matches noted here are now judged coincidental too, not confirmed. **Corrected characterisation: `DAT_0351f8f8` is an engine-side singleton object with its own, distinct vtable** (a scope/lock/profiling-bracket pattern, per its `+0x24`/`+0x28` begin/end use in §16.1–§16.2), not a pass-through D3D9 device wrapper. **Class identity RESOLVED 2026-09-28 via RTTI (§18.10): the object is an instance of `rl_renderer`** — its constructor/destructor pair (`FUN_00e38350`/`FUN_00e38400`) installs vtable literal `0x0125a97c`, and the Microsoft C++ RTTI Complete Object Locator sitting immediately before that vtable (`0x0125a97c − 4`) decodes to the mangled type name `.?AVrl_renderer@@`. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + RTTI structure decode.]** **Still OPEN:** the object's full field layout and its exact relationship (if any) to `DAT_03171ac4`/`ac8`/`b68`; see §18.10 for a real complication found while resolving this (many of the class's own vtable slots resolve to one shared address, not yet explained).

## 5. Device Reset / Resize

`FUN_0047c800` handles device loss/resize (window-mode change, monitor change, or an explicit resize request). Structure, in order: release cached render targets/surfaces held at several small fixed offsets of its parameter object; call `GetMonitorInfoA`/`MoveWindow` when a fullscreen-monitor flag is set; branch on `DAT_03171ac8` to call `Reset` (via `DAT_03171ac4`, offset `0x40`) or `ResetEx` (via `DAT_03171ac8`, offset `0x210`, §4.2); on success, recreate the auto depth/stencil-dependent state and several `CreateTexture` (offset `0x5C`) calls for what are almost certainly shadow-map or render-target-sized textures, sized from fields of the reset parameters. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for the call structure and offsets; the exact purpose of each recreated texture (shadow map vs. some other render target) is HYPOTHESIS — not traced to a consumer this pass.]**

## 6. The Deferred Render-Command-Buffer Architecture

This is the central renderer-core finding of this pass, and the answer to the task's "per-frame render loop" question: there isn't a single per-frame function that walks visible objects and calls D3D9 directly in the ordinary sense. Instead, D3D9 calls are **recorded** as they're decided and **replayed later, on a different thread**, through an opcode dispatch table.

### 6.1 The render-command thread

`FUN_00475090` is called once, from the bring-up sequence (§3.6, immediately after device creation), and spawns a dedicated thread via the generic thread-creation wrapper `FUN_00db6920`, with the entry point set to `FUN_0049dde0` (§6.3) and the literal thread name **`"rl_command_thread"`**. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly — the thread name is a literal argument at the exact `FUN_00db6920` call site, not an inference.]** The same function also allocates the initial write-side command buffer (§6.2) and records the calling (game/logic) thread's ID into `DAT_02d024c8`.

### 6.2 Recording side (producer)

A "reserve space" helper, `FUN_0049c890`, has **58 distinct callers**, almost all clustered in the address range `0x00475xxx`–`0x004a9xxx` — a family of small "Record*"-shaped wrapper functions (not individually named/enumerated this pass) that each: (1) call the reserve helper to ensure the growable buffer has room, growing it via a callback (`FUN_00475000`, itself stored at a fixed slot `DAT_02d024c4`) if not; (2) write an opcode dword followed by a fixed number of argument dwords at the current write cursor (`DAT_02d024c0`, bounded by `DAT_02d024bc`); (3) advance the cursor past the record. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for the mechanism and the 58-caller census; the individual mapping of each of the 58 callers to a specific abstract "RecordXxx" operation was not exhaustively enumerated this pass — OPEN, a natural follow-up.]** At least nine distinct such producer functions were identified by their shared use of the buffer-descriptor globals (`FUN_004752c0`, `FUN_00475090`, `FUN_00475190`, `FUN_00475200`, `FUN_00475270`, `FUN_00475320`, `FUN_00478490`, `FUN_0047e750`, `FUN_004a2e30`).

**Correction 2026-09-25 (§16.1, sharpened by an external advisory review, §17): `FUN_0049c890` itself is NOT a generic, arbitrary-opcode reserve/write primitive — its own body hardcodes one fixed opcode-`3` write with a fixed payload address into a *third*, separate buffer (`DAT_02e5d630`-family), i.e. it is itself one specific instance of the reserve+write+advance pattern, dedicated to posting a fixed cross-thread notification.** This does not overturn the 58-caller census as a reference count, but it means "calls `FUN_0049c890`" only demonstrates "wants to post this one fixed notification" — it is **not**, by itself, evidence that a caller writes an arbitrary opcode of its own choosing (§16.1 confirmed this the hard way: two callers that satisfied this predicate turned out to write unrelated device-state opcodes, not `DrawIndexedPrimitive`). The better candidate for the *actual* generic, arbitrary-opcode record primitive is `FUN_00473770` (next paragraph) — **OPEN**: whether every one of the other ~50+ producer wrappers routes through `FUN_00473770`, through some other shared primitive, or inlines its own write directly, was not determined this pass.

Separately, `FUN_00473770` was observed being called with a small integer opcode literal (`0x17`, `0x18`) and a pointer to a small argument block, from inside the device-reset path (§5) — consistent with being (or wrapping) a genuinely generic record primitive, called directly with an already-known opcode rather than through one of the named per-operation wrappers. ~~**A follow-up should treat `FUN_00473770` as the primary candidate for "the" generic emit primitive, not `FUN_0049c890`.**~~

**Correction 2026-09-28 (§18.2): `FUN_00473770` is REFUTED as the generic emit primitive too.** Full decompilation shows it is a per-material/technique state-resolve-and-apply routine (reads cached colour/parameter fields, calls a texture-stage helper four times, issues two direct virtual calls) with no buffer-cursor read, check, write, or advance anywhere in its own body; its only connection to the command buffer is two hops removed, through its own callee `FUN_004755b0` (itself a fixed opcode-`0xA` writer). **There is no single generic reserve/write/advance function in this binary at all** — every "Record*" wrapper implements the same short compiled idiom independently, each hardcoded to its own opcode literal, argument count, and record size (§18.2 for the full mechanism, and §18.3 for how this let `RecordDrawPrimitive`/`RecordDrawIndexedPrimitive` themselves finally be found mechanically). The `0x17`/`0x18` literals `FUN_00473770` was seen called with, two paragraphs above, were re-examined directly this pass (the exact call sites, `0x0047cd7c`/`0x0047cdb1` inside `FUN_0047c800`): they are **not** opcodes at all. Each is `FUN_00473770`'s own third argument, pushed last/closest to the call; its first argument (the "this"-shaped register value at the call site) is a field read from `DAT_0351f8f8`'s own `+0xc0`, and its second argument is a pointer to a small on-stack structure freshly filled with a single repeated float value — none of that resembles a command-buffer opcode-plus-argument-block call in any way. The original "small integer opcode literal" reading of this call site was simply wrong; withdrawn here rather than left standing.

### 6.3 The replay driver

`FUN_0049dde0` is the thread body run by `rl_command_thread`. Structure:

1. Store the device pointer into `DAT_03171b68` (§4.3) and issue one `CreateQuery` (vtable `+0x1D8`, slot 118) for a synchronization query object.
2. Loop forever: wait for the next signal (a semaphore/event wrapper, `FUN_00fc0c80`); check a shutdown flag; otherwise pull the next queued command-buffer descriptor from a small ring array (base `DAT_033938a0`-adjacent, stride `0x18` = 24 bytes, indexed by a 16-bit slot number packed into `DAT_0339389c`); reset the **read cursor** global `DAT_02e5d650` to the buffer's start and its end-of-data pointer to start + length; record the current (render) thread ID into a diagnostic global (`DAT_02e5d658`).
3. Inner loop: while the read cursor is before the end pointer, read one opcode dword directly from the cursor (`*DAT_02e5d650`); **if the opcode exceeds `0x49` (73 decimal), stop** — the bound-check literal; otherwise call through a fixed function-pointer table (base address `0x013509F8`, §6.4) indexed by the opcode, with **no explicit arguments** — every handler independently re-reads the current cursor position itself (§7).
4. Loop back to step 2 for the next queued buffer.

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly, every step above, including the exact bound-check literal and the table base address (cross-checked against the table's own natural end, §6.4).]** This is a classic single-producer/single-consumer deferred command-queue pattern, common in engines of this era to decouple game-logic-thread work from GPU submission.

### 6.4 The opcode dispatch table

A flat array of **74 function pointers** (opcodes `0`–`73` inclusive, matching the driver's own `> 0x49` bound-check exactly) sits at virtual address **`0x013509F8`**, ending at `0x01350B1C`; the dword immediately after (`0x01350B20`) is unrelated small-integer metadata, not a table entry, marking the table's natural end independently of the bound check. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + empirical — every one of the 74 dwords in this range is a valid, instruction-aligned code address; the table's measured end coincides exactly with the driver's own bound-check constant.]** A recurring filler entry, `FUN_004bf550` (a single-instruction stub — its very first byte is a bare return), occupies **exactly 16 of the 74 slots** (orchestrator re-count directly against the raw table bytes, correcting the pass's own "at least 17") — almost certainly the "reserved/unimplemented opcode" placeholder. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for the stub's own triviality; CONFIRMED — empirical for the exact count; HIGH CONFIDENCE that its role is "unused slot" given how many slots point at the identical trivial stub.]**

## 7. Confirmed Opcode Handlers

Each populated table slot points to a small (typically 16–112-byte), self-contained routine that: reads the current record pointer from `DAT_02e5d650`; reads its own fixed number of argument dwords starting at record offset `+0x4` (the opcode tag itself occupies `+0x0`, already consumed by the driver); issues exactly one call through the device pointer at `DAT_03171b68` (or checks a device-lost/pending flag on `DAT_0351f8f8+0x50` and skips the call, §4.4, when set); advances the global cursor by `4 + 4·N` bytes where `N` is that D3D9 method's own real, public parameter count (excluding the implicit `this`); and returns.

The following handlers were individually disassembled and confirmed against the public API's own documented parameter count and vtable slot — the argument-count-to-record-size arithmetic below is a second, independent confirmation on top of the vtable-offset match, since both must agree with the *same* public method signature:

| Table index (opcode) | Handler address | Public method | Vtable offset | Params (excl. `this`) | Record size (bytes) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 5 | `0x0049C9F0` | `BeginScene` | `0xA4` | 0 | 4 |
| 6 | `0x0049CA10` | `EndScene` | `0xA8` | 0 | 4 |
| 7 | `0x0049ca30` | `Present` (all params `NULL`; §19.1) | `0x44` | 4 | 8 |
| 8 | `0x0049caa0` | `Present` (parameterised: source rect + dest window; §19.1) | `0x44` | 4 | 32 |
| 9 | `0x0049CB00` | `SetRenderTarget` | `0x94` | — | — |
| 11 | `0x0049CB60` | `Clear` | `0xAC` | — | — |
| 18 | `0x0049CFE0` | `SetRenderState` | `0xE4` | 2 | 12 |
| 19 | `0x0049D010` | `SetSamplerState` | `0x114` | 3 | 16 |
| 40 | `0x0049D560` | `DrawPrimitive` | `0x144` | 3 | 16 |
| 42 | `0x0049D650` | `DrawIndexedPrimitive` | `0x148` | 6 | 28 |

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly, all rows.]** `SetRenderTarget` and `Clear`'s exact record sizes were not individually measured this pass (their handlers were located via the whole-binary offset census, §Method, but not fully disassembled byte-for-byte) — safe to treat as CONFIRMED for *which opcode calls which method*, OPEN for their exact record layout.

`DrawIndexedPrimitive`'s handler (opcode 42, `0x0049D650`) is, to date, **the only place in this project's entire investigation history where a fully-decoded call chain terminates in the actual public `DrawIndexedPrimitive` COM call** — it pushes six argument dwords read from the record (`+0x4` through `+0x18`) directly onto the stack in the exact order the public signature requires (`PrimitiveType, BaseVertexIndex, MinVertexIndex, NumVertices, startIndex, primCount`), then calls through vtable offset `0x148`. This closes the "where does the draw actually happen" question the task posed: the answer is *not* inside `FUN_00e72d00`/`FUN_00e72c60`/`FUN_00e73e80` (those decode a packed draw-range handle into vertex-range bounds — a **`getVertexRange`**-shaped helper, not a draw call, per `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.9.5, cross-referenced not re-derived) — it is inside this one small opcode-42 stub, reached only through the deferred command buffer, with its actual arguments supplied by whatever producer code recorded the opcode-42 command in the first place. **That producer-side call site was not identified in this pass, but IS identified in a later pass (§18): the writer function is `FUN_004764d0` (`RecordDrawIndexedPrimitive`), and a complete real caller chain reaching it from a resolved mesh draw-range record is traced in §18.6/§18.7 — see §14 item 1 for the current, only-partially-open state.**

Both `Draw*` handlers additionally check a device-status byte at `0x03395EA4`; if set, instead of drawing they set bit `0x80` of a dword at `DAT_0351f8f8+0x50+0x4` and skip the call — a device-lost/paused guard. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

## 8. Vertex Declaration and the Vertex-Attribute Hand-off (Cross-Reference, Not Deferred)

`FUN_00476ca0` (already known, `HANDOFF.md` §27.6) builds the D3D9 vertex declaration element array and calls `CreateVertexDeclaration` (vtable `+0x158`) **directly on `DAT_03171ac4`**, immediately, at the point the call is made in code — **not** through the command buffer of §6. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly — the call site was individually re-disassembled this pass (see the calibration script's raw output) and shows the ordinary `MOV reg,[base+0x158]` / `CALL reg` idiom operating on the base device pointer directly, with no cursor/opcode-table involvement.]** This is consistent with vertex declarations being a *resource*, created once (or on demand, out of band) rather than per-draw, matching the pattern established in §4.1 for buffer/texture creation. The whole-binary census this pass found exactly 3 sites using this exact vtable offset in the indirect-call idiom: `FUN_00476ca0` itself, plus two further sites (`FUN_00f3a6d0` and one further unlabeled address in the same `0xf3xxxx` module) not otherwise investigated this pass — ~~**OPEN**, plausible candidates for a second, DX11-adjacent or debug-tool vertex-declaration path, not confirmed.~~ **REFUTED, 2026-09-29** (`spec-vertex-format.md` §12.10.4, a follow-up chasing this exact lead for the tree/zone layout-code question): both sites take **zero explicit arguments** where the public `CreateVertexDeclaration` needs two, and the containing `0xf3xxxx` region is unambiguously **Wwise audio middleware**, not a renderer — a directly adjacent sibling function in the same region calls the literal Wwise API symbol `AK::MemoryMgr::Malloc` under a critical section, and three more siblings share one device/capability-negotiation body template. This is a fresh instance of `WALLS.md`'s own catalogued "coincidental vtable-slot offset" failure mode (same shape as `DAT_0351f8f8`'s `0x98`/`0xAC` and the OpenSSL-bignum `0xd0c890`/`0xd0e6c0` pair, §12.1 above). There is no second vertex-declaration path here.

Semantic tables `0x01351d58`/`0x01351c98`/`0x01351d10` and the full per-channel decode are documented in `spec-vertex-format.md`; not repeated here.

## 9. The Mesh Draw-Range Chain and Where It Meets the Command Buffer

`spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.9 already traces, from the data/GPU-upload side: `FUN_00e71310` (group/draw-range array parser) → `FUN_00482e00`/`FUN_00482f00` (renderer-class construction and full GPU buffer upload via `DAT_03171ac4`'s `+0x68`/`+0x6c` slots, §4.1) → the packed 16:16 draw-range handle decoders `FUN_00e72d00`/`FUN_00e72c60`/`FUN_00e73e80`, the latter called from a vtable slot at `FUN_00b138a0`. That section's own conclusion (§11.9.5) is that these three functions decode a handle into a **vertex-range** (`getVertexRange`-shaped: lowest/highest vertex index), not a draw call.

This pass adds the other end of the chain: the actual `DrawIndexedPrimitive` call (§7) is reached only through the deferred command buffer's opcode 42, whose six arguments must ultimately derive from a resolved mesh/group/draw-range (index count, base vertex, index-buffer start) — almost certainly downstream of `FUN_00e73e80`'s call site (`FUN_00b138a0`) or a sibling in the same class hierarchy, since that is the only place in the project's whole investigation history a draw-range handle is read at actual render time. **HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred from the two chains' natural continuation; not directly traced this pass** — the specific producer function that (a) calls `FUN_00e73e80` or a sibling to resolve a draw range, and (b) then calls one of the "Record*" wrapper functions of §6.2 to emit the opcode-42 command, was not identified. This is the second-highest-value OPEN item for follow-up (§14 item 2), and closing it would fully connect "mesh on disk" to "triangles issued to the GPU."

## 10. Shader and Texture Binding

`spec-fxo-format.md` §6.4–§6.5 already documents, from the shader-loading side: `CreateVertexShader`/`CreatePixelShader` (vtable `0x16C`/`0x1A8`) called once per loaded shader file via small wrappers (`0x004A8790`/`0x004A8810`), and a per-file "shader object" (init `0x0048EFE0`, vtable `0x012A1254`) whose bind method `0x00474890` "caches the (object, pass index) pair" and switches technique/pass — that is resource creation and technique selection, not per-draw state.

This pass confirms, independently, that the actual **per-draw** binding calls — `SetVertexShader` (opcode landing at `0x0049D398`), `SetPixelShader` (`0x0049D3C8`), `SetTexture` (two handlers near `0x0049D4F0`/`0x0049D533`), `SetSamplerState` (opcode 19, §7, plus a second handler near `0x0049D4D9`), `SetVertexShaderConstantF` (two handlers near `0x0049D1E5`/`0x0049D231`), and `SetPixelShaderConstantF` (two handlers near `0x0049D2C5`/`0x0049D311`) — are **all** opcodes in the *same* §6 dispatch table, in the same tight address range as the confirmed `Draw*`/`Clear`/`SetRenderState` handlers. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, for the offset match and table-region placement of every one of these; the individual byte-for-byte handler bodies were not all fully disassembled the way §7's worked examples were.]** This means material/technique binding, in this engine, funnels through the exact same deferred producer/consumer pipeline as everything else in §6 — there is no separate, more-direct "immediate mode" path for shader/texture state that bypasses the command buffer. The exact glue connecting the `.fxo_pc`-side "shader object" bind method (`0x00474890`) to the specific "Record*" producer wrappers that emit these opcodes was **not** traced this pass — **OPEN** (§14 item 3). **Traced for one concrete producer in §22: `rl_static_material`'s own technique-select method reaches directly into the per-file shader-object's `+0x14` role-select method (`0x0048F7C0`, exact vtable-address match to this section's own citation), via a fixed material-category/tier/variant table lookup (`FUN_00e4a180`) rather than a name-hash.**

## 11. Camera, View and Projection Matrices — Partial, Mostly OPEN

The whole-binary census (§Method) found 14 indirect-call sites at the `SetTransform` vtable offset (`0xB0`, the **fixed-function** transform-state slot), clustered in several different modules (`0x0047xxxx`, `0x005dexxx`, `0x0093xxxx`, `0x00e70700` in the mesh/vehicle module, `0x00f5baxx`, `0x00f87xxx`) rather than inside the §6 opcode table. **HYPOTHESIS:** since this engine is confirmed shader-based (vertex/pixel shaders loaded and bound per §10, `vs_3_0`/`ps_3_0` per `spec-fxo-format.md` §3), a programmable-pipeline draw would normally ignore the legacy fixed-function transform state entirely; these `SetTransform` call sites more plausibly belong to a secondary, fixed-function-pipeline consumer — candidates include debug/wireframe overlays, a Bink video-playback quad, or 2D/UI compositing — rather than general 3D camera setup. This was **not confirmed** this pass.

The two `SetVertexShaderConstantF` opcodes (§10) are the far more likely real path for uploading view/projection matrices to shader constant registers, consistent with a standard D3D9 shader-based renderer of this era. **OPEN:** the producer-side function that computes a view/projection matrix and calls the corresponding "Record*" wrapper (to emit one of those two opcodes) was not identified this pass — this is the natural place a follow-up connecting to `spec-tables-ui-controls.md`'s `vehicle_cameras.xtbl`/`vehicle_group_cameras.xtbl` and `spec-cutscene-camera-format.md`'s camera sampler should start, but that connection is not made here. Recorded as an honest gap rather than guessed at, per this project's standing policy (`HANDOFF.md` §27.8).

## 12. Render Passes and Render Targets — Enumeration Only, Mostly OPEN

`SetRenderTarget` opcode-table calls (§7, opcode 9) exist inside the deferred command buffer, but the whole-binary census also found **12 distinct `SetRenderTarget`-shaped indirect-call sites outside the command-buffer region**, spread across clearly separate modules: `0x0047c1xx` (inside the device-reset path, §5 — almost certainly restoring the primary back-buffer/depth-stencil target after a resize, not a "pass"), `0x00619bxx`/`0x0061b7xx`, `0x00d0cxxx`/`0x00d0exxx`, `0x00ddd1xx`, `0x00e585xx`, `0x00e70380` (the mesh/vehicle module), `0x00f39cxx`, `0x00f5e7xx`, and `0x00f61exx`. **HYPOTHESIS — plausible, not confirmed:** this spread across at least eight distinct, non-adjacent modules is consistent with multiple distinct render passes each binding its own target (a shadow-map pass, one or more post-process/off-screen passes, and the main scene), but this pass did not decompile any of these sites to identify what each one actually renders into, nor determine their relative order. **This is explicitly flagged as follow-up territory** (§14 item 4), exactly as anticipated in the task's suggested angle of attack — at minimum, this document establishes *that* multiple distinct render-target-binding call sites exist and roughly where they live, which is a starting point for a deeper pass, not a finished answer.

### 12.1 All eleven remaining candidate sites individually verified (2026-09-29) — all REFUTED

Per the task's own instruction to verify §12's "unverified offset hits" properly (the same discipline §20.7 already applied to one site), a fresh whole-binary re-scan of the exact idiom (`MOV reg,[base+0x94]` then `CALL reg`, the corrected MOV-then-CALL form already established in §16.1) recovered the twelve original census hits at exact instruction addresses (the original pass only reported page-rounded buckets) — `tools/scratchpad/Sec12Census1.java`. **One of the twelve is the already-confirmed command-buffer replay handler itself** (`0x0049cb1d`, inside the opcode-9 `SetRenderTarget` handler at `0x0049CB00`, §7) — not a separate "outside" site at all. This resolves a long-standing off-by-one in this document's own "12 distinct sites outside the command buffer" phrasing: there are genuinely only **eleven** outside candidates, of which `0x00e70380` was already REFUTED (§20.7). The remaining ten were each fully disassembled, and where the shape warranted it, decompiled (`tools/scratchpad/Sec12Detail1.java`/`Sec12Detail2.java`), checking the exact argument count against the real public `IDirect3DDevice9::SetRenderTarget(DWORD, IDirect3DSurface9*)` signature (this + 2 explicit arguments) — exactly the check that already refuted `0x00e70380` and the two sites `WALLS.md` records from earlier passes. **All ten are REFUTED:**

- **`0x0047c13c`** (inside `FUN_0047ba00`, the device-creation function itself, §3.1/§15 — not the separate device-*reset* function `FUN_0047c800` as originally guessed by address proximity; the call sits in the post-`CreateDevice`/`CreateDeviceEx` bring-up tail) — **REFUTED, 1 explicit argument pushed** (a single value in a register) where the public method needs 2.
- **`0x00619c18`** (`FUN_00619b80`) — **REFUTED, 0 explicit arguments**; the return value is used as a boolean (tested and branched on) immediately after.
- **`0x0061b7c5`** (`FUN_0061b740`) — **REFUTED, 0 explicit arguments**; the return value is immediately loaded as an integer and converted to a float two instructions later, a usage pattern inconsistent with an `HRESULT`.
- **`0x00d0c9a0`** (`FUN_00d0c890`) and **`0x00d0ea54`** (`FUN_00d0e6c0`) — **REFUTED, both by base-object identity, not merely arg count.** Decompilation shows both functions are OpenSSL bignum/elliptic-curve arithmetic (`FUN_00d0e6c0`'s own body cites the literal source path `.\crypto\ec\ecp_simple.c`; `FUN_00d0c890` is structurally identical — the same sparse, explicitly NULL-checked callback table at fixed offsets including `+0x8c`/`+0x94`). A genuine C++/COM device vtable is never null-checked before use (a null entry would mean the interface itself is broken); this is an `EC_METHOD`-style optional-callback table whose slot `0x94` happens to numerically coincide with the D3D9 `SetRenderTarget` vtable offset by pure chance — exactly the coincidental-offset failure mode `WALLS.md` already warns about. Once read correctly (the object pointer itself is passed as an explicit first argument to the callback, not as an implicit `this`), the slot-`0x94` call in both functions takes **3 explicit arguments**, not 2, independently confirming the refutation even without the identity evidence.
- **`0x00ddd1f9`** (`FUN_00ddd1d0`) — **REFUTED, 5 explicit arguments** passed (the caller's own `this` plus four more values), not 2.
- **`0x00e586cc`** (`FUN_00e585f0`) — **REFUTED, 3 explicit arguments**, not 2. (Incidentally, this function's own gating structure calls two OTHER methods, at vtable `+0x10`/`+0x14`, on the object at this function's own `this+0x130` field — the same still-unidentified sub-object §16.2/§16.3/§18.8/§22.1 already flag; not pursued further here as it is unrelated to the `SetRenderTarget` question.)
- **`0x00f39c2c`** (`FUN_00f39c10`) and **`0x00f61e7c`** (`FUN_00f61e60`, a near-identical sibling function iterating a differently-offset list) — **REFUTED, 0 explicit arguments** in both; return value used as a boolean immediately after in both.
- **`0x00f5e809`** (`FUN_00f5e7a0`) — **REFUTED, 0 explicit arguments**; return value used as a boolean immediately after.

**Conclusion: every one of §12's original twelve census hits is now individually accounted for** — one is the genuine command-buffer replay handler (§7, already confirmed), and the other eleven are all REFUTED. None of the eleven needed formal RTTI class resolution to be dispositive; every one failed on argument count alone, and two of those ten additionally failed on base-object identity via a definitive non-RTTI marker (the OpenSSL source-path string literal). **This closes §14 item 5 in the negative: none of this project's originally-hypothesized "≥8 non-command-buffer render-target-binding call sites" are real `SetRenderTarget` calls.** The render-pass/render-target question itself (which code binds the shadow map, post-process targets, etc.) remains genuinely open — but not via this census, which is now fully exhausted. A different strategy (e.g. tracing `CreateRenderTarget`/`CreateDepthStencilSurface` allocation sites forward to their consumers) would be needed for a future pass. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for all ten refutations; two independently corroborated by decompiled source-string evidence.]**

Evidence anchors: census script `tools/scratchpad/Sec12Census1.java`; per-site detail scripts `tools/scratchpad/Sec12Detail1.java`/`Sec12Detail2.java`; the ten refuted addresses and containing functions listed above; OpenSSL identity string `.\crypto\ec\ecp_simple.c` inside `FUN_00d0e6c0`.

## 13. Direct3D 11 Executable — Cross-Check Only

Per the task's scope guidance, only a single cheap check was run against the already-imported, already-analysed DX11 project (`tools/gp_fxo_dx11`, used strictly read-only, not modified): a search for the same literal boot-log strings found in §3.6/§6.1.

| String | DX9 | DX11 |
|---|---|---|
| `"RL: create d3d device"` | present | **present** (at `0x012A3078`) |
| `"RL: initialize thread"` | present | **present** (at `0x012A3090`) |
| `"RL: init d3d mempool"` | present | **present** (at `0x012A30A8`) |
| `"RL: init command"` | present | not found (exact-substring search) |
| `"rl_command_thread"` | present | not found (exact-substring search) |

**[CONFIRMED — empirical, string presence/absence as measured.]** The three bring-up log lines are shared verbatim between both executables, strongly suggesting the DX11 build's device-creation/mempool-init bring-up sequence is the **same shared engine code**, only targeting a different graphics API underneath (consistent with this being a single "RL" renderer-abstraction module compiled for both backends, matching the general architecture already established for shader loading in `spec-fxo-format.md` §8). The absence of the exact thread-name and "init command" strings under a literal substring search is **HYPOTHESIS** as to cause (a genuinely different DX11 threading model that doesn't need the same command-buffer thread; the DX11 build using native command lists/deferred contexts instead of a hand-rolled buffer; or simply a differently-worded string not caught by an exact match) — **not investigated further**, per the task's explicit instruction to keep the DX11 side to an easy differences-only check rather than a full pass.

8. **The exact settings/config source feeding `D3DPRESENT_PARAMETERS`' `Windowed`/related flags (§3.4).**
9. **`FUN_00e98df0`'s exact meaning** (the tier/capability check gating the Ex device-creation path, §3.2) — not traced to its own source this pass.
10. ~~**`Present`/`PresentEx` were never located**~~ **CLOSED (§19).** `Present` is called from two handlers newly identified inside the already-known opcode dispatch table itself — opcode 7 (`0x0049ca30`, the plain full-buffer-flip form, all four public parameters `NULL`, bracketed by a before/after timer read feeding a new global `DAT_03171b70`) and opcode 8 (`0x0049caa0`, a parameterised form with a caller-supplied source rect and destination window handle) — both confirmed against `Present`'s own public argument count, both calling through the same already-confirmed replay-thread device pointer `DAT_03171b68`. `PresentEx` was searched for whole-binary at its own public vtable offset and found nowhere; this engine appears to always call the inherited base `Present` regardless of Ex-ness. The practical answer: ending a frame is itself just another opcode in the same deferred command buffer, not a separate mechanism — there is no standalone "per-frame loop that calls Present" to find. **Still OPEN: which producer function decides to record the opcode-7/8 command** (§19.3) — not traced this pass, opcodes `7`/`8` being too common as plain integers for the same mechanical literal-store scan that found the draw-record writers to stay selective.
11. **Threading topology is not fully resolved** (§17): §16.1's producer functions branch on caller thread ID against two different globals (`DAT_02d024c8` vs. `DAT_02e5d640`), while §6.3's replay driver records ITS OWN thread ID into a third, distinct global (`DAT_02e5d658`). Whether there are genuinely two threads (game, render/replay) or three (game, a separate scene-traversal/render thread, and the command-replay thread) was assumed rather than confirmed — resolving which thread ID lives in which global is a prerequisite for trusting any "produced by the game thread" claim in this document.
12. **Command granularity may not be as uniform as §1/§6 imply** (§17): §16.1 already shows at least two "engine-control" opcodes (`3`, `0x17`/`0x18`) alongside the D3D9-mirroring ones — roughly 50 of 74 handlers remain undisassembled, and some may be composite (calling back into engine code that itself issues several further direct device calls) rather than uniform 1:1 D3D9-call stubs. `SetVertexShaderConstantF`'s two handlers (§10) are a specific, cheap next check: a variable-length payload (count × 16 bytes) cannot fit a truly "fixed-size argument block," so those two handlers likely reveal a by-reference/variable-length record convention this document does not yet describe.
13. **The offset-only census counts in §10–§12 (`SetVertexShader`/`SetPixelShader`/`SetTexture` handler locations, "14 `SetTransform` sites," "≥8/12 non-command-buffer `SetRenderTarget` sites") should be treated as unverified offset hits, not confirmed call sites** (§17) — §16.1 demonstrated directly, in this same document, that a matching vtable offset alone (without checking the base object's real class) can be entirely coincidental. None of these were re-checked against the base object's identity the way §16.1 did for `DAT_0351f8f8`/the thread-local object. This does not necessarily mean they are wrong, only that their confidence label should read HYPOTHESIS, not the implied CONFIRMED of a plain census count.
