## 16. Producer-Side Follow-Up, Pass 2 — the FUN_004a2e30/FUN_004a8900 Lead Refuted; a Stronger Candidate Chain Found, Not Yet Closed

**Scope of this pass.** A follow-up session (prior to this one) had converged on a
lead, never checked to completion: a whole-binary vtable-offset census found that
`FUN_004a2e30` and `FUN_004a8900` -- both direct callers of `FUN_0049c890` (the
command-buffer reserve/write helper, §6.2) -- each also make exactly one
indirect call at vtable offset `0x28`, the same numeric offset as `FUN_00e73e80`'s
own slot on the three `FUN_00e73e80`/`FUN_00e72c60`/`FUN_00e72d00` sibling classes
(§9, cross-referencing `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.9.5). This pass re-ran the
prior session's own scripts (`tools/scripts/RenderProducer1-4.java`, previously
never executed to completion against a saved-output run) against a fresh disposable
project (`tools/gp_render3`), captured full output this time, and checked the lead
against three specific predicates: does either function write an opcode-42/40
command-buffer record; do its argument dwords trace to a resolved draw range; and is
its vtable+0x28 call genuinely on the same object/class as `FUN_00e73e80`'s siblings.

### 16.1 The lead is REFUTED

**`FUN_004a2e30`** (`0x004a2e30`, 663 bytes, fully decompiled+disassembled) is a
device-lost/resource-invalidation dispatcher, not a mesh-draw producer: it walks
three separate resource-handle arrays (fields `+0x1038`/`+0x1040`, `+0x9050`/`+0x9058`,
`+0x5044`/`+0x504c` of its parameter object) calling a per-item release/recreate
callback (`FUN_0047b1b0` or `FUN_004a85a0`) on each entry -- the same shape as the
already-documented device-reset path (§5, `FUN_0047c800`). Its only command-buffer
writes are: one opcode `0x17` record (arguments `0x480`, `1`) written unconditionally,
matching the *already-known* §6.2 observation that opcode `0x17`/`0x18` are used "from
inside the device-reset path"; and one opcode `3` record (a fixed data-address argument,
`&DAT_03245710`), written into the game-thread buffer (`DAT_02d024b8`-family) if the
calling thread's ID matches `DAT_02d024c8` (the game/logic thread, per §6.1), or else
handed off via a bare call to `FUN_0049c890()` if the calling thread instead matches
`DAT_02e5d640` (evidently the render/replay thread's own ID). **Opcode 42 (`0x2a`,
`DrawIndexedPrimitive`) and opcode 40 (`0x28`, `DrawPrimitive`) never appear anywhere
in this function.** `FUN_0049c890` itself (`0x0049c890`, 85 bytes, decompiled for
reference) is not a generic "reserve space only" routine as §6.2's phrasing could be
read to suggest -- its body hardcodes an opcode-`3` write with a fixed payload address
(`&DAT_03393850`) into a *third*, separate buffer (`DAT_02e5d630`-family), i.e. it is
itself one more instance of the same reserve+write+advance pattern, dedicated to a
fixed cross-thread "notify" record. This does not change §6.2's 58-caller count (still
accurate as a reference census) but narrows what calling `FUN_0049c890` actually
proves: only that the caller wants to post *this one fixed notification*, not that it
is writing an arbitrary opcode of its own choosing. **[CONFIRMED -- disassembly.]**

**`FUN_004a8900`** (`0x004a8900`, 2589 bytes, fully decompiled+disassembled across two
passes after the first pass's default line/instruction caps truncated it) is a
viewport/aspect-ratio reconfiguration routine: it divides by the same constant
(`DAT_012a30b0`) `FUN_004a2e30`'s sibling code uses, computes several derived float
values consistent with FOV/aspect-ratio bookkeeping, and calls `FUN_00475e10` three
times with them. Its only command-buffer writes are **four** opcode `0x17` records
(second-argument values `0x20`, `0x10`, `0x80`, `0x80`; third argument always `0`) --
again matching §6.2's known opcode-`0x17` device/viewport-notification family, most
plausibly per-category invalidation flags (individual power-of-two bits) rather than
draw data. Each write is followed by the identical thread-conditional
notify-or-call-`FUN_0049c890` pattern seen in `FUN_004a2e30` (game thread: call
`FUN_004752c0`; render thread, `DAT_02e5d640`: call `FUN_0049c890`). **Opcode 42/40
never appear.** **[CONFIRMED -- disassembly.]**

**Both functions' vtable+`0x28` calls are on the wrong object.** `FUN_004a2e30` makes
a zero-argument indirect call through `DAT_0351f8f8`'s own vtable slot `+0x28` -- on
the *render-context singleton* already characterised in §4.4, not a per-mesh handle.
It is the closing half of a begin/end bracket: the same function opens with an
identical zero-argument call at `DAT_0351f8f8+0x24`. `FUN_004a8900`'s vtable+`0x28`
call is a one-argument indirect call through the vtable of a *different* object,
reached via the same `FS:[0x2c]`-based thread-local "current render object" pointer
used throughout §3.7's teardown path (dereferenced at its own `+0x674`) -- a *third*,
still-unnamed object, distinct from both `DAT_0351f8f8` and from the
`rl_scene`/`rl_raycast_occluder` mesh-handle classes. **Neither call is on an instance
of the three sibling classes `FUN_00e73e80` sits on.** The numeric match to offset `0x28` is coincidental: as shown
in §16.2 below, `DAT_0351f8f8`'s own vtable+`0x24`/+`0x28` begin/end bracket recurs
verbatim in a genuine mesh-draw function too, confirming it is a widely-reused
generic idiom (a scope/lock/profiling bracket) rather than a signal of mesh-handle
resolution -- exactly the "no selectivity" failure mode already on record
(`HANDOFF.md` §27.4) for whole-binary small-displacement predicates. **[CONFIRMED --
disassembly, both call sites, object identity checked directly, not inferred from the
offset alone.]**

**Conclusion: the lead does not pan out.** Both functions satisfy the census's
predicate (call `FUN_0049c890`; make one vtable+`0x28` call) but fail every other test
-- wrong opcodes, wrong objects, and a plausible unrelated explanation (device
reset / viewport reconfiguration) for everything they actually do.

### 16.2 A stronger candidate found: `FUN_00e74880` and the vtable+`0x60`/+`0x80` chain

Per this task's own escalation path, the search was broadened to the wider "2+
distinct offset" census (`tools/scripts/RenderProducer3.java`, also re-run and
captured this pass for the first time: `totalIndirectRegisterCalls=21797`,
`matchedOffsetCalls=897`, 565 distinct functions hit, 31 with 2+ of the four watched
offsets `0x28`/`0x3c`/`0x40`/`0x80`). One entry stood out on structural grounds, not
just offset count: **`FUN_00e74880`** (`0x00e74880`, 1695 bytes), offsets
`{0x80: 1, 0x28: 1}`, sitting directly in the `0xe7xxxx` mesh module alongside
`FUN_00e73e80` itself, with `0x80` independently already flagged (pass-1 notes,
§9) as "the next hop `FUN_00e73e80` itself calls at its own tail."

Full decompilation confirms this is not a coincidence. `FUN_00e74880` is
`__thiscall(this, count, itemArray[], flags4, flags5)`. Its body:

1. Loops over `count` entries of `itemArray`, each a per-instance handle.
2. For each visible entry, reads a group-index `u16` from a fixed offset of that per-instance handle, then indexes
   **the exact same runtime record array `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.9.5 already
   found at holder `+0xfc`**: `this[0x3f]` (`0x3f*4 = 0xFC`) is the record-array base,
   stride `0x28` (`groupIndexU16 * 0x28`), field `+0x24` -- byte-for-byte the same
   stride and field §11.9.5's own disassembly of `FUN_00e72d00` established.
3. Resolves the mesh header via `this[0x2f]` (`0x2f*4 = 0xBC`, the same "mesh-resource
   holder" field §11.9.5 named), `+0x30` (loaded Mesh header), `+0x04` (group count,
   used as a bound), `+0x08` (group array base), stride `0x30` -- again byte-for-byte
   matching §11.9.5's own `FUN_00e72d00` disassembly.
4. Binds per-item material/technique state (`FUN_00e78200`, `FUN_00477060`/`FUN_00477030`
   or `FUN_00476f70`/`FUN_00476ff0`), then loops a range index from `0` to the
   resolved group record's own `+0x00` field (a newly-observed fact: the `0x30`-byte
   group record's first dword is its own draw-range **count** -- consistent with, and
   filling in one more field of, the group record `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.9.1
   already partially mapped) -- and for each range index, after a visibility/LOD gate,
   makes an indirect call through the object's own vtable slot `+0x80`, passing the
   resolved group-record pointer and the current range index -- the identical
   `(this+0x80)(groupRecordPtr, rangeIndex)` call shape RenderProducer2's pass-1 notes
   recorded at `FUN_00e73e80`'s own tail.
5. Also brackets its body with `DAT_0351f8f8` vtable+`0x24`/+`0x28` (start/end, zero
   args each) -- the same coincidental idiom identified in §16.1, now seen a third
   time in a function that genuinely does resolve mesh draw ranges, confirming it is
   unrelated to that resolution.

**Reference chain, all directly disassembled this pass:** `FUN_00b13850`
(`0x00b13850`) calls `FUN_00e58b70` (`0x00e58b70`, itself installed as `rl_scene`'s own
vtable+`0x60` override at `0125b2e4`), which calls `FUN_00e74880` directly
(`0x00e58b9c`); `FUN_00e74880` is separately installed as the *third* sibling class's
(unnamed in §9/§11.9.5, `rl_raycast_occluder`'s neighbour at base `0125c4a4`) own
vtable+`0x60` override (`0125c504`). vtable+`0x80` resolves (dumped for all three
sibling vtable bases, `01186c24`/`0125b284`/`0125c4a4`) to **`FUN_00e58480`** (66
bytes) for the first two classes and a 3-byte empty stub (`FUN_008a5bf0`, effectively
a no-op override) for the third (`rl_raycast_occluder` itself -- sensible, since an
occlusion-test volume plausibly never submits a visible draw). `FUN_00e58480` reads a
further `u16` from the draw-range record's own `+0x2` field (base `group+0x20`, stride
`0x14` -- again exactly §11.9.5's own range-array/stride) and forwards it as the sole
argument to a *third* sub-object at `this+0x130`'s own vtable+`0x1c`, after first
gating on that same sub-object's vtable+`0x24`. **[CONFIRMED -- disassembly, every
hop in this chain.]**

**This is a materially stronger, structurally-verified candidate than the refuted
lead** -- it is a genuine per-visible-item, per-draw-range iterator built directly on
top of the same holder`+0xbc`/holder`+0xfc` mesh-handle structure `FUN_00e73e80`
itself decodes, reached from a small, tight dispatch module (`0xb13xxx`/`0xe58xxx`)
that also contains the already-known vtable-only entry point `FUN_00b138a0`.

### 16.3 Where the chain currently ends -- OPEN, not yet confirmed

The trail was followed as far as `this+0x130`'s vtable+`0x1c` (called from
`FUN_00e58480`, §16.2 above) before running out of directly-resolvable evidence this
pass: `this+0x130` is a pointer to a further, still-unidentified sub-object, and its
concrete class/vtable was not established. Two candidate constructor/destructor
addresses (`0x00e576b0`, `0x00435660`) were read directly out of the header block
immediately preceding the `"rl_scene"` debug string (at `0125b26c`, the same header
block §9/§11.9.5 already cites for the class names) -- but neither address is yet a
Ghidra-recognised function in this project, so neither could be decompiled this pass
to confirm it is a constructor or to see what it installs at `+0x130`. An opcode-42/40
write was not observed anywhere in the `FUN_00e74880` chain traced this pass -- **but
per the §16.4 correction below, that absence is no longer treated as meaningful
negative evidence** (§6.2's fix means the chain was never expected to route through
`FUN_0049c890` regardless of whether it produces the draw call). **[OPEN --
the natural next step is resolving `this+0x130`'s class (most directly: disassemble
`0x00e576b0`/`0x00435660` as raw code even before they are Ghidra Functions, to check
whether either is `rl_scene`'s constructor and what vtable it installs at `+0x130`),
then decompiling that vtable's own `+0x1c` method.**

**Honest scope note:** this pass narrows §14 items 1/2 considerably (a real,
disassembly-confirmed per-item/per-range submission loop, four hops closer to the
draw call than §9 left things) but does not close them. See §14 for the updated
item text, and §16.4 for a significant re-reading of what this chain most likely is.

### 16.4 Re-reading this chain, per an external advisory review (§17): probably not the draw producer at all, but a genuine cross-spec find

**§16.2's chain is very unlikely to terminate in the actual `DrawIndexedPrimitive` producer, and this should be stated plainly rather than left implicit.** `spec-vertex-format.md` §8.2 documents the `0x14`-byte draw-range record's real shape: `+0x00` packed material/high16 selector, `+0x04` start index, `+0x08` index count, `+0x0C` min vertex, `+0x10` max vertex. `FUN_00e58480` (§16.2) reads and forwards **only** the `+0x2` sub-field of that record (the high16 selector) to `this+0x130`'s vtable+`0x1c` — it does not pass along start index, count, or either vertex bound. A function that receives only a ≤15-valued selector cannot, by itself, supply the six arguments a `DrawIndexedPrimitive` opcode-42 record needs (§7) — unless the range/group pointer itself is stashed into `this+0x130`'s object ahead of the call (via some field write not yet checked), that specific final hop is very unlikely to be the draw emission point.

**A more likely reading of the whole `FUN_00e74880` chain (HYPOTHESIS, not confirmed):** it is a **per-part state selector** (choosing a per-range transform, damage/visibility state, or stream-source set before drawing) rather than the draw call itself. The actual `DrawIndexedPrimitive` emission more plausibly sits either inline in `FUN_00e74880`'s own per-range loop body (not fully disassembled instruction-by-instruction this pass — only summarised) or inside one of the four per-item material/technique-bind calls already named in §16.2 (`FUN_00477060`/`FUN_00477030`/`FUN_00476f70`/`FUN_00476ff0`) — all four sit inside the same `0x00475xxx`–`0x004a9xxx` producer address range §6.2 already flags as where the Record* wrapper family lives, which was not previously connected to this observation.

**A genuine, independent cross-spec finding falls out of this regardless of the producer question: `FUN_00e58480` is very likely the first identified runtime *consumer* of the packed "high16" selector value that `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.6/§11.10 spent multiple sessions searching for** (that search used instruction-form predicates — `SHR 0x10`/`CMP 0xe` — that are structurally blind to the direct sub-field read `FUN_00e58480` actually performs; the same instruction-form blind spot `HANDOFF.md` §27.4 already documents for other cases). This is worth carrying back to `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` as a lead in a future pass, independent of whether the draw-producer question above is ever closed from this specific chain. **[HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred from the field-width/argument-count mismatch (a hard constraint, not a style judgment) and the byte-for-byte structural match to the already-documented high16 selector; not independently re-verified against a fresh Ghidra trace this pass — flagged for the next agent to confirm directly.]**

**Evidence anchors added this pass:** `FUN_004a2e30` (`0x004a2e30`), `FUN_004a8900`
(`0x004a8900`, refuted candidates); `FUN_00e74880` (`0x00e74880`), `FUN_00e58480`
(`0x00e58480`), `FUN_008a5bf0` (`0x008a5bf0`), `FUN_00e58b70` (`0x00e58b70`),
`FUN_00b13850` (`0x00b13850`) (the new chain); sibling-class vtable bases
`01186c24`/`0125b284`/`0125c4a4` (unnamed/`rl_scene`/`rl_raycast_occluder`, per the
literal debug strings at their header blocks, cross-referencing §9); candidate
`this+0x130` ctor/dtor addresses `0x00e576b0`/`0x00435660` (unresolved, OPEN).

## 17. External Advisory Review (Fable 5.1, 2026-09-25)

**Per direct user instruction ("check with fable at the end"), the finished state of this document (through §16) was handed to a Fable 5.1 advisory-only consult** — read-only, no file edits, no Ghidra access, asked to independently sanity-check the architecture, the confidence labeling, and the §16.3 open gap, and told explicitly to disagree rather than rubber-stamp. Full transcript not reproduced here (it is model output, not authoritative); this section records what was actually acted on.

**Verdict on the headline architecture (§1/§6):** endorsed as sound and expected for a 2011 Volition title sharing code with the Xbox 360 build (whose native API is D3D9-shaped) — the reviewer independently re-derived every vtable offset and `D3DPRESENT_PARAMETERS` field cited in §3–§7 against the public documented layouts and found all of them correct, including the `0x210 = ResetEx` slot match.

**Issues raised that were acted on directly in this document** (see the dated corrections at each location, not repeated here): the §16 cleanroom-compliance slip (raw decompiler expressions/auto-named variables quoted verbatim, contradicting this document's own compliance claim in the header) — paraphrased throughout §16.1–§16.2; the §6.4/§14-item-6 filler-slot count inconsistency (16 vs. "~17") — fixed to the exact, already-orchestrator-verified 16; §6.2's "generic reserve helper" mischaracterisation of `FUN_0049c890`, contradicted by this document's own §16.1 findings — corrected in place, `FUN_00473770` promoted to primary candidate for the real generic emit primitive; §4.4's "vtable mirrors D3D9 device slots" claim, shown to be contradicted by a hard calling-convention argument-count mismatch in this document's own §16.1 data — downgraded; the §16.3 chain's likely misidentification as a draw-call producer, corrected via a field-width argument (the final hop forwards only a selector value, not a full draw-range record) that also surfaces a genuine, independent cross-spec lead for `spec-vehicle-geometry.md`'s previously-unresolved high16 selector consumer (new §16.4); three new OPEN items added to §14 (missing `Present`/`PresentEx`, unresolved threading topology, non-uniform command granularity); a caveat added to §14 on treating §10–§12's offset-only census counts as unverified rather than confirmed, given §16.1 demonstrated exactly this coincidence risk within this same document.

**Raised but not acted on this pass (recorded as still OPEN, not silently dropped):** whether `DAT_03171b68`'s assignment source is a trivial one-line confirm (§4.3); whether `CreateDeviceEx`'s second output pointer is provably the same value as the base pointer rather than merely presumed (§3.5); `D3DPERF_SetOptions(1)`'s public meaning as a retail anti-profiling flag, worth a note for the project's own planned dynamic-analysis work (`HANDOFF.md` §27.5); a suggested MSAA-loop-bound reconciliation in §3.3 (`D3DMULTISAMPLE_TYPE` publicly tops out at 16, but §3.3 describes probing up to 64 candidates — the loop is very likely over quality levels and/or formats as well as sample counts, not sample-count alone; not re-checked against disassembly this pass); a suggestion to diff the already-analysed DX11 executable's equivalent producer code if the DX9-side trace stalls again; and a scope-priority suggestion (deprioritise the full 74-opcode enumeration in favour of camera/main-scene-pass identification and the `FUN_00e98df0` Ex-path gate, on the reasoning that this project's working directories suggest an RTX-Remix-compatibility angle where those three matter more) — noted for whoever picks up the next pass, not adopted as a re-prioritisation here since it rests on an inference about project intent this document should not assert on the reviewer's behalf.

**[This section records an external advisory opinion, not a new disassembly finding.** Confidence labels on the corrections above follow from re-reading this document's OWN already-CONFIRMED §16.1 data under the reviewer's arguments — a logic/consistency check, not new Ghidra work — except where marked HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred (§16.4), which is flagged for direct re-verification by a future pass.]

## 18. Producer-Side Follow-Up, Pass 3 — the Four Material-Bind Functions Opened, the Generic Record Primitive Found (Not FUN_00473770), and a Real DrawIndexedPrimitive Producer Chain Confirmed

**Scope of this pass.** Per the task brief following §16/§17: (1) fully decompile and disassemble the four per-item material/technique-bind calls named in §16.2 (`FUN_00477060`, `FUN_00477030`, `FUN_00476f70`, `FUN_00476ff0`), checking specifically for calls to `FUN_00473770` (§6.2's own corrected candidate for the generic command-buffer emit primitive) and for direct or indirect writes of opcode `0x2a`/`0x28`; (2) instruction-step `FUN_00e74880`'s own per-range loop body directly, which §16.2 had only summarised. Both were done. The investigation then went further than originally scoped, because the first two steps directly surfaced the actual generic record-write mechanism and, from there, a complete, disassembly-confirmed path from a resolved draw-range record to a real `DrawIndexedPrimitive` command-buffer write — closing §14 items 1 and 2 for one genuine code path (with an honestly-scoped remainder, §18.8).

### 18.1 The four material/technique-bind functions

All four were fully decompiled and disassembled (project `tools/gp_render4`, a fresh disposable copy of the main analysed project). None of the four calls `FUN_00473770`, and none writes opcode `0x2a`/`0x28` itself. Three of the four are thin, one-line "record a state change" wrappers; the fourth is not a command-buffer producer at all. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, all four.]**

- **`FUN_00477060`** (26 bytes): guarded by a null check on the thread-local "current render object" (the same object reached via `+0x674` throughout §3.7/§16.1/§16.2), it calls `FUN_00475930` with its own two caller-supplied arguments, then sets a two-byte dirty/rebind flag pair at the current render object's own `+0x50a`/`+0x50b`.
- **`FUN_00477030`** (40 bytes): same guard, calls `FUN_004758c0` with its two arguments; does not touch the `+0x50a`/`+0x50b` flag pair.
- **`FUN_00476ff0`** (37 bytes): same guard, calls `FUN_00475790` with a single field read from the current render object's own `+0x760`; also sets the `+0x50a`/`+0x50b` flag pair.
- **`FUN_00476f70`** (43 bytes): structurally different from the other three — it is a small, self-contained accumulator, not a command-buffer producer at all. Given a small array object (bound-checked to at most 32 entries) it appends a (key, value) pair at the object's own growing offset, increments the object's own entry count, and ORs a bit — looked up from a small table at `0x01351e28`, indexed by the key — into a flags dword at the object's own `+0x508`. **No call to any Record* wrapper, no thread-local guard, no command-buffer reference of any kind.**

### 18.2 `FUN_00473770` is refuted as the generic emit primitive; there is no single generic primitive

§6.2 (corrected 2026-09-25) had proposed `FUN_00473770` as the primary candidate for "the" generic, arbitrary-opcode command-buffer write primitive, based on it being called with small integer literals from the device-reset path. Full decompilation this pass refutes this directly.

`FUN_00473770` (556 bytes) is a per-material/technique **state-resolve-and-apply** routine, not a record-write primitive: depending on a flag byte read from its own material-index argument, it computes an offset into either a per-material array (stride `0xB8`) or the current render object's own array, reads a small cluster of cached colour/parameter fields at that object's `+0x768`..`+0x77c`, calls a texture-stage-shaped helper (`FUN_0047a680`) **four times** with stage indices `0`–`3`, and issues **two direct virtual calls** — one on the current render object's own vtable at `+0x24` (a paired begin/end bracket, called with one pointer argument here, distinct from the zero-argument `+0x24`/`+0x28` bracket on `DAT_0351f8f8` already documented in §16.1/§16.2 as a different, coincidentally-same-offset object), and one on `DAT_0351f8f8`'s own vtable at `+0x3c` (four arguments, one a literal `1.0f`). **None of this is a buffer-cursor read, check, write, or advance.** `FUN_00473770` calls `FUN_004755b0` twice (§18.3) — this is its only indirect connection to the command buffer, two hops removed, and only for a single fixed-shape record, not an arbitrary one. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

There is, in fact, **no single generic "reserve+write+advance" function anywhere in this binary.** What §6.2 originally described as "a family of small Record*-shaped wrapper functions" is exactly that: the reserve/write/advance/notify logic is a short, repeated **compiled idiom**, appearing separately — with the same instruction shape but a different hardcoded opcode literal, argument count, and record size each time — inside dozens of tiny functions in the `0x00475xxx`–`0x004a9xxx` range. Each instance: reads a buffer-descriptor pointer (passed in a register, not a stack argument); compares the descriptor's own end-pointer field (`+0x4`) minus its cursor field (`+0x8`) against the record size, calling a grow callback at the descriptor's own `+0xc` if too small; writes the opcode as a plain (no-displacement) store through the register that already points at the cursor; writes each argument at fixed `+4`, `+8`, … offsets from that same register; advances the descriptor's cursor field by the exact record size; and finally, if a global flag byte (`DAT_01351c4a`) is set, compares `GetCurrentThreadId()` against `DAT_02d024c8` (game thread) to call `FUN_004752c0`, or against `DAT_02e5d640` (render/replay thread) to call `FUN_0049c890` — confirming §16.1's own finding that `FUN_0049c890` is itself just one more instance of this same idiom, hardcoded to a fixed opcode-`3` notify record, now understood as the render-thread-side sibling of the game-thread-side `FUN_004752c0`.

Four concrete instances were fully disassembled this pass, closing part of §14 item 6/12 along the way:

| Function | Opcode written | Args | Record size |
|---|---|---|---|
| `FUN_004755b0` | `0xA` (10) | 1 | 8 bytes |
| `FUN_00475930` (called by `FUN_00477060`) | `0x17` (23) | 2 | 12 bytes |
| `FUN_004758c0` (called by `FUN_00477030`) | `0x16` (22) | 2 | 12 bytes |
| `FUN_00475790` (called by `FUN_00476ff0`) | `0x14` (20) | variable | `0x18` + two caller-array lengths (bytes) |

`FUN_00475790` is the concrete confirmation of §14 item 12's prediction: it writes a fixed 6-dword header (two counts, two more fields, one derived length) then `memcpy`s in **two** variable-length arrays taken directly from the current render object's own storage — the exact "by-reference/variable-length record" convention item 12 hypothesised from `SetVertexShaderConstantF`'s shape alone. Its source object is exactly the same accumulator structure `FUN_00476f70` (§18.1) appends into (same count field, same per-entry array, same `+0x508` flags dword, plus a second parallel array at `+0x104`/`+0x108`) — so `FUN_00476f70`/`FUN_00476ff0` together implement a small batched "accumulate up to 32 (key, value) state pairs, then flush them as one variable-length opcode-`0x14` record" mechanism, most plausibly a batched sampler/texture-stage-state update. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, all four functions and the accumulator/flush relationship.]**

### 18.3 `RecordDrawPrimitive` and `RecordDrawIndexedPrimitive` identified

Knowing the exact idiom (§18.2) made the actual opcode-`0x2a`/`0x28` writers mechanically findable: a whole-binary scan for a bare-register, no-displacement store of the literal `0x28` or `0x2a` (i.e. the opcode-write instruction of the idiom, as opposed to a `+0x28` displacement, which is common and was the false-positive-prone approach of earlier passes) found **exactly two genuine hits** in the entire executable; every other literal-`0x28`/`0x2a` bare-register store found by the same scan is a Microsoft CRT `errno`-constant assignment deep in the C runtime (`EDEADLK`=`0x28`, `ENOLCK`=`0x2a`), immediately distinguishable because none of them sit next to the idiom's record-size cursor-advance or thread-notify code. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + empirical, whole-binary scan, `tools/scripts/RenderProducer12.java`.]**

- **`FUN_00476360`** (110 bytes) = **`RecordDrawPrimitive`**: writes opcode `0x28` (40) with 3 arguments (matching the public `DrawPrimitive`'s own 3-parameter signature exactly), record size `0x10` (16 bytes) — this is byte-for-byte the same record size §7 already confirmed for opcode 40's replay-side handler.
- **`FUN_004764d0`** (131 bytes) = **`RecordDrawIndexedPrimitive`**: writes opcode `0x2a` (42) with 6 arguments (matching the public `DrawIndexedPrimitive`'s own 6-parameter signature exactly), record size `0x1c` (28 bytes) — again byte-for-byte the record size §7 already confirmed for opcode 42's replay-side handler.

This is, for the first time in this project's three-pass history on this question, a disassembly-confirmed identification of the actual producer-side write function for `DrawIndexedPrimitive`. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

### 18.4 The generic `DrawIndexedPrimitive` entry point and its real callers

`FUN_004764d0` has exactly **6 direct callers** in the whole binary. Five of them (`FUN_00e5a290`, `FUN_00485dc0`, `FUN_005d80b0`, `FUN_00fcf8e0`, `FUN_004ad070`) hardcode both `MinVertexIndex` and `BaseVertexIndex` to the literal `0` at the call site, and derive their vertex/index counts from their own local, self-contained state — the shape of a small, self-drawn immediate batch (a UI quad, a debug shape, a particle/decal batch) rather than a general mesh consumer; one of the five (`FUN_00e5a290`) explicitly passes `D3DPT_TRIANGLELIST` (public value `4`) with `NumVertices`/`PrimitiveCount` taken from its own object's fields, consistent with a fixed-shape quad-list draw.

The sixth caller, **`FUN_00477400`** (81 bytes), is different in kind: a `thiscall` method that forwards **all six** caller-supplied D3D9 arguments to `FUN_004764d0` completely unchanged, adding only two things of its own — it looks up the command-buffer descriptor from the current render object's own `+0x760` field (the same field `FUN_00477060`/`FUN_00476ff0` already use, §18.1), and it increments a draw-call counter at its own `this` object's `+0x50`→`+0x8c` field. This is the generic, argument-preserving `DrawIndexedPrimitive` entry point a real mesh-draw-range consumer would have to call. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

`FUN_00477400` itself has exactly **2 direct callers** (plus two further raw call sites the current Ghidra analysis does not attribute to any named function, not investigated further this pass):

- **`FUN_00b13bf0`** (222 bytes) — the mesh case, detailed in §18.6.
- **`FUN_0047b7c0`** (250 bytes) — a `switch`-dispatched routine (on a small state field, cases matching a simple kind selector) that also calls `FUN_00477400` with `D3DPT_TRIANGLESTRIP` (public value `5`), `BaseVertexIndex`/`MinVertexIndex` both `0`, and `NumVertices`/`PrimitiveCount` taken from two of its own object's count fields (the second minus `2`, the standard `IndexCount − 2 = PrimitiveCount` relation for an indexed triangle strip). Its field names/switch shape (offsets in the `+0xa0xx`–`+0xa2xx` range) do not match the mesh/holder structure used elsewhere in this document; this looks like an independent dynamic/procedural triangle-strip subsystem (a trail, ribbon, or rope-shaped effect is the most likely candidate given the shape) — genuinely a **second**, distinct real caller of the same generic entry point, not investigated further this pass and not claimed as mesh-related.

### 18.5 `FUN_00e74880`'s own loop body, instruction-stepped directly

Per the task's second requirement, `FUN_00e74880`'s per-range loop body (previously only summarised in §16.2) was instruction-stepped directly, address by address, rather than read from the decompiler's summary alone. Confirmed structure of the innermost loop (the one iterating a per-range sub-item index from `0` up to the resolved group record's own count field, §16.2 point 4):

1. On each iteration, after the existing visibility/culling gate (§16.2's own description, unchanged), the loop makes the already-documented call through this object's own vtable at `+0x80`, passing the resolved group-record pointer and the current range index — confirmed at address `0x00e74e1d`–`0x00e74e29`, exactly as §16.2 described.
2. **Immediately afterward, in the same iteration, before advancing to the next sub-item** — not merely "at the end of the function" — the loop reads the mesh-header pointer's own flag byte; if a specific bit is clear, it resolves a further per-sub-item byte through `FUN_00e7bf80` and gates on a second object (`DAT_0351f914`'s own vtable at `+0x1c`) before proceeding.
3. If that second gate passes (or the flag byte was set, skipping the gate), the loop makes **a second virtual call**, on `DAT_0351f8f8`'s own vtable at `+0x10`, with three arguments: the mesh-resource holder pointer, the `u16` group index, and the current sub-item index — confirmed at `0x00e74e74`–`0x00e74e8a`.

This second call is the same call, with the same three-argument shape (holder pointer, group index, range index), that §9 already attributes to `FUN_00e73e80`'s own tail. Finding it interleaved directly inside `FUN_00e74880`'s own loop, immediately after the vtable-`+0x80` call, confirms `DAT_0351f8f8`'s vtable slot `+0x10` — not the vtable-`+0x80`/`FUN_00e58480`/`this+0x130` chain §16.2/§16.3 already correctly downgraded — is the shared next hop both known per-range submission paths route through. **No opcode-`0x2a`/`0x28` write, and no call to `FUN_00473770` or any of the four material-bind functions' own callees, occurs inline in this loop** — the loop's own contribution is resolving and gating the range, not the eventual draw call itself, consistent with §16.4's downgrade of the vtable-`+0x80` chain. `DAT_0351f8f8`'s own class was identified this pass as `rl_renderer` via RTTI (§18.10), but its vtable `+0x10` slot's target address turns out to be shared with many other slots on the same vtable rather than a single distinguishable method — see §18.10 for the full finding and why this is reported as a genuine complication rather than a clean resolution. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, both calls in this loop and their exact interleaving; the target-method identity itself is OPEN for the reason given in §18.10, not simply unresolved.]**

### 18.6 A complete, confirmed mesh-draw producer chain

Starting from `FUN_00477400`'s mesh-shaped caller (§18.4), the following chain was traced upward, hop by hop, each one fully decompiled:

**`FUN_00b13bf0`** (`__thiscall`, two further explicit arguments — one a "holder" object matching the mesh-resource-holder structure already used throughout §9/§16.2 (its own `+0x30` field is the loaded Mesh header, exactly as established), one a range index): it indexes a `0x14`-byte-stride range-record array (its base read from the calling object's own `+0x3a0` field, `+8`) by the range index, matching **exactly** the draw-range record shape `spec-vertex-format.md` §8.2 documents. It reads the record's own `u16` sub-field at offset `+0x2` — the same sub-field §16.4 already identified as the "high16" selector `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` had been searching for — and uses it as an index into a **second**, `0x18`-byte-stride array read from the holder's own mesh header at `+0x18` (the vertex-range array already partially characterised via `FUN_00e72d00`/`FUN_00e72c60`/`FUN_00e73e80`), obtaining a vertex count from that array's own first field, plus a visibility byte at `+5` (gated via `FUN_00e7bf80`, exactly mirroring the gating already seen in `FUN_00e74880`'s own loop, §18.5). It then calls **`FUN_00477400`** with `D3DPT_TRIANGLESTRIP` (public value `5`), `BaseVertexIndex = 0`, `MinVertexIndex = 0`, `NumVertices` from the vertex-range array entry just resolved, `StartIndex` from the range record's own `+0x4` field, and `PrimitiveCount` from the range record's own `+0x8` field minus `2` — again the standard indexed-triangle-strip `IndexCount − 2` relation. **This is a real, non-hardcoded, per-range-record-resolved `DrawIndexedPrimitive` submission — the first one directly confirmed in this project's investigation history.** **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]** **Answering Team B's precise follow-up question directly (2026-09-28): the `+0x2` sub-field ("high16") is read as-is and used with no additional scaling, masking, or offset applied to it before indexing — it is multiplied by the target array's own stride (`0x18`) and added to that array's base, exactly the ordinary compiler-generated array-access pattern, and nothing else touches the value in between the read and the index computation. This is now the causal half of the joint finding recorded in `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.1 (detailed text) and §11.5 (summary table): `high16` is used, in the one confirmed real consumer, as a plain vertex-channel index.**

`FUN_00b13bf0` has exactly **one** caller: **`FUN_00b16d50`** (677 bytes). This function's own first argument is the same holder object (`+0x2f`-indexed field, i.e. `+0xbc`, exactly matching every other holder access in this document), and it contains a per-range `do`/`while` loop, gated by the same visibility/LOD pattern already established in `FUN_00e74880` (§18.5) and iterating up to the same `+0x3a0`-rooted range array's own count field. Inside the loop, per range, it resolves a per-item sub-batch count via `FUN_00e3f290`, then for each sub-item calls `FUN_00e3f740` (a technique/sub-batch selection call already seen inside `FUN_00e74880` itself, in its own technique-rebind branch) immediately followed by **`FUN_00b13bf0`**, passing the holder and the current range index. This is a second, independent per-item render-submission function, structurally parallel to `FUN_00e74880` but a distinct code path that this pass has now shown genuinely reaches a real draw. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

`FUN_00b16d50` itself has exactly **one** caller: **`FUN_00b17000`** (100 bytes). This function opens with the identical `+0x370` teardown-check idiom already documented for `FUN_00b13850` (§16, the already-known chain-start function) — strong circumstantial evidence that `FUN_00b17000`, `FUN_00b13850`, `FUN_00b138a0`, `FUN_00b13bf0`, and `FUN_00b13df0` (the last two both newly tied together this pass, the last already known from §16.2 as a callee of `FUN_00e74880` itself) are sibling methods on the same small object/vtable family in the same `0xb13xxx`/`0xb16xxx`/`0xb17xxx` neighbourhood. `FUN_00b17000` branches on a global flag (`DAT_0130ea40`) together with two non-null checks on its own object's `+0x3a0` and `+0x398` fields to decide between two alternatives: call **`FUN_00b16d50`** (the path just traced to a real draw, above) or call **`FUN_00e58a60`** — an address immediately adjacent to `FUN_00e58480`/`FUN_00e58b70`, the already-documented (§16.2/§16.3) vtable-`+0x80` chain that stalls at the still-unidentified `this+0x130` sub-object. **`FUN_00e58a60` itself was not opened this pass** — it is flagged, not investigated, as the natural place to check whether the two chains this document now documents (the vtable-`+0x80`/`FUN_00e58480` one, and the `FUN_00b13bf0` one closed here) are two genuinely different code paths for two different cases, or converge somewhere neither pass has yet reached. `FUN_00b17000` has **zero** direct callers found — it is reached only virtually, consistent with being a further vtable-slot override on the same class hierarchy `FUN_00b13850` (vtable `+0x28`, per §9/§16) and `FUN_00e58b70` (vtable `+0x60`, per §16.2) already sit on; which slot, and what the actual per-frame/per-visible-list walker that invokes it is, was not identified this pass. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for the dispatch and the sibling-idiom match; OPEN — the vtable slot `FUN_00b17000` itself occupies, and its own caller.]**

### 18.7 Summary: the producer question, narrowed to a genuinely closed path plus an honest remainder

Putting §18.3–§18.6 together, end to end, this pass confirms one complete, real chain from a resolved mesh draw-range record to an actual `DrawIndexedPrimitive` command-buffer write:

**`FUN_00b17000`** (vtable-dispatched, caller/slot not identified) → **`FUN_00b16d50`** (per-visible-item, per-range loop over the holder's range array) → **`FUN_00b13bf0`** (resolves the `0x14`-byte range record and its `0x18`-byte-stride vertex-range companion, extracting the "high16" selector along the way) → **`FUN_00477400`** (generic argument-preserving `DrawIndexedPrimitive` entry point) → **`FUN_004764d0`** (`RecordDrawIndexedPrimitive`, writes opcode `0x2a` with 6 arguments, record size `0x1c`) → replayed later by the already-confirmed opcode-42 handler at `0x0049D650` (§7).

This closes §14 items 1 and 2 **for this path**. It does not, and should not be read to, resolve the *other*, previously-more-prominent chain (`FUN_00b13850`→`FUN_00e58b70`→`FUN_00e74880`→vtable`+0x80`→`FUN_00e58480`→`this+0x130`) — that chain's own final hop is still unidentified (§18.5/§18.8), and whether it is a genuinely separate submission path (e.g. for a different LOD tier, geometry type, or the `rl_raycast_occluder` sibling class already known not to draw at all) or eventually reconverges with the path closed here was not determined. Both are real, disassembly-confirmed pieces of this engine's rendering; this document now documents one complete path and one still-partial one, rather than zero complete paths, which is where the previous two passes left it.

### 18.8 Honest remaining gaps

- **`FUN_00e58a60`** (§18.6) — the alternative branch `FUN_00b17000` can take instead of the now-closed `FUN_00b16d50` path — was not opened this pass.
- **`DAT_0351f8f8`'s vtable `+0x10`** (§18.5) — called identically from both `FUN_00e73e80`'s tail (§9) and `FUN_00e74880`'s own loop (§18.5) with the same three-argument shape. The object's class is now identified (`rl_renderer`, §18.10), but the vtable slot itself resolves to an address shared with roughly two dozen neighbouring slots on the same static vtable — genuinely confusing, not a simple miss, and reported as such rather than forced into either "resolved" or a silent re-statement of the old OPEN note (§18.10).
- **`this+0x130`** (§16.3) — the sub-object the vtable-`+0x80`/`FUN_00e58480` chain stalls at — remains unidentified; this pass did not add new evidence toward it, having found a different, independently-closable path instead. **Update (§22.1): the same `this+0x130`/vtable+0x1c call, with the identical high16-selector argument and vtable+0x24 gate, is now confirmed called directly inside the CLOSED `FUN_00b16d50` chain too — it is shared infrastructure between both chains, not evidence they diverge. Its class is still unidentified.**
- **The very top of the closed chain** — `FUN_00b17000` has no direct callers; the vtable slot it occupies and the per-frame/per-visible-list walker that actually invokes that slot were not identified. The chain is confirmed complete from "resolved range record" to "opcode-42 write," but not yet tied to a named "per-frame render pass" caller at its very top.
- **`FUN_0047b7c0`** (§18.4) — the second real caller of the generic `DrawIndexedPrimitive` entry point, structurally unrelated to mesh geometry — was identified but not investigated; flagged as a plausible trail/ribbon/rope-effect lead for a future pass, not claimed with any confidence beyond "a second genuine caller exists."

### 18.9 Evidence anchors added this pass

Material-bind functions: `FUN_00477060`, `FUN_00477030`, `FUN_00476f70`, `FUN_00476ff0` and their callees `FUN_00475930` (opcode `0x17`), `FUN_004758c0` (opcode `0x16`), `FUN_00475790` (opcode `0x14`, variable-length). Refuted candidate: `FUN_00473770` (556 bytes, decompiled in full; calls `FUN_0047a680`, `FUN_004755b0` (opcode `0xA`), `FUN_00e47fb0`/`FUN_00e47fd0`/`FUN_00e47fa0`, `FUN_00474f20`). The generic idiom's other confirmed instance: `FUN_004755b0`. The draw-record writers: `FUN_00476360` (`RecordDrawPrimitive`, opcode `0x28`) and `FUN_004764d0` (`RecordDrawIndexedPrimitive`, opcode `0x2a`). The generic entry point: `FUN_00477400`. The closed chain: `FUN_00b17000`, `FUN_00b16d50`, `FUN_00b13bf0`. Non-mesh caller of the same entry point: `FUN_0047b7c0`. Self-contained immediate-batch callers of `RecordDrawIndexedPrimitive` (not mesh-related, not pursued further): `FUN_00e5a290`, `FUN_00485dc0`, `FUN_005d80b0`, `FUN_00fcf8e0`, `FUN_004ad070`. `DAT_0351f8f8`'s class (`rl_renderer`, §18.10). Still fully open: `FUN_00e58a60`.

### 18.10 `DAT_0351f8f8`'s class identity resolved via RTTI — and a genuine complication found

**Technique used (relayed by the orchestrator mid-pass as a supplementary lead, not self-originated):** Microsoft C++ 32-bit RTTI stores a "Complete Object Locator" immediately before a class's vtable (at `vtable_address − 4`), containing a signature, two offsets, a pointer to a `TypeDescriptor` (whose own `+8` byte offset holds the class's null-terminated mangled name), and a pointer to a class-hierarchy descriptor. This does not require Ghidra's own RTTI analyzer to have run — the structure can be read directly out of the raw image once the vtable address itself is known.

**Finding the vtable address.** `DAT_0351f8f8` is zero in the shipped image (it is a runtime-constructed singleton, §4.4) and has exactly one constructor/destructor pair touching it directly: `FUN_00e38350` (constructor: initialises roughly two dozen fields, assigns `DAT_0351f8f8` to the new object, and installs vtable literal `0x0125a97c` at the object's own offset `+0`, plus a second, separate vtable-shaped pointer `0x0125a970` at offset `+4` — a second embedded interface, not investigated further) and `FUN_00e38400` (destructor: releases three owned sub-objects at the object's own `+0x50`, `DAT_02a8a804`, and `+0xc0`, each through a COM-style vtable-slot-`+8` "Release" call, before re-arming the vtable to a base-class placeholder and zeroing `DAT_0351f8f8`). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

**RTTI decode.** Reading the dword at `0x0125a97c − 4` (`0x0125a978`) yields a pointer into this same executable's own `.rdata` (`0x012beaa8`) — itself the expected shape of a Complete Object Locator (signature `0`, offset `0`, cdOffset `0`, a `TypeDescriptor` pointer at `0x01355cb8`, a class-hierarchy-descriptor pointer at `0x012c2700`, both also inside this executable's own image). The `TypeDescriptor`'s name field (`0x01355cb8 + 8`) reads the literal, null-terminated string **`.?AVrl_renderer@@`** — standard MSVC name mangling for a class named **`rl_renderer`**. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + RTTI structure decode, both pointers independently verified to land inside this executable's own image rather than being a coincidental garbage read.]** This closes §14 item 7 for the class-identity half of that item, and is consistent with, and reinforces, the "RL" (Render Library) naming already established from the boot-log literal strings (§3.6).

**The complication.** Dumping `rl_renderer`'s own vtable (`0x0125a97c`, 32 dwords read) finds only **4 distinct addresses** among the first four slots (`+0x0`: `FUN_00e389d0`; `+0x4`: `FUN_008a5bf0` — the same trivial no-op stub §16.2 already identified as `rl_raycast_occluder`'s own "doesn't draw" override; `+0x8`: `0x004835d0`; `+0xc`: `0x00435660` — one of the *exact two* candidate `this+0x130` constructor/destructor addresses §16.3 already flagged as unresolved) — and then **every single slot from `+0x10` through `+0x7c` inclusive — exactly 28 consecutive slots, orchestrator-recounted directly against the raw executable bytes (32 dwords read, not just the ones actually needed) — reads the identical address, `0x00ea2778`**, including the `+0x10`, `+0x24`, `+0x28`, and `+0x3c` slots this document has variously called from `FUN_00e73e80`, `FUN_00e74880`, `FUN_004a2e30`, and `FUN_00473770` over three passes. `0x00ea2778` itself is confirmed, by direct raw-byte read, to be a real, non-trivial routine (opens with a push of a global followed by a call, a conditional call through the result, then two further calls each with small fixed literal arguments) — a shape consistent with the pass's own description (a shared diagnostic/fail-soft or lazy-resolve handler, not a degenerate one-byte stub) — rather than a trivial no-op that a naive read might have mistaken it for. **This was not expected, and this document does not have a confirmed explanation for it.** The most plausible reading (**HYPOTHESIS, not confirmed**) is MSVC/link.exe identical-code-folding (`/OPT:ICF`) merging many originally-distinct but byte-identical trivial virtual-method bodies into one shared address, which would mean the `+0x10`/`+0x24`/`+0x28`/`+0x3c` calls this document has treated as calling different, distinguishable methods are, at the machine-code level, all calling the *same* shared routine — not necessarily wrong in effect (each call site still passes its own distinct arguments, which the shared routine may use polymorphically or partially ignore), but it means "what does `rl_renderer`'s vtable `+0x10` do" cannot be answered as a single distinguishable method the way `+0x0`/`+0x4`/`+0x8`/`+0xc` can. **This is reported plainly as a confusing result rather than forced into either "resolved" or "no new information," per this project's standing policy on not inventing an explanation once confirmable facts run out (`HANDOFF.md` §27.8).** A future pass with more time could check this by disassembling `0x00ea2778` to a proper function boundary and checking whether its own body branches on anything that would make its behaviour depend on which "logical" slot/call-site invoked it.

**Evidence anchors:** `FUN_00e38350`, `FUN_00e38400` (constructor/destructor); vtable `0x0125a97c`; RTTI Complete Object Locator `0x012beaa8`; `TypeDescriptor` `0x01355cb8` (name `.?AVrl_renderer@@`); shared vtable-slot target `0x00ea2778`; the two still-distinct real slots `0x004835d0` (`+0x8`, not investigated) and `0x00435660` (`+0xc`, the already-flagged `this+0x130` candidate, §16.3 — its presence on `rl_renderer`'s own vtable rather than confirmed as `this+0x130`'s own installer is noted but not claimed as a link).

## 19. Present/PresentEx Located (Secondary Task) — Opcodes 7 and 8 in the Existing Command-Buffer Dispatch Table

**Scope.** §14 item 10 (added 2026-09-25 following the external advisory review, §17) flagged that `Present`/`PresentEx` had never been located anywhere in this project's renderer-core work. This pass ran a whole-binary indirect-call census (the same corrected `MOV`-then-`CALL` idiom used throughout this document) for the public `IDirect3DDevice9::Present` vtable offset (`0x44`, slot 17) and the public `IDirect3DDevice9Ex::PresentEx` offset (`0x1E4`, slot 121 — the 3rd Ex-added method, derived from the same vtable layout that already confirmed `ResetEx` at `0x210`/slot 132 as the 14th Ex-added method, §4.2). Offset `0x44` alone is not selective (166 hits whole-binary, the same low-selectivity problem already on record for small displacements, `HANDOFF.md` §27.4) — each candidate was individually checked against the real object identity and the public method's own argument count, exactly the discipline §16.1 already established for this document, rather than trusted on the offset match alone.

### 19.1 `Present` located, opcode 7 — CONFIRMED

Two of the 166 offset-`0x44` hits (`0x0049ca5a`, `0x0049cac7`) sit **inside the already-confirmed opcode dispatch table's address range**, between the confirmed `EndScene` handler (opcode 6, `0x0049CA10`) and the confirmed `SetRenderTarget` handler (opcode 9, `0x0049CB00`). Reading the table itself directly confirms opcode 7's slot points at `0x0049ca30` and opcode 8's slot points at `0x0049caa0` — two previously-unidentified handlers sitting exactly in this gap.

**Opcode 7's handler (`0x0049ca30`)** calls `Present` on the device pointer at `DAT_03171b68` (the same replay-thread device pointer every other handler in §7 uses) with all four public parameters — source rect, dest rect, window override, dirty region — passed as `NULL`, confirmed by counting the pushed values leading into the call: five pushes total (four `0` literals plus the device pointer itself as the implicit `this`), matching the public `Present(RECT*, RECT*, HWND, RGNDATA*)` signature's own argument count exactly, the same calling-convention cross-check §4.4 already relied on to *rule out* a false match — here it *confirms* a true one. The call is bracketed by a before/after timer read (`FUN_00dad740`, called with the literal `1000000`, consistent with a microsecond-scale performance counter), and the elapsed delta is converted to a float and stored into `DAT_03171b70` — a new global, immediately adjacent to the already-known `DAT_03171b68`, almost certainly a "last frame/Present duration" metric. The handler's own record size is 8 bytes (opcode plus one argument dword); that one argument is not read anywhere in the handler body, so whatever the producer writes there is currently unused by the replay side. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, both the vtable offset and the exact public-API argument count.]**

**Opcode 8's handler (`0x0049caa0`)** is a second, parameterised `Present` variant, record size `0x20` (32 bytes, opcode plus 7 argument dwords): it branches on one argument field, and on the common path calls the same `Present` slot on the same `DAT_03171b68` device with a *non-null* source rect (a pointer into the record's own argument bytes, `+0x8`..`+0x14`, i.e. the caller supplied an actual rectangle rather than requesting a full-buffer flip), a null dest rect, a window-handle argument read from the record's own `+0x18` field as `hDestWindowOverride`, and a null dirty region — again five total pushed values, matching the public signature exactly. The alternative branch (when that first argument field is non-zero) instead calls a method at offset `+0xc` on a *different* object, whose pointer is itself stored in the record — plausibly an alternate swap-chain/viewport object's own present-equivalent method, not investigated further this pass. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for the offset-`0x44` branch and its argument count; HYPOTHESIS for the alternate-object branch's purpose.]**

No hit at all was found anywhere in the whole binary at the `PresentEx` offset (`0x1E4`). Given `DAT_03171ac8` (the Ex-specific device pointer, §4.2) is confirmed non-null only on the Ex device-creation path, and the replay driver's own device pointer `DAT_03171b68` is filled from whichever path device creation took (§4.3), the most likely reading is that this engine always calls the base `Present` method through the base interface pointer regardless of whether the underlying device object is Ex or not — which is legal (the base `Present` slot is inherited unchanged on an `IDirect3DDevice9Ex` object) even though it forgoes `PresentEx`'s extra dirty-region/flip-interval controls. **[HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred from the absence of any `PresentEx`-offset call anywhere in the binary, combined with the already-confirmed device-pointer architecture; not a direct disassembly proof of *why* `PresentEx` is unused, only that it demonstrably is.]**

### 19.2 The frame boundary is itself just another command-buffer opcode

The practical answer to "what happens once per frame" that this closes: **there is no separate per-frame loop that calls `Present` directly.** Ending a frame is recorded by the game/logic thread exactly like every other D3D9 call in this architecture (§6) — as an opcode-7 or opcode-8 command in the same growable buffer — and is only actually executed later, on `rl_command_thread`, when the replay driver's dispatch loop reaches that record in sequence (§6.3). This is consistent with, and strengthens, this document's own headline finding (§1/§6.1): the deferred command buffer is not just for per-draw state, it covers the entire frame including its own boundary.

### 19.3 Honest remaining gap

**The producer side — which function decides to record an opcode-7/8 `Present` command, and where in the game's own per-frame tick that decision is made — was not traced this pass.** Both opcode literals (`7`, `8`) are too common as plain integers for the same mechanical bare-register-literal scan that found `RecordDrawPrimitive`/`RecordDrawIndexedPrimitive` (§18.3) to be usable without a very high false-positive rate; finding the specific producer would need either a narrower structural anchor (e.g. searching specifically near the timer-pair idiom, or near known per-frame tick functions) or a dynamic trace. This is recorded as an open item rather than guessed at, per this project's standing policy (`HANDOFF.md` §27.8).

## 20. Producer-Side Follow-Up, Pass 4 -- Shader-Constant/Texture/Sampler Producers Found and Traced to Their Value Sources

**Scope of this pass.** Direct follow-up on §10/§14 items 3–5 and 13, and on the still-partial §18.10 vtable puzzle. Primary target: trace the PRODUCER side of the `SetVertexShaderConstantF`/`SetPixelShaderConstantF`/`SetTexture`/`SetSamplerState` opcodes §10 only located approximately, using the exact §18.2/§18.3 technique (a whole-binary scan for the compiled opcode-write idiom — a bare-register, no-displacement store of the opcode literal, immediately followed by the reserve-check/cursor-advance/thread-notify pattern), then trace those producers' own callers far enough to answer whether shader-constant/material binding uses this project's general CRC-32 name-hash routine (`FUN_00D9E8B0`/`FUN_00D9E740`). Secondary: sampler/texture-stage state, render-state, one §12 render-target site verified properly, and (per a supplementary lead relayed mid-pass) the §18.10 `rl_renderer` shared-vtable-slot puzzle. Method throughout: disposable Ghidra project `tools/gp_render5` (robocopied from `tools/ghidra_projects`), PowerShell-only headless invocation, Function boundaries and disassembly forced at every opcode-table slot and producer candidate before decompiling (§10's own approximate addresses turned out to sit inside functions that had never actually been disassembled in this project, not merely un-named).

### 20.1 §10's approximate handler list, corrected and completed with exact addresses/sizes

Forcing linear disassembly and Function creation across the opcode-table's whole code region (`0x0049C940`–`0x0049DDB0`) let every one of the 74 slots be read as a real Function with a real size for the first time (previously many were "not yet disassembled," including several §7 never touched). The eight handlers §10 could only place "near" an approximate address resolve to these exact opcodes:

| Opcode | Handler | Size | Public method | Vtable offset | Record shape |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 24 (0x18) | `0x0049D1C0` | 69B | `SetVertexShaderConstantF` | `0x178` | variable: header (StartRegister, Vector4fCount) + inline float payload, `Vector4fCount×16` bytes |
| 25 (0x19) | `0x0049D210` | 51B | `SetVertexShaderConstantF` | `0x178` | fixed 16B: StartRegister, Vector4fCount, a stored POINTER to float data (not copied into the buffer) |
| 27 (0x1B) | `0x0049D2A0` | 69B | `SetPixelShaderConstantF` | `0x1B4` | variable, same shape as opcode 24 |
| 28 (0x1C) | `0x0049D2F0` | 51B | `SetPixelShaderConstantF` | `0x1B4` | fixed 16B by-reference, same shape as opcode 25 |
| 31 (0x1F) | `0x0049D380` | 41B | `SetVertexShader` | `0x170` | fixed 8B: shader pointer |
| 32 (0x20) | `0x0049D3B0` | 41B | `SetPixelShader` | `0x1AC` | fixed 8B: shader pointer |
| 36 (0x24) | `0x0049D4A0` | 99B | `SetSamplerState`+`SetTexture` (combined, see below) | `0x114` then `0x104` | fixed 16B: stage, texture pointer, sRGB flag |
| 37 (0x25) | `0x0049D510` | 53B | `SetTexture` (vertex-texture-sampler stages) | `0x104` | fixed 12B: raw stage index, texture pointer |

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly, all rows; `tools/scratchpad/ShaderConstProducer2.java`/`ShaderConstProducer3.java`.]** Opcode 36 resolves a genuine ambiguity in §10's own approximate citations: the two addresses §10 attributed separately to "SetSamplerState's second handler" (`~0x0049D4D9`) and "SetTexture's first handler" (`~0x0049D4F0`) are in fact two DIFFERENT device calls inside the SAME one handler/opcode — it first calls `SetSamplerState(stage, D3DSAMP_SRGBTEXTURE, flag)` (state-type literal `11`, matching the public `D3DSAMPLERSTATETYPE` enum's `D3DSAMP_SRGBTEXTURE` value exactly) with the value gated on both a per-record flag and a global sRGB-enable byte (`DAT_03395e80`), then immediately calls `SetTexture` on the same stage with a texture pointer stored in the same record. Opcode 37's handler adds the literal `0x101` (257 decimal) to the record's raw stage index before calling `SetTexture` — `257` is the public numeric value of `D3DVERTEXTEXTURESAMPLER0`, confirming this opcode is specifically for binding a texture to a vertex-shader texture-fetch sampler stage, distinct from opcode 36's ordinary pixel-stage binding.

### 20.2 The ten producer-writer functions, found by the exact §18.2/§18.3 idiom scan

A whole-binary scan for the bare-register, no-displacement opcode-write instruction for each of the eight opcode literals above (`0x18`,`0x19`,`0x1B`,`0x1C`,`0x1F`,`0x20`,`0x24`,`0x25`) found 34 raw hits; all ten genuine ones cluster tightly in `0x00475B50`–`0x00476281`, immediately adjacent to (and in the identical source-file neighbourhood as) the already-documented §18.2/§18.9 `Record*` family (`FUN_004755B0`, `FUN_00475790`, `FUN_00475930`, `FUN_004758C0`). Every other hit is a genuine false positive of the exact kind §18.3 already put on record — large, unrelated CRT internals (`__fsopen`, `__wfsopen`, `__tsopen_nolock`, `__write_nolock`) or one specific large (4,836-byte) unrelated function, `FUN_00DE4DC0`, that happens to contain bare-register stores of several of the wanted literals for unrelated reasons (it recurred as noise for the render-state/sampler-state scan too, §20.6 below — flagged explicitly rather than treated as a hit). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + empirical, `tools/scratchpad/ShaderConstProducer4.java`.]**

| Function | Size | Opcode | Producer shape |
|---|---|---|---|
| `FUN_00475B50` | 129B | 0x18 | `RecordSetVertexShaderConstantF` — array form: (StartRegister, floatArrayPointer, vector4Count); copies `vector4Count×16` bytes from the caller's own pointer into the command buffer |
| `FUN_00475BE0` | 148B | 0x18 | `RecordSetVertexShaderConstantF` — single-Vector4-by-value form: (StartRegister, x, y, z); w is implicitly 0, count fixed at 1; the four floats are the caller's own by-value arguments, not read through a pointer |
| `FUN_00475C80` | 108B | 0x19 | `RecordSetVertexShaderConstantF` — by-reference form: (StartRegister, floatDataPointer, count); stores the pointer itself, not a copy |
| `FUN_00475D80` | 129B | 0x1B | `RecordSetPixelShaderConstantF` — array form, mirrors `FUN_00475B50` |
| `FUN_00475E10` | 148B | 0x1B | `RecordSetPixelShaderConstantF` — single-Vector4-by-value form, mirrors `FUN_00475BE0` |
| `FUN_00475EB0` | 108B | 0x1C | `RecordSetPixelShaderConstantF` — by-reference form, mirrors `FUN_00475C80` |
| `FUN_00475FB0` | 94B | 0x1F | `RecordSetVertexShader`(shaderPointer) |
| `FUN_00476010` | 94B | 0x20 | `RecordSetPixelShader`(shaderPointer) |
| `FUN_004761B0` | 108B | 0x24 | `RecordSetTexture`, combined stage+sRGB form: (stage, texturePointer, sRGBflag) |
| `FUN_00476220` | 101B | 0x25 | `RecordSetTexture`, vertex-texture-sampler form: (rawStage, texturePointer) — the `+0x101` base is added on the REPLAY side (§20.1), not by this producer |

Each follows the exact reserve-check / bare-register opcode write / argument write / cursor-advance / thread-conditional-notify idiom already documented in §18.2, confirming genuine membership in the same `Record*` family rather than a coincidental literal match. Four trivial one-line pass-through wrappers sit immediately adjacent, in the same `0x00477000`–`0x00477420` neighbourhood as the already-known generic `DrawIndexedPrimitive` entry point `FUN_00477400` (§18.4) and the four material-bind functions (§18.1): `FUN_0047727F`→`FUN_00475B50`, `FUN_004772A0`→`FUN_00475C80`, `FUN_004772DF`→`FUN_00475D80`, `FUN_00477300`→`FUN_00475EB0`. Each does nothing but forward to its underlying producer — the same "generic, argument-preserving top-level entry point" shape §18.4 already established for draws.

### 20.3 What actually feeds vertex/pixel shader constants — four concrete, disassembly-confirmed sourcing patterns

Tracing a deliberately diverse sample of the producers' own callers (full caller census in §20.9; `tools/scratchpad/ShaderConstProducer5.java`/`7.java`) found four distinct, concrete patterns for where a shader-constant's target REGISTER NUMBER and its VALUE each come from — no two identical, all disassembly-confirmed:

**(a) Per-object world transform — `FUN_00489140` (283 bytes).** Given a per-render-item pointer, it uploads exactly three Vector4 registers (a 3×4 affine matrix — the standard shape for a world matrix in this era, the implicit fourth row `[0,0,0,1]` omitted) via the array-form producer (`FUN_00475B50`), reading the twelve floats directly from the render item's own memory (three consecutive rows starting 4 bytes into a sub-object) — the starting register number comes from a NAMED global, `DAT_01354700`. It then uploads two more individual Vector4 constants via the single-Vector4 producer (`FUN_00475BE0`), each from a different fixed triple of struct fields on the same render item (`+0x44/+0x48/+0x4c` and `+0x38/+0x3c/+0x40`), register numbers again from named globals (`DAT_01354704`, `DAT_01354708`), plus a FOURTH constant conditionally uploaded from GLOBAL (not per-object) floats (`DAT_031729a8/ac/b0`) gated by a flag (`DAT_03171b10`) — most plausibly a shared, engine-wide parameter (a previous-frame matrix for motion blur, or a wind/animation parameter) rather than anything per-instance. Immediately afterward it calls the same `rl_renderer` vtable`+0x10` method already documented in §18.5/§18.10, on the same render item — strong circumstantial confirmation this function runs in the same per-visible-item submission context §18.5 already traced. **Answer for the world matrix specifically: read positionally from the render item's own instance data at draw time, never looked up by name or hash.**

**(b) Per-light parameters — `FUN_0049BDB0` (440 bytes).** Given a pointer to what is almost certainly a light record, it uploads four CONSECUTIVE Vector4 registers at LITERAL register numbers `0x14`–`0x17` (not named globals) via the single-Vector4 producer, sourced from the light record's own fields (`+0x14/+0x18/+0x1c`, plus a further block resolved through a helper call at `+0x2c`) — consistent with a light-space transform occupying a fixed, reserved block of registers. A fifth register, literal `0x1b`, receives a colour value pre-multiplied by an intensity scalar (three separate multiplications of a base-colour field by an intensity field, both read from the same light record). **Second concrete pattern: a fixed, engine-reserved block of literal registers for "the current light," filled per-light from a light-record structure — again positional.**

**(c) Shadow-cascade / whole-frame lighting setup — `FUN_004AB730` (2,593 bytes) and its close sibling `FUN_004AAD00` (2,604 bytes, structurally near-identical, most plausibly a second cascade or a point-light variant).** Both are large, per-FRAME (not per-material, not per-object) setup functions that build shadow view/light-space matrices from a static 4×8 pre-baked matrix table (`DAT_0125bb60` and its immediate neighbours) indexed by two rotating cursor globals (`DAT_03171b98`/`DAT_03171b9c`), combine them with a shadow-bias/depth-range pair derived from global engine-config floats (the `DAT_0132e15x`/`DAT_0132e17x`/`DAT_0132e18x` cluster) and a runtime helper (`FUN_00e4a180`), and upload the results via BOTH the array producer (`FUN_00475D80`) and the single-Vector4 producer (`FUN_00475E10`) — register numbers this time from a THIRD kind of source, a different family of NAMED GLOBALS (the `DAT_0132e1xx` cluster, distinct from `FUN_00489140`'s `DAT_01354xxx` cluster). Both also drive sampler-stage state directly (three calls to the texture-stage helper `FUN_0047a680` with stage arguments `1`/`2`/`3`) and read fixed sub-offsets (`0x2618`/`0x2624`/`0x26d0`/`0x26dc`/`0x2a68`/`0x2a74`) of the `rl_renderer` singleton's own `+0xc0` sub-object — almost certainly shadow-map-specific fields of that sub-object. **Third concrete pattern: whole-frame shadow/lighting setup, sourced from a static matrix table plus global engine config, register indices from a dedicated named-global cluster.**

**(d) Post-process / depth-of-field-shaped setup — `FUN_004A9420` (2,519 bytes) — CLOSED, register indices resolve to a permanent sentinel, not a real number (§20.11).** Builds a view-space reconstruction matrix (two matrix-multiply helper calls) and near/far-plane-derived scale factors from the current camera's own fields, then uploads six pixel-shader constants at register numbers read from a PARAMETER STRUCT passed into the function (fixed offsets `+0x40` through `+0x54` of that struct). Traced fully in §20.11: the struct is not a call-site temporary but a fixed sub-offset (`+0x4d8a0`) of a large, persistent, RTTI-identified `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` instance; the function has exactly one call site anywhere in the binary; and two independent exhaustive whole-binary scans confirm the ONLY code that ever writes those six fields is the class's own constructor, which sets all six to the sentinel `-1` (`0xFFFFFFFF`) — no other function in the shipped executable ever assigns them a different value. **This closes the "plausibly connects to name-based resolution" lead from a different direction than hoped: there is no live register number to look up in a CTAB at all.** For every instance of this class in this build, `FUN_004A9420`'s six PSCF/array-PSCF calls execute with `StartRegister = -1`, not a genuine D3D9 register index. See §20.11 for the full trace and evidence anchors.

### 20.4 The name-hash question, answered directly

Across all ten producer functions (§20.2) and every caller function decompiled this pass — spanning the world-matrix, per-light, shadow-cascade, and post-process categories above, deliberately sampled across different subsystems rather than clustered in one call site — **none call `FUN_00D9E8B0`/`FUN_00D9E740`, and none index a hash-bucket table the way `spec-customization-data.md`'s bundle-name recipe or this project's vehicle material-binding code do.** [HIGH CONFIDENCE — not exhaustive; dozens of further callers in the full census (§20.9) were not individually decompiled.] The register-INDEX side of every shader-constant call observed this pass is one of: a literal constant, a named global, or a struct field most plausibly resolved once at shader-load time from the shader's own `CTAB` data. The VALUE side is always read positionally, from render-item / light / camera / global-config memory — never through a runtime name lookup. **Shader-constant binding in this engine appears to be a genuinely different mechanism from the CRC-32 name-hash scheme used for on-disk asset/bundle naming elsewhere in this project — the two solve different problems (persistent-asset naming vs. picking a register number for an already-compiled shader), and this pass found no evidence they share code.** This is offered as the primary answer to the task's central question, honestly scoped to what was actually traced rather than claimed as an exhaustive negative.

### 20.5 Samplers and texture-stage binding

Opcode 36's combined `SetSamplerState`(D3DSAMP_SRGBTEXTURE)+`SetTexture` producer (`FUN_004761B0`, §20.2) has a wide caller set spanning both the shadow-setup functions of §20.3(c) and a device-lost cleanup routine, `FUN_00474D00` (122 bytes): on device loss it walks a 16-entry texture-stage-handle array (a render object's own `+0x8c8` field) and a 4-entry vertex-texture-stage array (`+0x918`), zeroing each live entry and calling the matching `SetTexture` producer (opcode 36's `FUN_004761B0` for the 16 ordinary stages, opcode 37's `FUN_00476220` for the 4 vertex-texture stages) to unbind it — confirming both producers are also the unbind path, not just the bind path. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/ShaderConstProducer8.java`.]** No sampler/texture caller decompiled this pass showed any name-hash lookup either; texture pointers are read positionally from render-item/material sub-object fields the same way the shader-constant values are (§20.3).

### 20.6 Render state: `RecordSetRenderState` found

The same idiom scan for opcode 18 (`SetRenderState`, already confirmed in §7 at handler `0x0049CFE0`) found exactly one genuine producer: **`FUN_00475720`** (101 bytes) = `RecordSetRenderState`(state, value), fixed 12-byte record — matching §7's own confirmed record size exactly. (`FUN_00DE4DC0`, the same 4,836-byte unrelated function flagged as noise in §20.2, recurred here too, containing coincidental bare-register stores of both the `0x12` and `0x13` literals — explicitly excluded, not counted as a hit.) Its 7 callers: two small (~100-byte) wrappers immediately adjacent to the other trivial top-level entry points (`FUN_00477F30`, `FUN_00477FA0`, not individually traced further), the device bring-up/reset routine `FUN_0047C150` itself (§3.6, setting baseline render state once at startup), and **`FUN_00487460`** (1,383 bytes, called twice) — which, per §20.9's caller table, ALSO calls the per-light single-Vector4 constant producers, making it a plausible "bind this light's full render state and constants" function; not individually decompiled this pass. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for the producer and its callers; the caller functions' own bodies are OPEN for a future pass.]** SetSamplerState's OWN dedicated opcode (19, distinct from opcode 36's combined form) did not turn up a clean dedicated producer under this same literal-store scan — **OPEN**, worth a future check with a wider net on the idiom (e.g. it may be folded into the same accumulator/batch mechanism §18.2 already documented for opcode `0x14`, rather than having its own dedicated bare-register writer).

### 20.7 One §12 render-target site verified, and REFUTED

Per the task's instruction to verify §12's "unverified offset hits" properly rather than trust the offset, `0x00e70380` (the "mesh/vehicle module" entry in §12's twelve-site list) was fully disassembled. **The call at `0x00e70450` reads `[thisVtable + 0x94]` and calls it with the implicit `this` only — zero additional stack-pushed arguments.** The public `IDirect3DDevice9::SetRenderTarget(DWORD RenderTargetIndex, IDirect3DSurface9* pRenderTarget)` takes two explicit parameters; under the `__thiscall`-style convention this project's disassembly consistently uses elsewhere (the same convention §4.4/§16.1 already used to refute a different coincidental vtable match), a genuine call to the real device method at that slot would need two pushed arguments. There are none. **This confirms §14 item 13's caution directly for this one site: it is a coincidental offset match on some other engine-internal class, not a real `SetRenderTarget` call.** The object in question (reached via a plain register, not `DAT_0351f8f8`) is some per-viewport/pass-setup class local to the `0xe7xxxx` module — its own fields include a small resource-handle pair around `+0xb0`/`+0xb8`, a nested viewport-shaped field at `+0x180`, and three calls into the `rl_renderer` singleton's own `+0xc0` sub-object with literal arguments `6`/`7`/`8` (most plausibly texture-stage or cvar indices, not investigated further) — but its `+0x94` slot is not a D3D9 device call. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/ShaderConstProducer11.java`/`13.java`.]** The other eleven §12 sites were not individually re-checked this pass; this is one concrete confirming data point for the existing caution, not a full re-audit.

### 20.8 The `rl_renderer` shared-vtable-slot puzzle (§18.10) — resolved for its cause, sharpened for what it now implies

Per a supplementary technique relayed mid-pass: two cheap checks were run against the 28-slot shared-address puzzle §18.10 left open (every slot `+0x10`–`+0x7c` on `rl_renderer`'s vtable, `0x0125a97c`, reading the identical address `0x00ea2778`).

**Check (a) — does the shared function's own behaviour depend only on `this`/arguments?** No. `0x00ea2778` (42 bytes, fully disassembled) takes no meaningful arguments and ignores whichever nominal slot reached it: it decodes an encoded, possibly-null diagnostic-hook function pointer via the CRT's own `__decode_pointer` (on `DAT_02cc4da4`) and calls it if present, then unconditionally calls the CRT's `__NMSG_WRITE` with message index `0x19` (decimal 25) — the well-known, publicly-documented MSVC CRT runtime-error index for **"R6025 — pure virtual function call"** — followed by two further CRT calls (`FUN_00eaeed1`, `FUN_00eaedba`) consistent with the CRT's own termination path. **This is the standard, compiler-generated `_purecall` trap MSVC installs into a vtable slot for a pure-virtual method a class does not itself override — not a linker identical-code-folding artefact, and not a hidden-context-dependent shared handler.** [CONFIRMED — disassembly; citing the public CRT message index and `_purecall` mechanism by name, not proprietary code.]

**Check (b) — does the same address appear on a base class's vtable in `rl_renderer`'s own RTTI hierarchy?** Yes, and the hierarchy itself explains the puzzle directly: walking the ClassHierarchyDescriptor from `rl_renderer`'s already-known Complete Object Locator (`0x012beaa8`) lists **four** base classes — `rl_renderer` itself, **`rl_irenderer`** (an "i"-prefixed, almost certainly abstract-interface, base), `rl_base_object`, and a CRTP template instantiation `rl_singleton<rl_renderer>`. This fully accounts for 28 nominally-different slots sharing one address: they are virtual methods declared on the abstract interface `rl_irenderer` that this build's `rl_renderer` never overrides, so the compiler filled every one of those slots with the shared `_purecall` trap — exactly check (b)'s predicted "inherited base-class default," not a folded custom handler. **This resolves §18.10's own "cause unconfirmed" note.** [CONFIRMED — disassembly + RTTI structure decode, `tools/scratchpad/ShaderConstProducer10.java`.]

**A sharper puzzle this created — RESOLVED (statically) in §21.** If slots `+0x10`/`+0x24`/`+0x28`/`+0x3c` on THIS exact vtable are genuine `_purecall` traps, the real, disassembly-confirmed calls this document already made through those same slots at runtime (§9, §18.5, §20.3a) should abort the process, which plainly does not happen in the shipped game. **§21's follow-up refutes the obvious escape hatch (a later derived-class vtable swap) with two exhaustive whole-binary negatives, and confirms the trap really does terminate the process (not a neutered no-op) — narrowing the open question to "why is the call site's own gating condition apparently always false for real content," a precisely-scoped, well-anchored item rather than the original open-ended puzzle.**
