Callers decompiled or at least enumerated this pass, by producer: `FUN_00475B50` (array VSCF) — `FUN_0049C530`×2, `FUN_004A3360`, `FUN_00489140` (§20.3a), one raw call site `0x0047727F` (its own trivial wrapper). `FUN_00475BE0` (single-Vector4 VSCF) — `FUN_0049C6A0`×2, `FUN_00487460`×2 (§20.6), `FUN_00489140`×2, `FUN_004AC1E0`×2, `FUN_004935B0`×2, `FUN_0049BB40`×3, `FUN_0049BDB0`×5 (§20.3b). `FUN_00475C80` (by-ref VSCF) — one caller, its own trivial wrapper `0x004772A0`. `FUN_00475D80` (array PSCF) — `FUN_0049C530`×2, `FUN_004A3360`, `FUN_004847C0`, `FUN_00484910`×2, `FUN_00482230`, `FUN_00484E60`, `FUN_004935B0`, `FUN_004AB730`×3 (§20.3c), `FUN_004A9F10`×2, `FUN_004AA2D0`×3, `FUN_004A9420`×2 (§20.3d), one raw call site `0x004772DF`. `FUN_00475E10` (single-Vector4 PSCF) — `FUN_0049C6A0`×2, `FUN_00482230`×5, `FUN_00487460`, `FUN_00487A90`, `FUN_004935B0`×3, `FUN_004AB730`×5, `FUN_004A9F10`×4, `FUN_004AA2D0`×3, `FUN_004A8900`×3 (the already-known §16.1 viewport/aspect-ratio function — confirming its FOV/aspect-ratio-derived floats really do end up as pixel-shader constants), `FUN_004A9420`×10 (§20.3d), two raw call sites near `0x00499AF5`. `FUN_00475EB0` (by-ref PSCF) — one caller, its own trivial wrapper `0x00477300`. `FUN_00475FB0` (SetVertexShader) — two raw call sites near `0x004748D4`. `FUN_00476010` (SetPixelShader) — two raw call sites near `0x004748E0`. `FUN_004761B0` (SetTexture+sRGB) — `FUN_00474D00` (§20.5), `FUN_004908D0`, `FUN_0048FEF0`, `FUN_004AAD00`×2 (§20.3c sibling), `FUN_004AB730`×2, `FUN_004A22C0`, `FUN_00497A40`, `FUN_00498900`, `FUN_0049ACB0`, `FUN_00474C80`. `FUN_00476220` (SetTexture vertex-tex) — `FUN_00474D00` (§20.5). `FUN_00475720` (RecordSetRenderState) — `FUN_00477F30`×2, `FUN_00477FA0`×2, `FUN_0047C150`, `FUN_00487460`×2.

New evidence anchors this pass: opcode handlers `0x0049D1C0`/`0x0049D210`/`0x0049D2A0`/`0x0049D2F0`/`0x0049D380`/`0x0049D3B0`/`0x0049D4A0`/`0x0049D510` (§20.1); producers `FUN_00475B50`, `FUN_00475BE0`, `FUN_00475C80`, `FUN_00475D80`, `FUN_00475E10`, `FUN_00475EB0`, `FUN_00475FB0`, `FUN_00476010`, `FUN_004761B0`, `FUN_00476220`, `FUN_00475720` (§20.2/§20.6); trivial top-level wrappers `FUN_0047727F`, `FUN_004772A0`, `FUN_004772DF`, `FUN_00477300`; value-source functions `FUN_00489140`, `FUN_0049BDB0`, `FUN_004AB730`, `FUN_004AAD00`, `FUN_004A9420` (§20.3); named register-index global clusters `DAT_01354700`–`0c` and `DAT_0132e1xx` (distinct clusters, §20.3a/c); cleanup function `FUN_00474D00` (§20.5); refuted render-target site `0x00e70380`/call `0x00e70450` (§20.7); `_purecall` trap `0x00ea2778`, its CRT callees `__decode_pointer`/`__NMSG_WRITE`/`FUN_00eaeed1`/`FUN_00eaedba`, and the RTTI base-class chain `rl_irenderer`/`rl_base_object`/`rl_singleton<rl_renderer>` (§20.8). ### 20.11 `FUN_004A9420`'s parameter-struct register indices — CLOSED: a permanent sentinel, not a resolvable register

**Scope.** Direct follow-up on §20.3(d)'s open lead and §20.10's note about it, prompted by a request to hand a peer team a concrete register number for a mechanical shader constant-table lookup. Method: disposable Ghidra project `tools/gp_render6` (robocopied from `tools/ghidra_projects`), PowerShell-only headless invocation, `tools/scratchpad/ParamStructTrace1.java` through `ParamStructTrace11.java`.

**The call site.** `FUN_004A9420` has **exactly one reference anywhere in the binary** — a direct, unconditional `CALL` (not virtual/vtable-dispatched) from `FUN_00494280` (74 bytes) at `0x004942c3`, confirmed by an exhaustive reference-type dump (every reference type, not just call-type). `FUN_00494280` receives a pointer to a large object in a register at entry, advances it by the literal `+0x4d8a0`, and pushes the result as `FUN_004A9420`'s one stack argument. **The "parameter struct" is therefore not a call-site-local temporary built fresh from literals — it is a fixed sub-offset of a persistent, long-lived object.** Concretely: struct offsets `+0x40`/`+0x44`/`+0x48`/`+0x4c`/`+0x50`/`+0x54` are ABSOLUTE offsets `+0x4d8e0`/`+0x4d8e4`/`+0x4d8e8`/`+0x4d8ec`/`+0x4d8f0`/`+0x4d8f4` on that containing object. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]**

**The containing object's identity (bonus, via public RTTI mechanics, same technique already used for `rl_renderer`).** Its vtable is `0x012A142C`; walking the standard MSVC RTTI chain (CompleteObjectLocator → TypeDescriptor / ClassHierarchyDescriptor, per §18.10/§20.8/§21) names the class **`rl_d3d_scene_renderer`**, deriving from `rl_scene_renderer`, deriving from `rl_base_object` — a sibling of the already-documented `rl_renderer`/`rl_irenderer` family, but a genuinely different class and a different live instance from `rl_renderer`'s own singleton `DAT_0351f8f8`. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + RTTI structure decode.]**

**Full call chain (each hop confirmed by direct disassembly).** A virtually-dispatched per-frame method, `FUN_00494440` (478 bytes — has **zero** direct callers anywhere in the binary, reached only through a vtable slot, receiving the scene-renderer instance in the standard `__thiscall` register), calls `FUN_004942d0` (358 bytes, forwards the same pointer unchanged) at `0x0049445d`, which calls `FUN_00494280` at `0x004943ed`, which computes `instance+0x4d8a0` and calls `FUN_004A9420` at `0x004942c3`. The same `FUN_00494280` call also touches two OTHER fixed sub-offsets of the identical instance immediately beforehand — `+0x4d58c` (passed to `FUN_00482230`, one of §20.3(c)'s shadow-cascade-adjacent PSCF callers) and `+0x4d558` (passed to `FUN_004A8900`, the already-documented §16.1/§20.9 FOV/aspect-ratio function) — confirming all three "value-source" functions this document has traced into the post-process/shadow/viewport family are sub-objects of ONE `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` instance, not independent objects.

**Correction to this task's own framing.** The brief that opened this line of inquiry characterized `FUN_004A9420` as called from within `FUN_004AB730`/`FUN_004AAD00`-family shadow-cascade code and `FUN_004A9F10`/`FUN_004AA2D0`. Disassembly does not support this: per §20.9's own caller census, `FUN_004AB730`, `FUN_004AAD00`, `FUN_004A9F10`, `FUN_004AA2D0`, and `FUN_004A9420` are five separate, SIBLING top-level callers of the same shader-constant producers (`FUN_00475D80`/`FUN_00475E10`) — none of them calls `FUN_004A9420`, and `FUN_004A9420`'s own sole caller (`FUN_00494280`, above) is unrelated to any of them. Flagged here so the misreading isn't repeated.

**The population site, and why there is no register number to hand off.** A whole-binary scan for any `MOV`/`LEA` instruction storing to any of the six absolute offsets (`+0x4d8e0` … `+0x4d8f4`), on any base register, found exactly six hits — all six in ONE function, `FUN_0048ffa0` (141 bytes), the class's own constructor (it installs the vtable literal `0x012A142C` at the object's own `+0`, the standard construction idiom this project already uses to identify constructors). It sets a register to `-1` (`OR EDI,0xFFFFFFFF`) and stores that same `-1` into all six target fields (and a sibling six-field block at `+0x4d8c8`..`+0x4d8dc`) in a straight, unconditional sequence — a sentinel reset, not real content. **A second, independent whole-binary scan** for any place that computes a pointer via the exact literal `+0x4d8a0` (the only way another function could reach these fields through a small relative offset instead of the big absolute one) found only two sites in the entire binary: the already-known `FUN_00494280` call above, and the matching sub-object destructor call inside the class's own destructor (`FUN_00490060`, reached via `FUN_00490030`'s vtable rearm — the standard destructor pattern, confirmed as the only OTHER site anywhere that installs the same `0x012A142C` vtable literal, ruling out a second constructor variant). **Neither of those two `+0x4d8a0` sites writes anything to the struct** — one only reads it (`FUN_004A9420`), the other only tears it down. Two complementary, exhaustive scans, zero hits for any writer other than the constructor's `-1` reset. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, exhaustive whole-binary scans; `tools/scratchpad/ParamStructTrace5.java`/`7.java`/`11.java`.]**

**Conclusion.** `FUN_004A9420`'s six pixel-shader-constant `StartRegister` arguments are the compile-time sentinel value **-1 (`0xFFFFFFFF`)** at the one real call site this binary contains, for every live instance of `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` — never a valid D3D9 register index (valid `ps_3_0` float-constant indices are non-negative). This is not "computed at runtime from something unresolvable" — it is a **CONFIRMED, exhaustively-checked negative**: no code path anywhere in this executable ever assigns these fields anything but the constructor's sentinel. **There is no concrete register number available for a constant-table lookup on this lead** — the honest answer is that the field is permanently unpopulated in the shipped binary, not that its real value is merely hard to trace. (The six-block upload IS reachable at runtime — it sits behind a separate gating check on a local flag, structurally similar to §21's gating-condition finding for `rl_renderer`'s shared `_purecall` slots — but that gate's truth value doesn't change this conclusion: even when the six PSCF calls do execute, they push `-1`.)

**Stretch goal (which `.fxo_pc` shader is bound here) — OPEN, not reached.** No call to either `SetPixelShader`/`SetVertexShader` producer (`FUN_00476010`/`FUN_00475FB0`) or to the shader-object bind method (`0x00474890`, `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5) appears anywhere in the four functions this trace covers (`FUN_004A9420`, `FUN_00494280`, `FUN_004942d0`, `FUN_00494440`) or in the class's constructor/destructor. Consistent with this engine's deferred command-buffer architecture (§6): `SetPixelShader` and `SetPixelShaderConstantF` are independent opcodes, recorded at whatever points in program order the game logic happens to call their respective `Record*` producers, and nothing in this trace's own call graph records a shader bind. Given the register-number question above already closes as a negative, resolving the bound shader would not enable the hoped-for join anyway — flagged honestly as unresolved, not pursued further.

**Evidence anchors:** call chain `FUN_00494440`→`FUN_004942d0`→`FUN_00494280`→`FUN_004A9420` (calls at `0x0049445d`/`0x004943ed`/`0x004942c3`); struct-base computation `0x004942bc` (`ADD ESI,0x4d8a0`); six field reads `0x004a9727`/`0x004a9750`/`0x004a9784`/`0x004a97ba`/`0x004a97ee`/`0x004a9859` (all `[EBX+0x40..0x54]`); constructor `FUN_0048ffa0` (vtable install at `0x0048ffa8`, six sentinel stores `0x00490005`–`0x00490023`); destructor pair `FUN_00490030`/`FUN_00490060` (sub-object cleanup call at `0x0049007f`/`0x00490085`); vtable `0x012A142C`, COL `0x012C3AC8`, RTTI names `rl_d3d_scene_renderer`/`rl_scene_renderer`/`rl_base_object`; sibling sub-offsets `+0x4d558` (`FUN_004A8900`)/`+0x4d58c` (`FUN_00482230`) on the same instance. Scripts: `tools/scratchpad/ParamStructTrace1.java` through `ParamStructTrace11.java`.

### 20.12 Producer-Side Follow-Up, Pass 6 -- One Named Vehicle-Body Shader (`ir_sr3carpaint_gr_v.fxo_pc`) Traced Register-by-Register (Peer-Team Join Request)

**Scope.** Direct follow-up requested for Team B's first real shader-driven vehicle-body draw (`car_4dr_genki_0` through `ir_sr3carpaint_gr_v.fxo_pc`, §22.11's rank-6 stem, the single most common real car-paint shader by material-record count, §22.11), which currently substitutes PLACEHOLDER constant values (identity objTM, zero instance params, flat specular `8.0`) because no prior pass had pinned this *one* shader's own `CTAB`-declared constant layout to its exact engine-side producer, register by register. Method: extracted the real file directly from `shaders.vpp_pc` (entry 495 of 1,693, 19,388 bytes) via the already-resolved mode-(a) physical-offset rule (`spec-vpp-container.md` §7, `tools/harnesses/vpp_modea.py`); read its header/`CTAB` with `tools/harnesses/fxo_parse.py`; wrote a new public-format D3D9 token walker (`tools/scratchpad/d3d9_tokens.py`) that finds `DEF`/`DEFI`/`DEFB` instructions (Microsoft's own public compile-time-literal-constant opcodes) to settle, byte-for-byte, which declared registers are genuine runtime-set constants versus compiled literals. Disassembly: disposable Ghidra project `tools/gp_render8` (robocopied from the master `tools/ghidra_projects`), PowerShell-only headless invocation, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace1.java` through `CarPaintTrace11.java`.

#### 20.12.1 The file is a 5-pass shader, not the 1-pass shape §7/§9 sampled

Unlike the single-pass sample `spec-fxo-format.md` §7.3/§9 used (`rfg-skybox-meteors.fxo_pc`: 1 VS + 1 PS + one T8 pass), `ir_sr3carpaint_gr_v.fxo_pc` declares **5 complete (VS, PS) pairs** via its own T8 table (header counts `nVS=5, nPS=5,` T8 count `5`), each an individually valid, self-terminating `vs_3_0`/`ps_3_0` blob. The header's role table (`spec-fxo-format.md` §7.4) maps roles **4→pass 0, 5→pass 1, 6→pass 2, 7→pass 3, 1→pass 4** (role-index bytes `(-1,4,-1,-1,0,1,2,3,-1,-1)`, flags `0x2f0`); role 10's "indexed group" bit is set but its base byte is `-1`, so per §7.4's own documented rule it never actually resolves. **[CONFIRMED — empirical, direct header decode, `tools/scratchpad/extract_carpaint_gr.py`.]** Which numeric role the engine selects for an ordinary main-camera opaque draw is not itself re-derived here (`spec-fxo-format.md` §7.4 already leaves "what roles 0–9 mean" OPEN) — but the five passes' own declared constants make a strong CTAB-content case, offered as **HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred, not disassembly-confirmed for the role NUMBERING itself**: pass 2/3 (role 6/7) declare the full lighting/fresnel/reflection/grime set (21 and 23 constants) and are the only two with `Dual_Paraboloid_Map_Front/BackSampler` — the ordinary main-view forward-shading pass, role 7 being the same pass plus `IR_Stipple_Pattern_Offset`/`IR_Similarity_Data` (a damage/LOD cross-fade dither variant); pass 0/1 (role 4/5) declare almost nothing (2, then 5, constants) — a cheap pass, plausibly shadow-cast; pass 4 (role 1) declares only ambient/vertex-light terms (`V_ambient_render`, `V_back_ambient_render`, `lightPos`, `V_light_color`, no specular/fresnel/paraboloid samplers) — plausibly the pass used when *rendering into* the dual-paraboloid reflection map itself, since that pass cannot recursively sample the map pass 2/3 read from.

#### 20.12.2 Full CTAB dump, both stages, all 5 passes

Read directly from each blob's own public D3D9 `CTAB` sub-chunk (not the wrapper's T0–T4 tables, which are a separate, secondary naming layer per `spec-fxo-format.md` §7.3) — register set `2` = `CONST` (float), `3` = `CONST2`/sampler:

| Pass (role) | VS constants (name @ register, count) | PS constants (name @ register, count) |
|---|---|---|
| 0 (role 4) | `objTM`@32×3, `projTM`@28×4, `Bone_weights`@52×192, `IR_World2View`@48×3, `Object_instance_params_2`@36×1 | `Specular_Power`@0×1, `Damage_Normal_MapSampler`(s0) |
| 1 (role 5) | same 5 as pass 0 | + `IR_Pixel_Steps`@48×1, `IR_Stipple_Pattern_2DSampler`(s11), `IR_Stipple_Repeat_Info`@51×1 |

**[CONFIRMED — empirical, direct read of the compiled bytecode's own public `CTAB` chunk, all 10 blobs, `tools/scratchpad/extract_carpaint_gr.py`.]** `objTM`@32/`projTM`@28 are identical, register-for-register, to the totally unrelated `rfg-skybox-meteors.fxo_pc` sample's own declared `objTM`@32/`projTM`@28 (`spec-fxo-format.md` §7.3/§9.2) — real, independent, cross-shader confirmation that these two register numbers are a fixed, universal authoring convention in this engine's shader library, not a coincidence of one file.

#### 20.12.3 Tiling constants: genuinely runtime-set in THIS shader, not a compile-time literal — direct bytecode answer

Per this session's `spec-vertex-format.md` §6.6 finding that some shaders' UV-tiling constants are compile-time `def` literals, every one of this shader's 10 blobs was scanned for D3D9 `DEF`/`DEFI`/`DEFB` instructions (Microsoft's own public bytecode opcodes for a compiler-baked constant-register value — `tools/scratchpad/d3d9_tokens.py`, walking the token stream directly). **Result: `grime_tiling_u`@c3 and `grime_tiling_v`@c4 (pass 2/3's VS blobs) are NOT `DEF` targets.** Pass 2's VS blob `DEF`s exactly `c0, c1, c2, c5` (four small helper constants — bias/scale and unpack-scale literals, none named in the `CTAB`); `c3`/`c4` are untouched. The same check across all 10 blobs found **zero** collisions between any `DEF`-family target register and any `CTAB`-named constant anywhere in this file (`DEF` targets cluster at `c0–c2`/`c5` in the VS blobs and `c0`/`c3`/`c4`/`c14–c18` in the PS blobs — all unnamed compiler helper constants for normal-unpacking/Fresnel-curve math, confirmed by cross-checking every `DEF` register against the full CTAB table above). **[CONFIRMED — empirical, direct bytecode read, all 10 blobs, `tools/scratchpad/d3d9_tokens.py`.]** **For this specific shader, the answer to the task's tiling question is unambiguous: `grime_tiling_u`/`grime_tiling_v` are genuine runtime-set registers, not literals — whoever fills register `c3`/`c4` for the VS pass 2/3 bind is a real engine-side producer, not a compile-time constant.** (Which producer that is remains open — §20.12.6.)

#### 20.12.4 `objTM` — CONFIRMED end-to-end: register resolved once at renderer-library init by NAME, value read per-object, positionally

**This is the single most load-bearing finding of this pass — the concrete fix for the placeholder identity matrix.** The array-form `RecordSetVertexShaderConstantF` producer already documented at §20.3(a), `FUN_00489140` (283 bytes, one caller — `FUN_004895d0`, invoked once per visible render item), decompiles to: upload exactly 3 Vector4 registers starting at `DAT_01354700`, sourced from **the render item's own memory at `renderItem+8` through `renderItem+0x34`** (three consecutive rows — a 3×4 affine matrix, the standard shape for a world matrix with the implicit `[0,0,0,1]` fourth row omitted); then two more single-Vector4 uploads at `DAT_01354704`/`DAT_01354708`, each from a different fixed triple of render-item fields (`+0x44/+0x48/+0x4c` and `+0x38/+0x3c/+0x40`); then, gated on a flag (`DAT_03171b10`), a fourth single-Vector4 upload at `DAT_0135470c` from three GLOBAL (not per-object) floats. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace1.java`.]**

**Where the four register numbers (`DAT_01354700/04/08/0c`) themselves come from — genuinely new this pass.** All four are written exactly once, by `FUN_00489260`, whose *only* caller anywhere in the binary is `FUN_00e49250` — the renderer-library bring-up routine already named in `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.2 (called once at startup, the same bring-up phase that loads every `.fxo_pc` file). Ghidra's decompiler mis-resolves the call's own "this" argument (a known decompiler blind spot for this specific instruction sequence — verified by reading the raw disassembly directly, not trusted from the decompiled C per `WALLS.md`'s cleanroom-pseudocode caution): the real sequence at each of the four call sites is `MOV ECX, &DAT_0135470x` (the OUTPUT slot, as the `__thiscall` "this") immediately followed by a call to **`FUN_00e49990`**, passing a small registry-class handle and a literal NAME STRING as the other two arguments. The four literal strings, read directly from `.rdata`, are — in write order — **`"World_xform"`, `"Scale"`, `"Offset"`, `"Camera_velocity"`**, registered against a class handle obtained via `FUN_00e4a730("rl_vis_query")`. `FUN_00e49990` hashes the name (`FUN_00d9e740`, the project's own lower-cased CRC-32, already established elsewhere) and calls `FUN_00e49d10`, which indexes the **exact same shader-object table** `spec-render-pipeline.md` §22.4/§22.9 and `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5 already document (`materialCategory·0x1b + tierB + tierA·4`, class/variant sub-selectors at the resolved entry's own `+0x68`/`+0x6c`) and, on a hit, calls the resolved per-file shader-object's own vtable **`+0x1c`** method — **`0x0048F5F0`, `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5's already-documented "T1 lookup by hash → register" accessor.** **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, raw instruction-level read at all four call sites, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace2.java`/`3.java`/`4.java`/`5.java`.]**

**In plain terms: `DAT_01354700` is not a hardcoded literal — it is "the register that whatever shader was current at renderer-init time declares for a constant named `World_xform`," resolved once via the identical per-file `CTAB`-hash-lookup mechanism `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5 already documents, then cached in a plain global and reused for every subsequent object for the rest of the process.** This is consistent with, and explains, this pass's own §20.12.2 cross-shader observation: `ir_sr3carpaint_gr_v.fxo_pc` and the unrelated `rfg-skybox-meteors.fxo_pc` sample both place their own `objTM`-equivalent constant at register 32 — not because the engine hardcodes 32, but because **every shader in this library authors its world-matrix constant at the same register by convention**, which is exactly what lets a single resolve-once-at-init cached register number work correctly for every shader the engine ever binds. **[HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred: the exact identity of "whatever shader was current at renderer-init" (i.e., which of the 844 files `FUN_00e49d10`'s `materialCategory` argument names at that specific call) was not traced: the value is circumstantially, not directly, confirmed to be 32 — by two independent real shaders agreeing, not by reading the live resolved integer out of memory.]**

**This also sharpens §20.4's own "no name-hash used" finding, not contradicts it.** A full caller census of `FUN_00e49990` (>150 call sites across ~20 distinct subsystem-init functions — shadow maps, SSAO, RAO/ambient-occlusion, dynamic per-light setup (`ir_light_pos`/`ir_light_color`/`ir_shadow_map`), depth-of-field, motion blur, bloom/tonemap, rain, billboards, and the meteor effect sample §7.3 of `spec-fxo-format.md` already used) shows this "resolve a named constant's register once, by `CTAB` name-hash, at subsystem bring-up" pattern is genuinely pervasive in this engine. §20.4's own per-draw-call sampling correctly found no *runtime* name-hash lookups because, by the time any draw happens, every one of these registers is already a plain cached integer — the hash lookup happened once, at init, not per frame or per draw. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, full caller census, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace6.java`/`7.java`.]**

**Actionable summary for a renderer prototype:** to replace the placeholder identity `objTM`, upload the vehicle part's own real world-space rigid-part transform (already the subject of `spec-vehicle-geometry.md`'s and this document's own §16–18 producer-chain work) as 3 consecutive `Vector4` registers starting at register **32**, in the exact 3×4-row-major layout `FUN_00489140` reads from the render item (`renderItem+0x08..+0x34`), for the vertex shader stage.

#### 20.12.5 `projTM` (view-projection) and `Object_instance_params_2` — OPEN, two real leads REFUTED, one further lead found and characterized but not closed (§20.12.10)

**`projTM`@28×4 is present, at the identical register, in all 5 passes of this file and in the unrelated skybox-meteors sample** — the same universal-authoring-convention evidence as `objTM` (§20.12.4) applies equally here, so the register number itself is HIGH CONFIDENCE (28, a 4×4 matrix). **No producer was found this pass.** Two concrete candidates were checked and did not pan out:
- The full caller census of `FUN_00e49990` (§20.12.4) — exhaustive over every named constant actually resolved this way — contains **no** `View`/`Proj`/`ViewProj`/`Camera_matrix`-named registration anywhere. **This rules out the `World_xform`-style resolve-once mechanism for `projTM` specifically** — whatever sets it must be a different code path.
- `FUN_0049C530` (a small generic VSCF/PSCF dispatcher reading a per-ID table at `DAT_01350b20`) and `FUN_004A3360` (2,733 bytes, a per-frame fog/reflection-plane setup function, and the one candidate producer whose own math plausibly matches `Reflection_Plane_Height`) were both fully decompiled and **do not** perform a 4-register array upload matching `projTM`'s shape. `FUN_004A3360` was additionally found to route two of its own upload-shaped calls through `rl_renderer`'s vtable `+0x44`/`+0x48` — **both of which resolve to the identical `_purecall` trap address (`0x00ea2778`) §20.8/§21 already established** for slots `+0x10`–`+0x7c`. This REFUTES `FUN_004A3360` as a live producer for anything in this file (`Reflection_Plane_Height` included) *unless* it is reached only through the same "always-false-in-retail" gate §21 left open — flagged as one more concrete corroborating call site for that still-open question, not chased further here. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for both refutations; `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace1.java`/`2.java`.]**

**`projTM`'s semantics are now settled, independent of the producer question, via real runtime evidence (dirty-side, out of this document's normal disassembly scope — an external runtime capture; findings summarized here, the source is not for clean-side consultation — verified directly against source, 2026-09-29).** `projTM` is not a plain projection matrix despite the name — the vertex shaders compute `clipPos = projTM · worldPos` directly with no separate view multiply, so **`projTM` (c28) is a fused view-projection matrix**, and `IR_World2View` (c48, already declared by this shader per §20.12.2's CTAB dump, passes 0/1/4) **is the separate, pure view matrix** — the true projection is `inverse(IR_World2View) · projTM`. This is confirmed as a completely invariant register assignment across thousands of real shaders (projTM@c28 in 3,398 shaders, objTM@c32 in 2,357, IR_World2View@c48 in 2,203 — all vertex-shader constants, no exceptions), not specific to this one file. **A concrete, disassembly-confirmed consequence for a renderer prototype: to fill projTM correctly without needing this document's own producer question resolved, upload the real fused view-projection product (view × projection, or equivalently query it however the reimplementation's own camera already computes it) at register 28, and upload the separate pure view matrix at register 48 — do NOT upload a plain projection-only matrix at c28, which is what the constant's own name would otherwise suggest.** **[CONFIRMED — real runtime/in-engine evidence, independent of and complementary to this section's own still-OPEN producer-function question.]**

**Follow-up pass (2026-09-29, §20.12.10): a third, structurally closer candidate was found one hop up from `FUN_0049C530` — `FUN_00474000`, which uploads TWO 4×4 matrices through the exact same dispatcher — but it could not be confirmed or refuted before this pass's budget ran out; still OPEN, now with a sharper, disassembly-anchored description of exactly what is and isn't known. See §20.12.10 for the full trace; the two refutations above are unchanged and still stand.**

**`Object_instance_params_2`@36×1 (VS, passes 0–3) is also OPEN.** It is not one of the four names (`World_xform`/`Scale`/`Offset`/`Camera_velocity`) `FUN_00489140`'s own sibling mechanism resolves, and no other producer was identified. Given the name's shape (`_2`, implying a sibling `_1` this file doesn't use), it most plausibly carries a per-material or per-instance customization value (paint variant, damage state, or similar) but this is a HYPOTHESIS, not traced.

#### 20.12.6 Material-authored constants (`Specular_Power` and the rest of pass 2/3's lighting set) — the producer of the VALUES is CONFIRMED; the exact GPU-upload call site is the one open sub-item

**This directly answers the task's specular question, and corrects the placeholder's assumed default.** The shared, no-identity-field vehicle material-record parser `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.2 already documents (`FUN_00e71090`) has two array fields this pass reads in full for the first time: **array B** (`B_count` at the record's own `+0xE`, 4 bytes/entry — a **`CRC-32` name hash per entry**) paired positionally with **array C** (`C_count` at `+0xF`, 16 bytes/entry — **one `Vector4` value per entry**). This is a genuine, per-material, name-hash-keyed `{name → float4}` parameter table, authored directly in the `.ccar_pc` material record. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `FUN_00e71090`, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace10.java`; the {hash, vec4} shape was already flagged by an earlier session's own bookmark comment on this exact function, independently corroborated here by fresh disassembly.]**

**The consumer, `FUN_00e70d30`** ("the material binder," already partially traced at `spec-render-pipeline.md` §22.9 for its `shaderHash`-resolution step) does the following, in order, **once per material record at mesh-load time** (not per draw, not per frame):
1. Resolves texture bindings (array A) via `FUN_00dcecc0` — unchanged from §22.9.
2. Resolves the record's `shaderHash` to a dense shader-object index via `FUN_00e4a6d0`, then fetches the shader-object pointer via `FUN_00e49ac0` — unchanged from §22.9.
3. **For every array-B/array-C entry:** looks up the entry's name-hash on the resolved shader-object's own `CTAB`-backed accessors — vtable **`+0x1c`** (register) and **`+0x20`** (register-count) — the exact same `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5 accessors §20.12.4 traces for `World_xform`. Each entry's authored `Vector4` is then **copied from its declaration-order slot into a REGISTER-INDEXED position** in the same buffer (register `r` → byte offset `r·0x10`), so the material's own per-material buffer ends up laid out exactly like a contiguous run of pixel-shader float constants, ready for a positional upload.
4. **Specifically for the name `"Specular_Power"`** (hashed once, cached in `DAT_02bd7634`/`DAT_02bd7638` bit 1) and, separately, `"Normal_Map_Height"` (`DAT_02bd7630`/bit 2): after the remap, each name's own register slot is read and validated. ~~if it is not a valid positive value (a `<= ~0` / minimum-threshold check against two named globals), it is overwritten with the fixed default `DAT_0127e354` = 0.1... A sibling check for `"Normal_Map_Height"` clamps that value into `[0, DAT_0111522c=128.0]`~~ **CORRECTED 2026-09-29 (§20.12.11) — this original description was wrong about which global belongs to which check, sourced from an ambiguous decompiler comma-operator expression rather than the raw instructions.** The real, raw-disassembly-confirmed semantics (both re-derived directly from x86 instructions, not decompiler pseudocode, this time): **`Specular_Power`** is a *three-way* clamp — value `<= 0.1` (compared in double precision against `DAT_012a2d38`, an 8-byte `double` equal to `0.10000000149011612` = `double(0.1f)`, i.e. the SAME threshold as the default, not a separate near-zero epsilon) is forced up to the fixed default `DAT_0127e354` = **0.1** (float); value `>= 128.0` (`DAT_0111522c`) is clamped DOWN to **128.0**; a value strictly between `0.1` and `128.0` passes through completely unmodified. **`Normal_Map_Height`** is clamped into **`[0.0, 1.0]`** — lower bound is a literal `0.0` (not a named global), upper bound is `DAT_01117a4c` = **1.0** (not `DAT_0111522c`/128.0, which belongs to `Specular_Power`'s upper clamp instead, per above) — values `<=0` become `0.0`, values `>=1.0` become `1.0`, values in between pass through unmodified. **[CONFIRMED — raw disassembly, ground truth over the decompiler, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace12.java`; all four globals' true sizes/values independently re-dumped as both float32 and float64 with Ghidra's own declared data type checked (`DAT_012a2d38` is genuinely `undefined8`/8 bytes, confirming the double-cast was real and the earlier 4-byte dump of it was itself misleading even though the printed float32 bit-pattern was technically "a value"; `DAT_0127e354`/`DAT_0111522c`/`DAT_01117a4c` are genuinely 4-byte floats, unchanged).]**
5. Finally, the record's own `+0xE` byte is overwritten with the shader-object's own **`+0x24`** accessor result (`spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5's "register span" — max register + span over the shader's T1 table) and bound-checked against `+0xF` (array C's real capacity) — i.e., after this one-time pass, the per-material buffer is sized and indexed exactly to the BOUND SHADER's own register layout, not the material author's original entry count.

**What this confirms for the task's specular question:** `Specular_Power`, and by the identical positional-remap mechanism (but, per the direct 2026-09-29 follow-up in §20.12.11, NOT by the same per-name validation/default mechanism — that part is now confirmed to be exhaustively limited to just these two names) `Specular_Color`, `Specular_Alpha`, `Tint_color`, `Base_Paint_Color`, `grime_amount`, `grime_override`, `Fresnel_Color`, `Fresnel_Min_Cos_Angle`, `Fresnel_Inverse_Cos_Angle_Range`, `Reflection_Cos_Min_Angles`, `Reflection_Inv_Range_Cos_Angles`, `Ambient_Reflection_Amount`, `Fog_color`, and `Target_dimensions` (every plain named float/Vector4 constant pass 2/3 declares) are **real, per-material, author-settable values when the `.ccar_pc` material record's own array B/C authors them by name** — not flat engine defaults — falling back to a small number of disassembly-confirmed fixed defaults/clamps (`Specular_Power`: `0.1` floor, `128.0` ceiling; `Normal_Map_Height`: `[0,1]` clamp) only for these two specific names when the authored value is missing or invalid. **For every one of the other 14 names, see §20.12.11 (direct follow-up, 2026-09-29): exhaustively CONFIRMED there is no equivalent default/clamp anywhere in this function or its callees — an unauthored register for any of those 14 sits at literal zero, permanently. [CONFIRMED for `Specular_Power`/`Normal_Map_Height` specifically, disassembly; CONFIRMED NEGATIVE for the remaining 14, §20.12.11.]**

**The one remaining open sub-item:** neither `FUN_00e3f740` (the `rl_static_material` vtable-`+0x10` dispatch target reached from the closed vehicle draw loop, §22.1 item 2) nor `FUN_00e5e530` (the technique-select body, §22.4/§22.5) contains a visible call to any `SetPixelShaderConstantF`/`SetVertexShaderConstantF` producer reading from this per-material register-indexed buffer — both were fully decompiled this pass and this pass's predecessor, and neither shows the upload. **The exact function that issues the GPU upload for this buffer (presumably a simple positional array-form `SetPixelShaderConstantF(0, buffer, registerSpan)` call somewhere in the same bind path) was not located.** **[OPEN — honest gap, not chased further within this pass's budget; the VALUE-preparation side is fully closed, only the final upload call site is missing.]**

**Follow-up pass (2026-09-29, §20.12.10): STILL OPEN after an exhaustive check of every plausible nearby call site** — `rl_static_material`'s entire vtable (all 27 slots), the fxo shader-object's own `+0x14` role-select method, and seven further confirmed real callers of the array-form PSCF producer were all individually decompiled this pass; none performs the upload. Two new, narrower leads are recorded for a future pass rather than chased to conclusion here. See §20.12.10.

#### 20.12.7 Everything else declared — OPEN, not investigated this pass

`Bone_weights`@52×192 (all 5 passes — plausibly the skin/rigid-part bone-palette upload already the subject of `spec-vehicle-geometry.md`'s rigid-part work, but not confirmed as the same producer); `IR_World2View`@48×3 (passes 0/1/4); `Fog_dist`@42×1, `eyePos`@41×1 (passes 2/3/4 — plausibly per-frame camera globals); the `IR_`-prefixed cluster (`IR_Pixel_Steps`, `IR_Stipple_Pattern_2DSampler`, `IR_Stipple_Repeat_Info`, `IR_Similarity_Data`, `IR_Stipple_Pattern_Offset`, `IR_GBuffer_DSF_DataSampler`, `IR_LBufferSampler`) — the shared `IR_` prefix is suggestive of one dedicated subsystem (the same naming family as §20.12.4's caller census turned up for dynamic lights, `ir_light_pos`/`ir_light_color`/`ir_shadow_map`/`ir_ambient_occlusion`/`projection_texture`, registered via the identical `FUN_00e49990` mechanism from `FUN_00e6ce70` — a real, HIGH-CONFIDENCE but unconfirmed lead for a future pass, since this pass's caller census did not check whether `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own registrations include any of THIS shader's exact `IR_`-prefixed names); pass 4's `V_ambient_render`/`V_back_ambient_render`/`lightPos`/`V_light_color`/`Self_Illumination` (consistent with, but not confirmed as, the reflection-probe-render pass hypothesis of §20.12.1). All samplers (`Damage_Normal_MapSampler`, `grime_mapSampler`, `Dual_Paraboloid_Map_Front/BackSampler`, `IR_Stipple_Pattern_2DSampler`) bind positionally per the already-documented §22.5 mechanism; which specific texture handle lands in each was not traced per-name this pass.

#### 20.12.8 Evidence anchors

CTAB extraction: `tools/scratchpad/extract_carpaint_gr.py`, `tools/scratchpad/d3d9_tokens.py`, real file `shaders.vpp_pc` entry 495 (`ir_sr3carpaint_gr_v.fxo_pc`, 19,388 bytes, mode-(a) physical offset via `tools/harnesses/vpp_modea.py`). `objTM` chain: `FUN_00489140` (producer, one caller `FUN_004895d0`), `FUN_00489260` (register-resolution, one caller `FUN_00e49250`), `FUN_00e49990`/`FUN_00e49d10`/`FUN_00e49dd0`/`FUN_00e49cb0` (name-hash-to-register primitive, full call census `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace6.java`/`7.java`), strings `"World_xform"`/`"Scale"`/`"Offset"`/`"Camera_velocity"` at `0x012a0e10`/`0x1c`/`0x24`/`0x2c`, globals `DAT_01354700`/`04`/`08`/`0c`. Refuted candidates: `FUN_0049C530`, `FUN_004A3360` (vtable `+0x44`/`+0x48` both = `_purecall` trap `0x00ea2778`), trivial wrappers `0x0047727F`/`0x004772A0`/`0x004772DF`/`0x00477300` (confirmed genuinely zero callers anywhere in the binary once Function boundaries were forced, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace9.java`). Material-authored constants: `FUN_00e71090` (array B/C layout), `FUN_00e70d30` (remap + `Specular_Power`/`Normal_Map_Height` validation, cached hashes `DAT_02bd7634`/`DAT_02bd7630`, default `DAT_0127e354=0.1`, bound `DAT_0111522c=128.0`), `FUN_00e49ac0` (shader-object accessor), `FUN_00e3f740`/`FUN_00e5e530` (checked, no upload call found). Scripts: `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace1.java` through `CarPaintTrace11.java`.
