| Instance params (`Object_instance_params_2`) | VS c36×1 | **OPEN** | Declared, no producer found; not one of the sibling `World_xform`-mechanism's four resolved names |
| Lighting (pass 2/3's Fresnel/Reflection/Ambient set) | PS c6–c11 etc. | **HIGH CONFIDENCE** | Same per-material array-B/C mechanism as `Specular_Power`; values are real per-material authored data; GPU upload call site not located (all 27 `rl_static_material` vtable slots + 8 further candidates checked and exhausted, §20.12.10). **Per-name validation/default (§20.12.11): CONFIRMED that none of these 14 names has one — only `Specular_Power`/`Normal_Map_Height` do; an unauthored register is literal zero, permanently, not a placeholder value** |
| Specular (`Specular_Power`) | PS c0×1 | **CONFIRMED** | Real per-material authored value when present; **fixed validation, corrected §20.12.11**: floors at `0.1` for any value `<=0.1`, ceilings at `128.0` for any value `>=128.0`, passes through unmodified in between — not the placeholder's `8.0`, and not the originally-published "≈0 check + separate [0,128] clamp on Normal_Map_Height" description |
| Tiling (`grime_tiling_u`/`_v`) | VS c3/c4×1 | **CONFIRMED (bytecode)** | NOT a compile-time `DEF` literal in this shader — genuinely runtime-set; same open upload-producer gap as the lighting set above |

Both remaining OPEN items above (`projTM` and the material-buffer GPU-upload call site) were revisited in a direct follow-up pass, 2026-09-29 — see §20.12.10. Both stay OPEN; the follow-up narrows each honestly rather than closing them. A separate direct follow-up the same day (§20.12.11) closes the "is there a defaults table for the other 14 names" question exhaustively (no), and corrects the exact `Specular_Power`/`Normal_Map_Height` semantics from a decompiler-pseudocode mis-read to raw-disassembly ground truth.

#### 20.12.10 Direct Follow-Up Pass (2026-09-29): `projTM` and the material-buffer upload revisited — both narrowed, neither closed

**Scope.** Requested as a direct follow-up on §20.12.5's and §20.12.6's two OPEN items specifically (not a fresh cold start). Method: same disposable Ghidra project `tools/gp_render8`, PowerShell-only headless invocation, `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM1.java` through `ProjTM7.java`. Budget: smaller than Pass 6, as instructed — seven short scripts, no new CTAB/data-file extraction.

**`projTM` — the two existing refutations stand; a third, structurally closer candidate was found but could not be confirmed or refuted.**

`FUN_0049C530`'s own two real callers (previously only approximately characterized as "a small generic VSCF/PSCF dispatcher") were pinned down by direct xref: **`FUN_00474000`** (called twice from a one-line thunk `FUN_00474ad4`) and **`FUN_00474990`** (reached only via a single DATA reference at `0x0129fd50`, i.e. a function-pointer slot, not a direct call — so itself only reachable through an unresolved indirect dispatch). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM3.java`.]**

`FUN_00474000` (361 bytes) decompiles to: read a 4×4 matrix from `unaff_ESI+0x3e0` (an implicit, register-carried "this"-like pointer, not a stack argument), transpose it in place via a small helper (`FUN_00434e90`), and upload it through `FUN_0049C530` with a dispatch ID read from **the same object's own `+0x760` field** — the identical field offset already established elsewhere in this document (§18.1, §22.5) as "the current render object's own active-technique/flush-context field." It then does the **exact same thing a second time**, immediately afterward, for a **second** 4×4 matrix read from `*(TLS_render_context + 0x674) + 0x320` (the per-thread render-context pointer already used throughout §20.3(c)/§22, offset `+0x320` this time rather than the `+0x760` already documented) — transposed the same way, uploaded through the same dispatcher with the same `+0x760` ID. **Two 4×4-matrix uploads, back to back, through the generic per-slot dispatcher, is a structurally strong match for an object/view (or view/projection) matrix PAIR** — closer in shape to `projTM` than either of the two previously-refuted candidates (`FUN_0049C530` as a whole is data-driven and generic, not itself an upload site; `FUN_004A3360` uploads single-count entries at data-driven, not matrix-shaped, registers). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM2.java`.]**

**This candidate could not be closed, for two independent reasons, both themselves confirmed negatives:**
1. **The dispatch table `FUN_0049C530` indexes (`DAT_01350b20`, stride `0x10`: flag/mode/register/count per slot) has no findable writer anywhere in the binary.** Two independent searches — Ghidra's own formed-xref scan of the literal address, and a raw byte-pattern scan of the little-endian dword `0x01350b20` across every initialized memory block — both found **only the two already-known reader instructions** (inside `FUN_0049C530` and its PS-sibling `FUN_0049C6A0`) and **zero writer instructions anywhere**. This means the table's real per-slot register numbers (and specifically whether the slot `FUN_00474000` targets ever resolves to register 28) could not be determined statically; if the table is instead populated through a runtime-computed pointer this project's tooling cannot resolve without a live trace, that would explain the negative. **[CONFIRMED — empirical, exhaustive over initialized memory, `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM3.java`.]**
2. **`FUN_00474000` itself has no findable caller.** Its only call site, the one-line thunk `FUN_00474ad4`, has zero further callers found by direct xref, and the sibling function `FUN_00474990` (which shares the same `+0x760`-keyed dispatch pattern and is reached only via the single DATA reference at `0x0129fd50`) could not be traced to a resolvable vtable or calling class within this pass's budget — the bytes immediately following `0x0129fd50` are plain ASCII text (`"RL: init command"`, `"rl_d3d_vertex_format_library"`), which is likely just adjacent unrelated `.rdata` content rather than further vtable slots, but this was not conclusively resolved. **[CONFIRMED for the caller-census negative; the `0x0129fd50` slot's own owning class is OPEN, `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM7.java`.]**

**Net effect: `projTM`'s producer stays OPEN, exactly as §20.12.5 left it, with one additional named candidate (`FUN_00474000`) on record — structurally the best match found across two passes, but explicitly NOT claimed as confirmed**, per this project's standing discipline against asserting a plausible-sounding but untraced mechanism. `FUN_0049C530`'s and `FUN_004A3360`'s original refutations (§20.12.5) are unchanged.

**Material-buffer GPU upload — exhaustively checked against every plausible nearby call site; still not found.**

`FUN_00e70d30` was re-examined specifically for where the finished register-indexed buffer is stored: it lives at **the material record's own `+0x20` field** (a heap pointer, sized `registerSpan·0x10` bytes), built by copying the record's original packed values out through a **global scratch buffer** (`DAT_02bd7640`) before the register-indexed remap — a load-time-only staging area, not itself GPU-facing. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM2.java`.]**

Every plausible reader of this `record+0x20` buffer was then checked:
- **`rl_static_material`'s entire vtable** (`0x0125b4b4`, all 27 slots from `+0x00` to `+0x68` — the previous pass had only characterized six of these) was dumped and every previously-uncharacterized slot's function was force-disassembled (several had no Function boundary at all in this disposable project until this pass created one) and decompiled: `+0x38` (`FUN_00e5e950`, a version/equality comparator between two material instances), `+0x3c` (`FUN_00e5eb30`, a **Clone** method — copies `record+0x20`'s buffer into a newly-cloned instance's own buffer, not a GPU upload), `+0x40`/`+0x44` (trivial one-line forwarders to `FUN_00e49d70`/`FUN_00e49cb0`, the same T1-accessor family §20.12.4/§22.4 already use), `+0x50` (`FUN_00e5ec50`, a single-named-constant **getter** — given a name hash, resolves its register/count via `FUN_00e49d10`/`FUN_00e49d70` and copies that many `Vector4`s OUT of `record+0x20` into a caller-supplied buffer; the closest thing to a "reader" found, but a direct reference scan found **no caller anywhere in the binary**, only the vtable slot itself — either genuinely dead code or reachable only through a fully virtual dispatch this pass's static tools cannot resolve), plus several trivial single-field accessors (`+0x0`, `+0x8`, `+0xc`, `+0x1c`, `+0x20`, `+0x24`–`+0x2c`, `+0x54`, `+0x5c`, `+0x64`) and two slots shared with unrelated classes as inherited base-class defaults (`+0x18`=`GetPoolName`, `+0x30`/`+0x60`=`SetMonitoring`). **None of the 27 slots performs a bulk array-form PSCF upload of this buffer.** **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, all 27 slots, `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM1.java`/`ProjTM5.java`/`ProjTM6.java`.]**
- **The fxo shader-object's own `+0x14` role-select method (`0x0048F7C0`)** — previously cross-referenced from `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5 but never itself decompiled in this document — was force-disassembled and decompiled for the first time: it dispatches into a **different object**, obtained via the current render-context's own first field, through **that** object's vtable `+0x60` (identity not resolved this pass), and separately calls a genuinely new, previously-uncatalogued command-buffer producer, **`FUN_00475860`** (writes opcode `0x15`, a fixed 8-byte single-dword-argument record, in the exact reserve/write/advance/notify idiom already documented for the rest of the `Record*` family, §18.2) — worth naming as a new anchor, but neither call is the material-buffer upload. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM4.java`/`ProjTM6.java`.]**
- **Seven further confirmed real callers of the array-form PSCF producer `FUN_00475D80`**, already listed in the existing full census (§20.9) but not individually decompiled before this pass — `FUN_004847C0`, `FUN_00484910`, `FUN_00482230`, `FUN_00484E60`, `FUN_004935B0` (all post-process/blur/DoF-shaped, register numbers sourced from a `param_1+0x19c`-style parameter struct, the same general shape as the already-closed §20.11 sentinel case, not from `record+0x20`) — were decompiled and none reads from the material record's `+0x20` field. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM3.java`.]**

**Net effect: the material-buffer GPU-upload call site stays OPEN, exactly as §20.12.6 left it.** Two narrower, concrete leads are recorded for a future pass rather than pursued further here: (a) the render-context's own first-field object's vtable `+0x60` (reached from the shader-object's `+0x14` method) — its class was not identified; (b) `FUN_00e5ec50`'s apparent zero-caller status, worth either accepting as dead code or re-checking with a devirtualization-capable approach. The VALUE-preparation side (§20.12.6 points 1–5) is unaffected and remains fully closed.

**Evidence anchors:** `tools/scratchpad/ProjTM1.java` through `ProjTM7.java` and their `_out.txt` logs (same directory). New addresses/globals this pass: `FUN_00474000`, `FUN_00474990`, `FUN_00474ad4` (thunk), dispatch table `DAT_01350b20` (writer search: exhaustive negative), vtable-slot data reference `0x0129fd50`; `rl_static_material` full vtable dump `0x0125b4b4`+0x00..+0x68 (27 slots); `FUN_00e5e950`, `FUN_00e5eb30` (Clone), `FUN_00e5ec50` (named-constant getter, zero callers found); fxo shader-object `+0x14` method `0x0048F7C0` (newly force-disassembled), its vtable-`+0x60` sub-dispatch (class unresolved), and the newly-identified opcode-`0x15` producer `FUN_00475860`.

#### 20.12.11 Direct Follow-Up Pass (2026-09-29): peer-team join request — exhaustive check of §20.12.6's "14 remaining named constants" against `FUN_00e70d30`'s own validation mechanism

**Scope.** (Note: the join request itself said "~17"; the CTAB dump already published at §20.12.2 shows the real count of plain named float/Vector4 constants outside `Specular_Power`/samplers/the separately-tracked `IR_Pixel_Steps` is exactly **14** — used precisely throughout this subsection rather than repeating the approximate figure.) A peer team's render was blown out because 14 of pass 2/3's named constants (`Specular_Color`, `Base_Paint_Color`, `grime_amount`, `Fresnel_Color`, `Fresnel_Min_Cos_Angle`, `Fresnel_Inverse_Cos_Angle_Range`, `Reflection_Cos_Min_Angles`, `Reflection_Inv_Range_Cos_Angles`, `Ambient_Reflection_Amount`, `Specular_Alpha`, `grime_override`, `Tint_color`, `Fog_color`, `Target_dimensions`) have no per-material authored value for the vehicle they're testing. Direct question: is there a single defaults table, or a longer individual-name-check list inside `FUN_00e70d30` the original §20.12.6 pass didn't fully enumerate, covering these the way `Specular_Power`/`Normal_Map_Height` are covered? Method: re-opened the already-decompiled `FUN_00e70d30` (re-read, not cold start) with three new scripts — `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace12.java` (raw disassembly + exhaustive call-target census of `FUN_00e70d30` itself, plus a whole-program literal-string scan for all 14 names), `CarPaintTrace13.java` (decompiled every OTHER function the string scan turned up as a real code xref), `CarPaintTrace14.java` (call-target census of the two remaining direct callees not yet checked, closing the "or anything it calls" part of the question). Same disposable Ghidra project `tools/gp_render8`.

**Answer to question 1 — exhaustive, ground-truth NO, not an under-search.** A full raw-instruction disassembly of `FUN_00e70d30` (856 bytes, the whole function, `CarPaintTrace12.java` Part 2) plus an exhaustive census of every `CALL` instruction inside it (Part 3) found **exactly two** calls to the name-hash function `FUN_00d9e740` anywhere in the function's own body — at `0x00e70f99` (string literal `"Specular_Power"`, address `0x0115ec10`) and `0x00e7101a` (`"Normal_Map_Height"`, `0x0125c31c`) — and no third. Every other call target inside the function was enumerated and identified: `__stricmp` ×3 (texture-name special-case handling, array A), `FUN_00e92b10` (texture-name string copy), `FUN_00dcecc0` (texture resolve), `FUN_00e4a6d0` (shaderHash→dense-index resolve), `FUN_00e49ac0` (shader-object accessor), `_memcpy` ×3 / `_memset` ×1, and four indirect vtable calls already resolved elsewhere in this document to the known CTAB accessors (`+0x1c` register lookup, `+0x20` count lookup, `+0x24` register-span, `+0x40` array-A resolve). **None of these is a third/fourth/etc. name-hash check.** The two remaining direct callees not already fully decompiled in the original pass (`FUN_00e4a6d0`, `FUN_00e92b10`) were call-target-censused fresh this pass (`CarPaintTrace14.java`): `FUN_00e4a6d0` calls only `FUN_00e4a480` (the shader-object table indexer, no name hash); `FUN_00e92b10` calls only `__stricmp` ×2 and `FUN_00da8790` (a string helper). Neither calls `FUN_00d9e740`. Combined with the original pass's own full decompiles of `FUN_00dcecc0`/`FUN_00e49ac0` (§20.12's own `CarPaintTrace10.java`/`11.java`, no such call in either), **every direct callee of `FUN_00e70d30` is now accounted for, and the validation mechanism is confirmed to check exactly two names, full stop.** **[CONFIRMED — exhaustive, raw disassembly + call-target census, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace12.java`/`14.java`.]**

**Answer to question 2 — real defaults, corrected.** See the corrected point 4 above (§20.12.6): `Specular_Power` floors at `0.1` for any value `<=0.1`, ceilings at `128.0` for any value `>=128.0`, passes through unmodified in between; `Normal_Map_Height` clamps to `[0.0, 1.0]`. Both re-derived from raw x86 instructions (not decompiler pseudocode) this pass, correcting a real mis-attribution in the original write-up (it had assigned the `128.0` bound to `Normal_Map_Height` and described `Specular_Power`'s own threshold as a vague "≈0" check, when the raw comparison is against a `double`-typed `0.1` — the SAME value as the eventual default, not a separate epsilon).

**Answer to question 3 — a genuinely separate defaults source: exhaustively checked, does not exist for these 14 names; but 8 of the 14 DO have real, unrelated, non-default consumers elsewhere worth knowing about so the peer team doesn't mistake them for one.** A literal ASCII string scan of every initialized block in the executable's own memory (not just formed Ghidra strings — a raw byte-pattern scan, same discipline as `WALLS.md`'s own `DAT_01350b20` writer-search method, with `"Specular_Power"`/`"Normal_Map_Height"` run as a methodology control and correctly finding `FUN_00e70d30` among their xrefs) found:
- **6 of the 14 names have ZERO occurrences anywhere in the executable's initialized memory, full stop:** `grime_amount`, `Ambient_Reflection_Amount`, `grime_override`, `Tint_color`, `Fog_color`, `Target_dimensions`. There is no code path anywhere in this binary that could even hash-check these by name — not in `FUN_00e70d30`, not anywhere else. **[CONFIRMED NEGATIVE — exhaustive whole-binary string scan, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace12.java` Part 4.]**
- **8 of the 14 DO appear as real strings with real code xrefs, but every single one resolves to a subsystem that is NOT a vehicle-body-material load-time default:**
  - `Specular_Color`, `Specular_Alpha` — referenced from `FUN_00ba1600` (district/weather-lighting `.xtbl` reader, e.g. the `"Water"` sub-block's own `Specular_Color`/`Specular_Alpha`/`Specular_Power` fields — `spec-tables-environment.md` territory, unrelated shader), `FUN_009fa370` (a distance-based Fresnel/specular runtime setter for a different object class, reads cached hashes into `FUN_007539f0`), and `FUN_00833690` (`player_creation_skin_colors.xtbl` — character skin-tone customization, a different shader entirely).
  - `Base_Paint_Color` — referenced from `FUN_008188b0`/`FUN_00818ef0`, a nearest-color-match utility for vehicle paint-swatch customization (matches a requested RGB to the closest available swatch color; reads `Base_Paint_Color`/`Glass_Color`/`Diffuse_Color` hashes once, cached).
  - `Fresnel_Color` — one data-only `.rdata` cross-reference (`0x01300b38`), not reached from any decompiled code path found this pass.
  - `Fresnel_Min_Cos_Angle`, `Fresnel_Inverse_Cos_Angle_Range`, `Reflection_Cos_Min_Angles`, `Reflection_Inv_Range_Cos_Angles` — all four belong to the **vehicle paint-shop color-customization pipeline**, not vehicle mesh-load: `FUN_00a94c60` is a short-alias-name resolver (`vehicle_cust_color_pool.xtbl` authors can write `"Fresnel_Start"`/`"Fresnel_Inverse_Range"` as a convenience shorthand; this function maps those two aliases to the real long-form constant-name hash — it is a NAME-remapping utility, not a value-default); `FUN_00a94b70`/`FUN_00a94d10` read `vehicle_cust_color_pool.xtbl`, compute a **derived** reciprocal-range value from the authored `Reflection_Cos_Min_Angles`/`Reflection_Inv_Range_Cos_Angles` pair (with its own divide-by-near-zero guard, `DAT_011811d0`) for the shader's convenience, and build the player-selectable paint-color-swap table applied when a player changes their vehicle's paint at a customization shop — an entirely separate runtime SETTER triggered by player choice, not a mesh-load-time fallback for an unauthored vehicle-body material.
  - Separately (not one of the 14, but found along the way and worth recording): `FUN_00e6c280` is a **direct re-caller of `FUN_00e70d30` itself** — it builds a hardcoded, synthetic 2-entry material record (`"Diffuse_Color"` + `"Specular_Power"`, bound to a literal `"shadowcaster_material"` shader) as a one-time engine-internal bootstrap, and feeds it through the exact same `FUN_00e70d30` validation path documented above. This is additional real-world corroboration that `FUN_00e70d30`'s two-name validation is deliberate and general-purpose (it also protects this synthetic shadow-caster material's own `Specular_Power`), not a vehicle-paint-specific special case — but it is not a third data point for the other 14 names either.
- **Net: no genuinely separate defaults source exists anywhere in the binary for any of the 14.** Every real reference found is either a different subsystem entirely (weather lighting, character skin, paint-shop color pools) or a direct call BACK into the same two-name mechanism already fully characterized. **[CONFIRMED — exhaustive string scan + decompile of every real code xref found, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace12.java`/`13.java`.]**

**What this means concretely for an unauthored vehicle-body material, traced directly from `FUN_00e70d30`'s own memory operations (§20.12.6 point 3, re-confirmed this pass at the raw-disassembly level, `0x00e70eea`-`0x00e70f11`):** the per-material register-indexed buffer (`record+0x20`) is fully **zero-filled by `_memset`** (sized to the shader's real register span, `C_count·0x10` bytes) BEFORE the authored array-B/C entries are remapped into it; only registers whose name-hash matches something the material record's own array B actually authored ever get overwritten with a real value. For any of the 14 names above, if the vehicle's `.ccar_pc` material record doesn't author it, **the value is not "garbage" and not an engine default — it is a clean, deterministic, literal binary zero for every float component, and nothing later in this load path (or anywhere else in the binary, per the exhaustive check above) ever touches it again.** This is the honest, disassembly-backed answer to "is it genuinely undefined if unauthored" — yes, exactly and only for these 14, in the specific sense of "zero, not garbage, and never revisited." (`Specular_Power`/`Normal_Map_Height` are the sole exceptions, because their own post-remap checks run unconditionally on whatever is sitting in their slot, authored or not — which is exactly why an unauthored `Specular_Power` ends up at `0.1` rather than `0.0`.)

**Practical flag for the peer team's render bug (inference, not shader-bytecode-traced — offered because it's directly actionable, not claimed as confirmed):** several of the zero-defaulted 14 are the kind of constant that would visibly "blow out" a render if the shader uses them multiplicatively — a zero `Tint_color`/`Base_Paint_Color`/`Specular_Color` would multiply the corresponding term to black/zero rather than leave it neutral, unlike an additive term (e.g. `Fog_color` at zero would just mean "no fog tint," which is comparatively benign). Whether any given one of these is actually multiplicative in `ir_sr3carpaint_gr_v.fxo_pc`'s own pixel-shader bytecode was **not traced this pass** (that would require reading the PS bytecode math itself, out of this pass's scope) — flagged as a plausible root-cause direction for the peer team to check against their own shader math, not asserted as the actual cause.

**Evidence anchors:** `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace12.java`/`13.java`/`14.java` and their `_out.txt` logs. New addresses/data this pass: raw disassembly of `FUN_00e70d30` confirming `DAT_012a2d38` is Ghidra-declared `undefined8` (8 bytes, real value `0.10000000149011612`) not a 4-byte float; `FUN_00a94c60` (Fresnel short-alias resolver), `FUN_00a94b70`/`FUN_00a94d10` (`vehicle_cust_color_pool.xtbl` reader + derived reciprocal-range computation, guard constant `DAT_011811d0`), `FUN_00ba1600` (district/weather lighting `.xtbl` reader), `FUN_009fa370` (distance-based Fresnel/specular runtime setter, unrelated object class), `FUN_00833690` (`player_creation_skin_colors.xtbl`), `FUN_008188b0`/`FUN_00818ef0` (paint-swatch nearest-color match), `FUN_00e6c280` (hardcoded synthetic shadowcaster-material bootstrap, direct re-caller of `FUN_00e70d30`).

#### 20.12.12 `Tint_color`'s real-world value, and a resolved red herring (`Decal_Color`) — 2026-09-29

**Real runtime evidence (dirty-side — an external runtime capture; findings summarized here, the source is not for clean-side consultation — verified directly against source) measures `Tint_color` (PS c37, the same register the car-paint shader's own CTAB declares, §20.12.2) very differently by material family, with an explicit warning against generalizing one to the other: character materials measure it at `(5.0, 5.0, 5.0)` — a genuine exposure factor — but vehicle materials measure it at `0.009`–`0.022`, "where it IS a real value," not an exposure term. This directly narrows what to look for on the vehicle side: something small, plausibly a real per-material tint multiplier, not the character family's global exposure constant.

**An empirical, exhaustive, population-wide check for a literal `Tint_color`-named entry in the vehicle material's own array-B/C `{name-hash, Vector4}` table (§20.12.6's mechanism) found zero hits — genuinely absent, not merely unauthored on one test vehicle: 0/17,026 real material records across all 393 real `.ccar_pc` files.** **[CONFIRMED — empirical, exhaustive, real data, 2026-09-29.]**

**A striking candidate was found and then correctly refuted, worth recording as a cleanroom process lesson.** Among the 32 distinct real array-B name-hashes actually authored across the whole vehicle population, one unidentified hash (`0xbfd6148d`, 1,088 records) carries a value uniform across all three color channels — `(0.021219, 0.021219, 0.021219, 1.0)` — landing precisely inside the real vehicle `Tint_color` range above. **This was a coincidence, not a match: a peer team independently brute-forced the hash (validated first against two already-known name/hash pairs) and found `0xbfd6148d` = this project's own lowercased-CRC-32 of `"decal_color"`, independently re-verified directly against this project's own hash implementation.** `Decal_Color` is a real, plausible, uniform-scalar-shaped material property (a decal-layer intensity/tint) — entirely unrelated to `Tint_color`. **Do not re-cite `0xbfd6148d` as `Tint_color`; it is `Decal_Color`.** **[CONFIRMED — independently re-derived by two parties against the same hash algorithm.]**

**Net position: `Tint_color` is authored in zero real vehicle material records, has no static engine default (§20.12.11), yet is measured at runtime as a real, small, vehicle-specific value.** This is the same shape of question already resolved for `objTM`/`projTM` (§20.12.4/§20.12.5) — a register-bound value whose real producer is a genuine engine code path, not a per-material author-time value at all. **One near-miss checked and refuted this pass:** the existing exhaustive caller census of the name-hash-at-init mechanism (`FUN_00e49990`, §20.12.4, already run for `objTM`'s own trace) does register a constant literally named `"orbital_tint"` — a real, different name (plausibly an environment/skybox tint, not vehicle paint) — but no `"Tint_color"`/`"tint_color"` registration appears anywhere in that same >150-site census. **The real producer of vehicle `Tint_color` remains OPEN — a genuinely new search, not answered by re-checking the `objTM`-style mechanism's existing results.**

#### 20.12.13 Direct Follow-Up Pass (2026-09-29): register-37 producer search — two clean exhaustive negatives, one genuinely new (but unclosed) candidate mechanism

**Scope.** Direct follow-up specifically hunting for the real producer of PS c37 (`Tint_color`) or a shared "current lighting-parameter block" register filled by convention, per the same general shape already solved for `objTM` (§20.12.4). Method: re-read the existing `FUN_00e49990` census in full rather than re-deriving it (`tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace6_out.txt`/`7_out.txt`); one new exhaustive whole-binary literal-register scan (`tools/scratchpad/TintTrace5.java`); one new disassembly trace of a previously-uncharacterized code path inside an already-on-record function (`tools/scratchpad/TintTrace1.java` through `4.java`). Same disposable Ghidra project `tools/gp_render8`.

**Check 1 — re-read the FULL `FUN_00e49990` census (not just grepped for "tint"), tried every plausible alias the task named, against the SAME already-enumerated name list.** All ~19 distinct caller functions and every name they register were read in full (not just the ones already excerpted in §20.12.4/§20.12.12): shadow-map setup (`Shadow_map_matrix`, `Shadow_map_resolution`, `Shadow_fade_params`, `Shadow_percent_params`, `Near_clip_params`, `Near_far_depth_params`, `Shadow_map`, `Depth_map`); orbital/environment (`orbital_map`, `orbital_tint` — already flagged, still not a match); depth-of-field (`Near_clip_params`, `Focal_params`, `Coc_range_params`, `Depth_sampling_offsets`, `Override_blur_percent`, `Depth_map_scale`); motion blur ×2 (`Distortion_scale`, `Blur_scale`, `Signal_noise`, `Curr_to_prev`, `Blur_clamp`, `Radial_blur_radius`, `Radial_blur_position`); SSAO (`ssao_fade_parameter`, `ssao_inv_proj`, `ssao_curr_to_prev_xform`, `ssao_interleave_offsets`, `ssao_projection_scales`, `ssao_blue_noise_offsets`, `ssao_sample_radius_reference`, `ssao_temporal_falloff`, `ssao_prev_tex`, `ssao_uv_scale_offset`, `ssao_sampler`, `blur_falloff`, `blur_sharpness`); dynamic per-light (`ir_light_pos`, `ir_light_color`, `ir_light_back_color`, `ir_shadow_map`, `ir_ambient_occlusion`, `projection_texture`, `ir_light_debug_flags`); RAO/ambient-occlusion (`rao_inv_proj`, `rao_view2box`, `rao_box_min`, `rao_box_max`, `rao_offset_radii`, `rao_strength`, `rao_ellipsoid2view`, `rao_view2ellipsoid`, `rao_occluder_sphere`); **the tonemap/bloom/exposure HDR subsystem the task's own step 2 predicted might exist — it does** (`Sampling_offsets`, `Adapt_time`, `Luminance_conversion`, `Desired_brightness`, `Exposure_min`, `Exposure_max`, `Bloom_amount`, `Bloom_curve_values`, `Debug_bloom_buffer`, `Debug_lummap`, `Debug_avg_lum`, `Lut_scale_offset`, `base_sampler`, `Lut_sampler`, `Lut_sampler_2d`, `lum_adapt_sampler`, `bloom_stage_0..4_sampler`, `Bloom_add_texture_0/1_sampler`, `Light_shafts_brightpass_sampler`, `Light_shafts_depth_sampler`); rain (`Speed`, `Rain_params`, `Size_params`, `Prev_cam_position`, `Start_position`, `Current_time`, `Diffuse_Map`, `Specular_Map`); billboards (`Camera_azimuth_trig`, `Billboard_tangents`, `Horiz_fade_value`, `Lod_profile`, `Num_billboards`, `Billboard_texcoords`, `Horiz_texcoords`, `Camera_angles`); meteor effects (`Meteor_strength`, `Star_diffuse`, `Star_strength`, `Draw_distance`, `Render_target_dimensions`); raycast/depth export (`Points_target_dimensions`, `Depth_buffer_dimensions`, `Depth_buffer_sampler`, `Near_clip`, `Far_clip`, `Raycast_translation`, `Export_base_address`, `target_pitch`, `Source_uvs`); plus the already-known `World_xform`/`Scale`/`Offset`/`Camera_velocity`. **None of these ~90 distinct literal names is `Tint_color`/`tint_color`, and none is a plausible semantic alias either** — `Exposure_min`/`Exposure_max`/`Desired_brightness`/`Luminance_conversion` exist, exactly the kind of name the task's hint 2 predicted, but all four are registered against the tonemap/bloom pass's own dedicated technique object (`param_1+0x75c`), not against the car-paint shader, and none is named plainly `Exposure`/`Scene_Exposure`/`Global_Exposure`/`Paint_Exposure` as suggested. **[CONFIRMED NEGATIVE — exhaustive re-read of the full existing census, no new disassembly needed, `tools/scratchpad/CarPaintTrace6_out.txt`/`7_out.txt`.]**

**Check 2 — new, exhaustive, whole-binary scan: does ANY call site to any of the six VSCF/PSCF producer functions push the literal register number 37 (`0x25`)?** All six producers (`FUN_00475B50`/`BE0`/`C80`/`D80`/`E10`/`EB0`, the array/single-Vector4/by-ref VS and PS forms already catalogued in §20.2/§20.9) share the identical calling convention observed at every real call site in this document: the register number is the function's first parameter, and every call site pushes arguments in reverse order, so the register is always the LAST push immediately before the `CALL`. A script walked every real reference to all six producers (**85 total call sites, matching §20.9's own census exactly, plus the one new site found in Check 3 below**) and inspected the immediately-preceding instruction. Result: **exactly 9 sites push a literal immediate register number, and all 9 are already-documented** — `0x14`–`0x1b` (20–27 decimal), the reserved per-light constant block `FUN_0049BB40`/`FUN_0049BDB0` already fully described in §20.3(b). **Zero of the 85 sites pushes the literal `0x25` (37).** The remaining 76 sites all push a register (`ECX`/`EDX`/`EAX`/`EDI`), i.e. a runtime-computed value, not a compile-time literal — consistent with, and not adding anything new beyond, this document's own existing per-site tracing (`DAT_01354700`-family globals for `objTM`, the `DAT_0132e1xx` cluster for shadow-cascade, `param_1+0x19c`-style parameter structs for post-process, and the new mechanism in Check 3). **This closes, cleanly and exhaustively, the simplest possible hypothesis — a single dedicated function that just always uploads register 37 with a hardcoded literal — as a genuine negative, not an under-search.** **[CONFIRMED NEGATIVE — exhaustive, `tools/scratchpad/TintTrace5.java`/`_out.txt`.]**

**Check 3 — a genuinely new, disassembly-confirmed candidate MECHANISM was found, but could not be closed this pass.** `FUN_004A3360` (already on record since §20.3(c)/§20.12.5 as a per-frame fog/reflection-plane setup function, and already known to route SOME of its own upload-shaped calls through `rl_renderer`'s `_purecall`-trapped vtable per §20.12.5) contains a SEPARATE, previously-uncharacterized loop — confirmed by raw disassembly, not decompiler pseudocode, per `WALLS.md`'s own standing caution — that walks a small per-object table and issues DIRECT (non-vtable, non-trapped, genuinely live) calls to the array-form single-Vector4 producers:
- A base array of 40-byte (`0x28`) entries, each `{unknown +0..+3, register: int16 @ +4, value: Vector4 @ +6..+0x15}` — the REGISTER NUMBER is baked into the entry's own data, not a literal in the calling code and not a name-hash lookup.
- TWO separate int16 "dirty index" lists reference entries in that same base array by index: one for VS constants (list pointer + count at offsets `+0x20`/`+0x28` of the table-holder object), one for PS constants (list pointer + count at `+0x38`/`+0x40`, with index value `-1` used as a skip/invalid sentinel — the identical "`-1` if absent" convention already documented for the shader-object's own T8 accessor, §22.4).
- For every live index in each list, the loop calls `FUN_00475b50`(VS)/`FUN_00475d80`(PS) with the entry's own register and a pointer to its own Vector4 value, count 1 — a genuine per-object "flush whatever's marked dirty" pass, structurally distinct from both the per-material name-hash array-B/C mechanism (§20.12.6) and the resolve-once-at-init name-hash cache (§20.12.4).

**This mechanism runs PER VISIBLE RENDER ITEM, which is the right granularity to explain a vehicle-specific value — traced one hop further than the loop itself:** `FUN_004A3360`'s only caller anywhere in the binary is `FUN_0047B440`, whose only caller is `FUN_0047B1B0` (itself reached only via a vtable slot — a `DATA`-type reference at `0x012a0104`, i.e. a genuine virtual method, class not yet identified), which in turn is called **once per element of several plain `{pointer-array, count}` item lists** inside `FUN_004A2820`/`FUN_004A2E30` — both of which are per-frame pass drivers that end by recording opcode `0x17` onto the deferred command buffer directly. This item-list-loop shape (not a single per-frame global call) is consistent with `FUN_0047B1B0`'s argument being a per-visible-item object, the right frequency for a vehicle-specific value — unlike the ALREADY-refuted per-frame-global candidates checked earlier (§20.12.5's `FUN_0049C530`/`FUN_004A3360`-via-vtable, §20.12.10's `FUN_00474000`). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for the loop shape and the full call chain; HIGH CONFIDENCE, not fully proven, that the per-item lists carry per-material granularity.]**

**Why this is recorded as OPEN, not as the answer, despite the promising shape — two concrete unresolved problems, both flagged honestly for whoever picks this up next:**
1. **The table-holder object's exact class could not be confirmed.** `FUN_0047B1B0` dereferences its argument's own `+0xb0` field, then THAT pointer's own `+0x14` field, to reach the table (`+0x14+0x50` is read as a flag dword, `+0x14+0x68`/`+0x6c` as two more fields) — a shape that superficially resembles `rl_static_material`'s own documented "`+0x14` = parsed material data-record pointer" relationship (§22.3), but this was not RTTI-confirmed; no constructor or vtable-install site tying the `+0xb0` object to `rl_static_material` specifically was traced this pass.
2. **A genuine, unresolved tension with this document's own already-published finding.** §20.12.10 states the material record's own `+0x20` field is a FLAT, register-indexed `Vector4` buffer (byte offset = register×`0x10`) — re-verified again this pass directly against `FUN_00e70d30`'s own decompiled body (a `memcpy` into the buffer at register-index × `0x10` bytes past the record's own `+0x20` field, at the register-remap step), which is unambiguous and consistent with the earlier finding. But `FUN_004A3360`'s loop treats what looks like the SAME relative position (`+0x18`/`+0x20`/`+0x28`/`+0x38`/`+0x40` on ITS table-holder) as a totally different shape (a stride-`0x28` entry array plus two int16 dirty-index lists) — these two characterizations cannot both describe the identical object's identical fields. **Net: either the `FUN_004A3360` table-holder is a genuinely different object from the `FUN_00e71090`/`FUN_00e70d30` material record despite the superficial `+0xb0`→`+0x14` resemblance, or one of the two field-layout readings needs correcting. Not resolved this pass — do not treat either characterization as suspect on its own; the conflict itself is the open item.**
3. **Because this mechanism's register numbers are always read from table DATA, never a code literal, Check 2's exhaustive literal scan cannot confirm or refute a link to register 37 here.** Confirming or refuting this candidate specifically for `Tint_color` would require locating the table's own constructor/writer (not found this pass) and checking, statically or via a live trace, whether any entry it ever populates carries register `37` with a small non-zero value — genuinely out of reach without a further, deeper pass or a dynamic capture.

**Net position, honestly stated.** Two real avenues are now CLOSED as clean exhaustive negatives: no name-hash registration of `Tint_color` or any plausible alias exists anywhere in the `FUN_00e49990` census (Check 1, this pass's own re-confirmation of §20.12.12's original finding against a fuller name list), and no call site anywhere in the binary uploads to register 37 via a hardcoded literal (Check 2, genuinely new this pass). One real, disassembly-confirmed, structurally promising NEW mechanism was found (Check 3) — a per-render-item dirty-constant-cache flush that is exactly the general shape the task hypothesized — but it stops short of being a confirmed producer: the table-holder's class is unconfirmed, and re-examining it surfaced an unresolved tension with an already-published finding elsewhere in this document that needs sorting out before this lead can be pursued further. **`Tint_color`'s real producer for vehicle-body materials remains OPEN.** This is a genuine, honestly-scoped OPEN per this project's standing discipline — not a forced-fit conclusion — with a materially narrower, better-characterized remaining search space than before this pass: the search is no longer "is there ANY code path," it is specifically "who writes the entries of the `FUN_004A3360`-style per-item `{register,value}` table, and is that table genuinely distinct from the material record's own `+0x20` buffer."

**Evidence anchors:** `tools/scratchpad/TintTrace1.java` through `TintTrace5.java` and their `_out.txt` logs. Raw-disassembly-confirmed loop `0x004a35a3`-`0x004a3662` inside `FUN_004A3360` (VS section base `+0x20`/count `+0x28`, PS section base `+0x38`/count `+0x40`, shared entry array `+0x18`, stride `0x28`, register `entry+4`, value `entry+6`); call chain `FUN_004A2820`/`FUN_004A2E30`→`FUN_0047B1B0`(vtable data-ref `0x012a0104`)→`FUN_0047B440`→`FUN_004A3360`; re-verified `record+0x20` remap site inside `FUN_00e70d30` (`param_1[8]`, unchanged from §20.12.10). Literal-register census: `tools/scratchpad/TintTrace5.java`, 85 call sites across all six producers, 9 literal hits (all `0x14`-`0x1b`, already documented), 0 hits at `0x25`.

## 21. Follow-Up -- The rl_renderer Live-Vtable Question Closed (Statically)

**Scope.** A narrow, low-priority follow-up on the one open item §20.8 left unresolved: does the live `rl_renderer` singleton's vtable pointer get patched away from the abstract, `_purecall`-trap-laden static vtable (`0x0125a97c`) to a real, fully-overriding derived-class vtable at some point after construction? Two concrete static checks were run, plus one further check the results made necessary.

**Check 1 — does any OTHER class's RTTI list `rl_renderer` or `rl_irenderer` as a base?** A whole-image scan (all initialized sections, ~51 MB) for every dword-aligned occurrence of either class's `TypeDescriptor` address (`0x01355cb8` for `rl_renderer`, `0x01355c9c` for `rl_irenderer`) found: for `rl_renderer`, exactly 3 occurrences — the already-known self-reference inside its own `ClassHierarchyDescriptor`'s base array, plus two more that both resolve to fields of a **second** Complete Object Locator sitting immediately adjacent to the first one in `.rdata` (consistent with the already-known "second, separate vtable-shaped pointer" at the object's own `+0x4`, §18.10 — i.e. `rl_renderer`'s own secondary vtable for a multiple-inheritance sub-object view, not a different class). For `rl_irenderer`, exactly **1** occurrence in the entire image — the one already known, inside `rl_renderer`'s own base-class list. **No other class anywhere in this binary's RTTI data inherits from either.** [CONFIRMED — disassembly + whole-image data scan, `tools/scratchpad/RendererVtableCheck1.java`.]

**Check 2 — is the vtable literal `0x0125a97c` (or its sibling `0x0125a970`) ever installed anywhere other than the one already-known constructor?** A whole-binary instruction scan for either literal used as a store immediate (bare-register or displaced, catching a sub-object install too) found **exactly 4 hits, in exactly 2 functions**: `FUN_00e38350` (the already-known constructor, installing `0x0125a97c` at the object's own offset `+0` and `0x0125a970` at `+0x4`) and `FUN_00e38400` (the already-known destructor, re-installing the SAME two literals at the SAME two offsets — this is standard C++ destructor vtable-rearming semantics, and confirms `rl_renderer` re-arms to ITS OWN vtable, not some further base's, on the way down). **No other function anywhere in the binary ever writes either vtable literal.** [CONFIRMED — disassembly + empirical whole-binary scan, `tools/scratchpad/RendererVtableCheck2.java`.]

**Both checks came back a clean, exhaustive negative — refuting, not merely leaving open, the "a derived class installs a real vtable later" hypothesis.** `rl_renderer` genuinely is the most-derived class for this singleton; `0x0125a97c` genuinely is its only, final, live vtable.

**This sharpens the puzzle rather than closing it, so one more check was run: does the `_purecall` trap (`0x00ea2778`, §20.8) actually terminate the process, or could it be a harmless no-op in this build?** Full decompilation of its two tail-call targets settles this: `FUN_00eaeed1` is a small bitmask setter (consistent with `_set_abort_behavior`-style configuration flags), but `FUN_00eaedba` is the CRT's own **real `abort()` implementation** — it conditionally emits a CRT diagnostic message, checks for a registered invalid-parameter handler (raising `SIGABRT`, signal value `0x16`/22, if one exists), otherwise raises the documented Windows "fatal application exit" exception code `0x40000015` through `UnhandledExceptionFilter`, and unconditionally finishes with `__exit(3)` — a call Ghidra's own control-flow analysis marks **does not return**. [CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/RendererVtableCheck3.java`.] There is no neutered/logged-only escape hatch: reaching this trap for real would end the process.

**Direct re-verification that the call site really reads this exact object.** To rule out a misattribution, `FUN_00e74880`'s own `+0x10` call (§18.5/§20.3a) was re-disassembled at the instruction level: it reads the global `DAT_0351f8f8` itself (address `0x00e74e78`), dereferences its first field for the vtable pointer (`0x00e74e7e`), reads that vtable's own `+0x10` slot (`0x00e74e80`), and calls it with three pushed arguments — exactly the shape already documented, on exactly the global whose only-ever-installed value is `0x0125a97c` per check 2. No aliasing, no different object. [CONFIRMED — disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/RendererVtableCheck6.java`.]

**Conclusion.** The three checks together are airtight on the static facts: this vtable slot is a genuine, unconditional, process-terminating `_purecall` trap; it is the only vtable this object ever has; and the call site genuinely reads it. Since the shipped game plainly renders meshes without crashing, **the only remaining honest explanation is that this specific branch of `FUN_00e74880` (and any sibling call sites doing the same) is gated, in the observed retail configuration, by a condition that is always false for real game content** — the gate is the per-sub-item flag byte read via `FUN_00e7bf80` and the second object's own vtable`+0x1c` check, both immediately preceding the `+0x10` call in §18.5's own account. **This was not traced further this pass** (confirming the gate is always false for real content would need either an exhaustive trace of every producer of that flag, or a dynamic/runtime trace — the kind of follow-up this project's own standing policy reserves for a dedicated pass, not a low-priority cleanup item). This is now a precisely-scoped, well-anchored OPEN item — a real narrowing from "why do 28 slots share an address" (closed) through "does something patch the vtable" (closed, refuted) to "why is this specific gate apparently always false in retail content" (the one thing left).

**Evidence anchors:** whole-image RTTI scan and second-COL finding (`0x012beabc`, check 1); exhaustive vtable-literal-store scan (check 2); `_purecall` trap's real CRT termination path — `FUN_00eaeed1`, `FUN_00eaedba` (calls `_raise`/`SetUnhandledExceptionFilter`/`UnhandledExceptionFilter`/`__exit(3)`, exception code `0x40000015`, signal `0x16`) (check 3); re-verified call site `0x00e74e78`-`0x00e74e8a` in `FUN_00e74880`.

### 21.1 The gate reframed (2026-09-29): the "second object's vtable+0x1c check" is a hardcoded stub; the real determinant is a caller-supplied byte parameter, traced three hops to the same still-unidentified top-of-chain gap

**Scope.** Direct follow-up on this section's own closing "one thing left" -- whether the per-sub-item gate (§18.5 point 2: the mesh/group-record's own flag byte, plus a second object's vtable `+0x1c` check) is provably always false for real content. This pass got the exact instruction-level disassembly of the whole gate for the first time (previously only summarised, §18.5) and traced the actual condition it depends on. Method: disposable Ghidra project `tools/gp_render8`, PowerShell-only headless invocation, `tools/scratchpad/Gate1.java` through `Gate7.java`.

**The "second object" is real and RTTI-identifies as `rl_vertex_format_library`, not a dead/never-constructed global -- ruling out one easy escape hatch.** `DAT_0351f914` (the "second object" §18.5/§21/§22.5 attribute gating significance to) has a genuine constructor/destructor pair (`FUN_00e7c030`/`FUN_00e7c060`, installing/re-arming vtable `0x0125c828`) and is read from well over 100 other locations across the binary -- this is a live, actively-used engine singleton, not a permanent-sentinel case like §20.11's `FUN_004A9420` fields. RTTI decode (Complete Object Locator `0x012bf790` → `TypeDescriptor` `0x01355c1c`) gives the mangled name **`.?AVrl_vertex_format_library@@`** -- class **`rl_vertex_format_library`**. **[CONFIRMED -- disassembly + RTTI structure decode, `Gate2.java`/`Gate3.java`.]**

**But its own vtable `+0x1c` slot -- the actual "gate" method -- is a hardcoded, argument-ignoring stub that always returns true.** Disassembling the slot's target directly (`0x00bcb310`, 4 bytes total) finds: set a return value to 1, then return, cleaning up 8 bytes (2 explicit stack arguments) of its own calling convention -- it never reads either of its two arguments. **This means the "second object's vtable+0x1c check" performs no real filtering at all: it can never be the reason the trap is avoided, because it never says no.** [CONFIRMED -- disassembly, `Gate4.java`.] This corrects a specific, load-bearing detail in this document's own standing hypothesis (§18.5/§21/§22.5 all treated this as a genuine, potentially content-dependent gate) -- it is dead weight, not a filter.

**The real determinant is `FUN_00e74880`'s own caller-supplied byte parameter, not anything computed from mesh data.** Full disassembly of the loop (`0x00e74dc0`-`0x00e74e96`) shows the gate this section already documented sits INSIDE a larger branch: the mesh-header/group-record flag byte and the (now-confirmed-dead) `rl_vertex_format_library` check are only reachable at all when `FUN_00e74880`'s own 5th parameter (a byte, `param_5`) has both bit 0 and bit 2 clear (`(param_5 & 5) == 0`); when either bit is set, a structurally separate, already-partially-documented visibility/culling check (via `FUN_00e3eba0`) runs instead, and -- critically -- that separate check can ALSO reach the exact same trap call if its own result matches a condition on `param_5`'s own bit 0. In the `(param_5 & 5) == 0` branch specifically, there is no escape: the mesh-header-flag/`rl_vertex_format_library` gate this section has been examining is provably a no-op (per the point above), so entering that branch means the `DAT_0351f8f8` vtable `+0x10` trap call executes unconditionally. **[CONFIRMED -- disassembly, full decompile, `Gate5.java`.]**

**This byte is forwarded unchanged through the entire chain this document has already traced, and bottoms out at the same unidentified top-of-chain gap §18.6/§18.7/§18.8 already left open.** `FUN_00e74880` has exactly one caller, `FUN_00e58b70`, which forwards its own same-position parameter unmodified as `FUN_00e74880`'s `param_5` (`Gate6.java`). `FUN_00e58b70` has exactly one caller, `FUN_00b13850` -- the same "already-known chain-start function" §16/§18.6 already named -- which likewise forwards its own same-position parameter unmodified (`Gate7.java`). **`FUN_00b13850` itself has zero direct callers anywhere in the binary -- it is reached only virtually** (one `DATA`-type reference, a vtable-slot literal at `0x01186c84`), exactly the same "the very top of the closed chain... the per-frame/per-visible-list walker that actually invokes it were not identified" gap this document already recorded at §18.6/§18.8. [CONFIRMED -- disassembly for every hop; the top caller's identity is OPEN, unchanged from §18.8.]

**Conclusion.** This pass sharpens, but does not close, §21's open item. Two concrete, useful results: (1) the "second object's vtable+0x1c check" is CONFIRMED to be a hardcoded always-true stub, not a real content-dependent gate -- the standing hypothesis that treated it as a meaningful filter was wrong on this specific point, and should not be repeated; (2) the actual, sole determinant of whether the `rl_renderer` `_purecall` trap is reached from this call site is a single caller-supplied byte parameter that this document can now trace, unmodified, through three full hops (`FUN_00b13850`→`FUN_00e58b70`→`FUN_00e74880`) to a virtual-dispatch call site whose caller is not yet identified -- the identical gap already on record since §18.6 ("which slot, and what the actual per-frame/per-visible-list walker that invokes it is, was not identified"). **Resolving this fully now reduces to one already-known, well-scoped task: identify whatever virtually dispatches through `FUN_00b13850`'s vtable slot (and `FUN_00b17000`'s sibling slot, §18.6) and determine what byte value it always passes for real shipped content** -- exactly the kind of dynamic-trace-or-exhaustive-caller-search follow-up §21 already anticipated, now with a single concrete byte-parameter value to check for, rather than an open-ended "why doesn't this crash" question. **OPEN, honestly, with a materially smaller remaining surface than before this pass.**

**Evidence anchors:** full gate disassembly `0x00e74dc0`-`0x00e74e96` (`Gate1.java`); `DAT_0351f914` RTTI (`rl_vertex_format_library`, ctor/dtor `FUN_00e7c030`/`FUN_00e7c060`, vtable `0x0125c828`, COL `0x012bf790`, TypeDescriptor `0x01355c1c`) (`Gate2.java`/`Gate3.java`); byte-lookup helper `FUN_00e7bf80` (bounds `<0x6e`, else `-1`); hardcoded-true gate stub `0x00bcb310`; `FUN_00e74880` full decompile and `param_5` branch (`Gate5.java`); forwarding chain `FUN_00b13850`→`FUN_00e58b70`→`FUN_00e74880` (`Gate6.java`/`Gate7.java`); top-of-chain vtable-slot reference `0x01186c84` (still unidentified, matches §18.6/§18.8).

## 22. Producer-Side Follow-Up, Pass 5 -- Vehicle-Body Material/Shader-Bind Chain Traced to a Confirmed fxo Shader-Object Instance (Peer-Team Join Request)

**Scope.** Direct follow-up requested by a peer team building a public-format D3D9 shader-bytecode-to-HLSL translator, joining against vehicle-body geometry (`spec-vehicle-geometry.md`, `spec-vehicle-data.md`) — this project's most mature, most independently-verified documentation area. Goal: trace the shader/technique-BIND call(s) that occur in the *same* code path as the already-confirmed vehicle-body mesh-draw chain (`FUN_00b17000`→`FUN_00b16d50`→`FUN_00b13bf0`→`FUN_00477400`→`FUN_004764d0`, §18.6), and get as concrete a shader identity as the trace supports. Method: disposable Ghidra project `tools/gp_render7` (robocopied from `tools/ghidra_projects`), PowerShell-only headless invocation, `tools/scratchpad/ShaderBindTrace1.java` through `ShaderBindTrace11.java`. Cross-references `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.4–§6.5 throughout rather than re-deriving it.

### 22.1 Two material/technique dispatches confirmed inside the already-closed draw loop

`FUN_00b16d50`'s own per-range loop (the second hop of the closed chain, §18.6) was re-disassembled in full. In the same iteration that calls `FUN_00b13bf0` (the confirmed per-range `DrawIndexedPrimitive` resolver), it makes two further, distinct virtual dispatches:

1. **A call on the object at the caller's own `+0x130` field** (`param_1[0x4c]`), gated by that object's own vtable `+0x24`, then dispatched through its vtable `+0x1c` with a single argument: the current draw-range record's own `+0x2` sub-field — the packed "high16" vertex-channel selector `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.1/§11.5 and `spec-render-pipeline.md` §18.6 already resolved. **This is the exact same object and the exact same call shape** (`this+0x130`, vtable `+0x1c`, high16 argument, vtable `+0x24` gate) that §16.2/§16.3/§18.8 already found and left OPEN in the *separate*, previously-more-prominent `FUN_00e74880`/`FUN_00e58480` chain. Finding it called directly inside the NOW-CLOSED `FUN_00b16d50` chain too means `this+0x130` is shared infrastructure between both draw paths, not evidence they are unrelated — it narrows, without fully closing, §18.8's own "genuinely parallel or reconverge" question: at minimum, they share this one hop. Its class is still not identified. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, for the call shape and its identity with the earlier-documented one; class identity remains OPEN, unchanged from §16.3/§18.8.]**
2. **A material/technique dispatch reached through a small positional index.** Immediately before calling `FUN_00b13bf0`, the loop resolves a **material index** by reading the low-16 bits of the draw-range record's own packed `+0x00` selector and using it, positionally, to index a small per-mesh array at the mesh-resource holder's own `+0xF8` field (the same holder structure §9/§16.2/§18.6 already established) — i.e., the low16 half of the packed selector is the *record's own material slot*, exactly as `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11's own vertex-range/material-id work already characterizes it. That resolved value is then passed to two small functions, **`FUN_00e3f290`** (returns a per-material sub-batch count) and, per sub-batch, **`FUN_00e3f740`** — which dispatches, through a global table entry, a virtual call at that entry's target object's own vtable **`+0x10`**, passing the sub-batch index. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, both functions fully decompiled.]**

### 22.2 The global material registry is the render-side half of an already cross-subsystem-shared table

`FUN_00e3f740`/`FUN_00e3f290` both index a flat global array, **`DAT_02ac693c`** (stride `0x14`, count bound `DAT_02ac6932`), by the low-16 bits of the material index (the high two bits carry two flag bits set at registration, §22.3). Each entry's own `+0x08` field is a "handle" whose first field is the actual target-object pointer (an `+0x0C` field can override it directly); `+0x10` is the entry's own self-index, written once at allocation as a validity/back-reference check. `FUN_00e3f740` and its sibling flush function `FUN_00e3f190` (called on deselect) each resolve that target object and call its vtable `+0x10` (bind sub-batch N) or `+0x14` (a bookkeeping flag toggle, §22.4) respectively.

**This table's registration path (`FUN_00e3fcb0`→`FUN_00e3faa0`/`FUN_00e3f8e0`, hash buckets at `DAT_02b2a958`, hash % `0x5000`) is the SAME "name-hash→material-handle cache" `HANDOFF.md` §27.6 already documented from the *unrelated* `.clmesh_pc` (Havok collision-mesh) investigation** — confirmed directly this pass, not merely by address-neighbourhood resemblance: one of `FUN_00e3fcb0`'s ~24 direct callers is `FUN_00e3e590` itself, the exact `.clmesh_pc` MIDDLE-record parser `HANDOFF.md` §27.6 names; separately, the render-side registration path (§22.3) pops its own "handle" object from `FUN_00e3ffd0`'s free-list (`DAT_0132d7f8`), the identical free-list function already documented for the `.clmesh_pc` material-handle cache. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly — one shared global material registry serves both the Havok collision-mesh material system and this render-side vehicle-body material path, not two independently-alike systems.]**

**The hash itself is computed via a virtual call on the key object** (`FUN_00e7a520`, a lazy one-shot cache wrapper around the key object's own vtable `+0x68`) — meaning the actual hash function is class-dependent, not a single fixed routine. For the render-mesh material path specifically (§22.3), that class's `+0x68` implementation hashes several of the *material record's own numeric fields* via `FUN_00d9e6c0` (a function in the same `0xd9exxx` neighbourhood as, but distinct from, this project's already-documented CRC-32 routine `FUN_00D9E8B0`/`FUN_00D9E740`) — **a content/structural hash for de-duplicating identical material instances, not a name-string hash.** This is consistent with, and reinforces, `spec-vehicle-geometry.md`'s own extensively-confirmed finding that vehicle material records carry **no identity/name field at all** (§11.2 there). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for the mechanism; HIGH CONFIDENCE that it is a de-dup key rather than a shader-name lookup, given the fields hashed are the record's own colour/texture/flag data, not a string.]**

### 22.3 The dispatched class: `rl_static_material` — RTTI-confirmed, allocated purely from positional mesh data

The object registered into the table (§22.2) — the class whose vtable `+0x10`/`+0x14`/`+0x18`/`+0x20`/`+0x68` are the ones actually called — was identified via the same Complete-Object-Locator/TypeDescriptor RTTI decode this document already used for `rl_renderer` (§18.10) and `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` (§20.11). Its allocator, **`FUN_00e5e240`** (a free-list pop off `DAT_0132df68`), installs vtable literal **`0x0125b4b4`**; decoding the Complete Object Locator immediately before it yields the mangled name **`.?AVrl_static_material@@`** — class **`rl_static_material`**. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + RTTI structure decode, same technique as §18.10/§20.11.]**

Tracing who constructs and registers an `rl_static_material` instance closes the loop back to already-documented vehicle-geometry work: `spec-vehicle-geometry.md`'s own line-991 citation of **`FUN_0074d260`** ("resolves a material via `FUN_00e401d0`/`FUN_00e71090`... allocates the renderer object, calls slot `+0x10`") is directly confirmed here as one of `FUN_00e3fcb0`'s real callers. Per-material-record, `FUN_0074d260` parses ONE material definition via the already-documented shared parser **`FUN_00e71090`** (no identity field, purely positional — unchanged from `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.2), obtains a fresh `rl_static_material` via `FUN_00e5e240`, and registers it into the global table (§22.2) keyed by the content hash of that same parsed record. **`rl_static_material`'s own `+0x14` field is exactly this parsed data-record pointer** — every accessor on its vtable (`+0x1c`→`record+4`, `+0x20`→`record+8 & 1`, and the technique-select body itself, §22.4) reads through it. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, the whole chain `FUN_0074d260`→`FUN_00e401d0`/`FUN_00e71090`→`FUN_00e5e240`→`FUN_00e3fcb0`; the same `FUN_00482d20`-class-pool "renderer object" `FUN_0074d260` also allocates for GPU buffer upload, `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.9, is a DIFFERENT, sibling object — `rl_d3d_mesh` by RTTI — not to be confused with `rl_static_material` itself.]**

### 22.4 The technique-select body and the fixed shader-object table

`rl_static_material`'s vtable `+0x10` (the slot `FUN_00e3f740` calls at draw time, §22.1) is a five-argument thin forwarder to **`FUN_00e5e530`** (1,017 bytes) — the real technique-select implementation. Its structure:

1. Resolves a small **pass-role number** — observed values `0`, `2`, `4`, `5`, `6`, `7` — from the current render-context's own state flags (or, when the instance carries an explicit override at its own `+0x8` field, from that override directly, gated by a capability check, `FUN_00e4a070`). This matches `spec-fxo-format.md` §7.4's own "role number" terminology exactly.
2. Calls **`FUN_00e4a180`** with (a small **material-category** value — either the data record's own first dword or one of three fixed state slots at a neighbouring global, `DAT_0351f8fc+0xce58/0xce5c/0xce60`, depending on render-context flags), the resolved pass-role number, and an optional override.
3. **`FUN_00e4a180`** performs a **fixed three-dimensional table lookup**: `materialCategory * 0x1b + tierB(0..3) + tierA(0..5) * 4`, bound-checked against a live count field on its own "this"-like first argument, into a pointer array at that same object's own `+0x08` field. The two small index values (`tierA` bound `<6`, `tierB` bound `<4`) come from two short, undecompiled helper calls (`FUN_00e48230`, `FUN_00e48250`) — most plausibly a render-context-derived quality/LOD-tier and a secondary variant selector, not traced further this pass. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly for the table-index arithmetic and bound checks; HIGH CONFIDENCE, not fully traced, for what `tierA`/`tierB` semantically select.]**
4. The resolved pointer's **class was verified, address-for-address, against `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5's own already-documented per-file `.fxo_pc` "shader object"** (vtable `0x012A1254`, init `0x0048EFE0`) — not merely an offset-number coincidence, the exact failure mode `WALLS.md` warns about: a direct raw-byte dump of vtable `0x012A1254` shows slot `+0x10` = `0x0048EFE0` (the init routine §6.5 already names), slot `+0x14` = `0x0048F7C0`, and slot `+0x3c` = `0x0048F770` — **byte-for-byte identical to the two addresses §6.5's own method table already cites** for "select pass by role, tell the render context to use it" (`+0x14`) and "T8 lookup by hash, `-1` if absent" (`+0x3c`). `FUN_00e4a180` calls exactly these two slots, with exactly the argument shapes §6.5 already documents: `+0x14` with the resolved role number, and `+0x3c` with a single argument whose result is checked `< 0` (the documented `-1`-if-absent shape). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, exact vtable-slot-address match, both slots independently checked.]**

This closes, for this one producer, the wiring §14 item 3 (as amended by §20) left open — "the literal `0x00474890` bind method's own wiring into these specific producers was not traced end-to-end": `rl_static_material`'s technique-select method (`FUN_00e5e530`) is a real, disassembly-confirmed caller that reaches directly into the per-file shader-object's own `+0x14` role-select method, which `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5 already documents as what in turn drives `0x00474890`'s (object, pass-index) cache. This pass did not itself re-decompile `0x0048F7C0`'s body to reconfirm the inner `0x00474890` call — that link is cross-referenced from the existing `.fxo_pc` spec, not re-derived, per this task's own instruction.

### 22.5 The same technique-select body confirms two more architectural links, cheaply

Two further, incidental findings fell out of fully decompiling `FUN_00e5e530`:

- **A direct tie into the already-documented §18.1/§18.2 material-bind-flush machinery.** When a render context exists, `FUN_00e5e530` calls **`FUN_00474ea0`**, which reads the *current render object's own `+0x760` field* — the identical field the four material-bind wrappers of §18.1 (`FUN_00477060`/`FUN_00477030`/`FUN_00476f70`/`FUN_00476ff0`) already read — and forwards it into **`FUN_00475790`**, the exact opcode-`0x14` variable-length "batched sampler/texture-stage state" producer §18.2 already fully documents, then sets the same render-object's own `+0x50a`/`+0x50b` dirty-flag pair §18.1's wrappers already set. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly.]** `rl_static_material`'s own texture references (a count/array pair at the data record's own `+0xC`/`+0x10` fields) are separately bound one-by-one through `rl_renderer`'s vtable `+0x54`, each classified into one of three semantic-table bitmasks at `DAT_01351cdc`/`DAT_01351ce0`/`DAT_01351ce4` — the same `0x01351cxx`/`0x01351dxx` semantic-table cluster §8 and `spec-vertex-format.md` already cite. **Texture binding here, like material binding (§22.2), is purely positional** (a texture-slot index plus a resolved handle) — no name lookup at this layer either.
- **A concrete new data point for §21's still-open "gating condition" question.** Gated specifically on the material data record's own `+0xE` byte flag (a genuine per-material content field, not an always-false-looking condition), `FUN_00e5e530` makes two direct calls through `rl_renderer`'s own vtable (`DAT_0351f8f8`) at offsets `+0x58` and `+0x5c` — both squarely inside the `+0x10`..`+0x7c` range §18.10/§20.8/§21 already established is filled with the shared MSVC `_purecall` trap for every `rl_irenderer`-declared virtual `rl_renderer` never overrides. This is offered as one more real, disassembly-confirmed call site landing in that trap range, worth folding into whatever eventually resolves §21's open gating question — not chased further here, as it is outside this task's scope.

### 22.6 Shader identity — RESOLVED: a genuine CRC-32 name-hash, exhaustively confirmed against real data (see §22.9-§22.11)

**This subsection's original conclusion (below, struck through in spirit, kept rather than silently deleted per this project's standing practice) was overturned one hop later in the same pass, after a direct real-data check contradicted it.** ~~The binding mechanism is settled end to end: positional/index-based, not name-hash-based, at every step. The one hash present (§22.2) is a content/de-dup key, not a shader-name lookup, because vehicle material records carry no identity field (`spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.2).~~ **Wrong about there being no hash in the chain: §22.9 finds the missing resolution step (`FUN_00e70d30`→`FUN_00e4a6d0`→`FUN_00e4a480`), and §22.10 confirms, exhaustively (37/37 real distinct values, zero exceptions), that the on-disk material-record field `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.2 already named "shaderHash" really is a CRC-32 hash of the compiled shader's own filename stem — resolved ONCE at load time into the small dense index this section's original trace correctly found being used positionally at draw time. Concrete, real, shipped filenames for vehicle-body paint/glass are given in §22.11.** The "no identity field" finding this subsection originally leaned on remains true and is not contradicted — it is about draw-range/part *association* (there is no field saying "this material belongs to part/run N"), a different identity question from "which shader does this material use," which the record answers in hashed form via `shaderHash`.

### 22.7 Evidence anchors

Closed-chain material dispatches: `this+0x130` vtable `+0x1c`/`+0x24` (shared with §16.2/§16.3), `FUN_00e3f290`, `FUN_00e3f740`. Global material registry: `DAT_02ac693c`/`DAT_02ac6932`, `FUN_00e3fcb0`, `FUN_00e3faa0`, `FUN_00e3f8e0`, `FUN_00e3f830`, `FUN_00e3f190`, `FUN_00e3ece0`, `FUN_00e3edf0`, `FUN_00e7a520`, hash buckets `DAT_02b2a958`; cross-subsystem confirmation via `FUN_00e3e590` (`.clmesh_pc`) and free-list `FUN_00e3ffd0`. `rl_static_material`: vtable `0x0125b4b4`, RTTI name `.?AVrl_static_material@@`, allocator `FUN_00e5e240` (free-list `DAT_0132df68`), hash method `FUN_00e5e450` (calls `FUN_00d9e6c0`), bind forwarder `FUN_00e5e930`→`FUN_00e5e530`, deselect `FUN_00e5e370`, accessors `FUN_00e5e170`/`FUN_00e5e180`/`FUN_00e5e100`/`FUN_00e5e220` (a one-line thiscall setter for `+0x14`, `*(ECX+0x14) = param_1` — newly named 2026-09-30 tracing the zone-side consumers, `spec-terrain-format.md` open item 3/`spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.9.3; distinct from the `+0x8`/`+0x4` role-override fields §23.2 documents). Loader chain: `FUN_0074d260` (`spec-vehicle-geometry.md` line-991 citation), `FUN_00e401d0`, `FUN_00e71090` (shared, no-identity-field material parser). Technique-select/table lookup: `FUN_00e5e530`, `FUN_00e4a070`, `FUN_00e4a180`, helper calls `FUN_00e48230`/`FUN_00e48250` (not decompiled). Fxo shader-object class verification: vtable `0x012A1254` slots `+0x10`=`0x0048EFE0`, `+0x14`=`0x0048F7C0`, `+0x3c`=`0x0048F770` (exact match to `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5). Material-bind-flush tie-in: `FUN_00474ea0`→`FUN_00475790` (opcode `0x14`, §18.2), render-object field `+0x760`, dirty-flag pair `+0x50a`/`+0x50b`. Texture-binding semantic tables: `DAT_01351cdc`/`DAT_01351ce0`/`DAT_01351ce4`. New `rl_renderer` shared-slot call site (§21 corroboration): `+0x58`/`+0x5c`, gated on data-record `+0xE`. Hash-resolution step (§22.9/§22.10): `FUN_00e70d30` (material binder), `FUN_00e4a6d0`, `FUN_00e4a480` (shared hash-bucket lookup, same one `FUN_00e4a590` uses to register), `FUN_00e49ac0` (sibling bound-checked table accessor), `FUN_00dcecc0` (texture-name resolve); CRC-32 routine (reflected `0xEDB88320`, init 0, no final XOR, `spec-save-format.md` §6); real-data harnesses `tools/harnesses/vehshader_dump.py`, `tools/harnesses/vehshader_dump2.py`; real filename list `tools/scratchpad/shaders_vpp_names.txt` (844 `.fxo_pc` names read directly from `shaders.vpp_pc`'s own directory/name table).

Real filenames given in §22.11.**

(These select the STAGE-SUFFIX file, §6.6, within the resolved stem's library entry — not which stem itself, which §22.9/§22.10 now answer separately.)
- **The handle-wiring detail inside `FUN_00e3f830`** (exactly how a freshly-registered material's target object gets installed into its pre-allocated handle slot) was read but not fully reconciled instruction-by-instruction; the overall registration mechanism (§22.2) is solid, this is a minor internal-plumbing detail.

### 22.9 The missing resolution step: `FUN_00e4a6d0`/`FUN_00e4a480` — the on-disk field genuinely is a CRC-32 name-hash

**Direct follow-up, same pass:** a real-data check (`tools/harnesses/vehshader_dump.py`, run against all 393 real `.ccar_pc` files via the already-validated `parse_materials()` parser, `tools/harnesses/veh_pairing.py`) surfaced a contradiction that reopened §22.6's original conclusion: the on-disk material record's own `+0x00` field — already named **`shaderHash`** in `spec-vehicle-geometry.md` §11.2, from an earlier, unrelated pass — takes only **37 distinct 32-bit values across all 17,026 non-null material records in the entire vehicle roster**, every one far too large (minimum `118744446`) to be the small, count-bounded array index `FUN_00e4a180` indexes with (§22.4). This directly contradicted §22.6's original reading and required tracing one hop further.

**The resolution step is `FUN_00e70d30`** — the "material binder" `FUN_00e71090` (§22.3's shared, no-identity-field record parser) tail-calls exactly when its own caller passes a nonzero flag, which the vehicle loader (`FUN_0074d260`) does. After binding the record's own texture references (via `FUN_00dcecc0`), it calls:

- **`FUN_00e4a6d0(rawShaderHash)`** — which itself calls **`FUN_00e4a480(hash, &outPtr)`**, the *exact same* hash-bucket lookup function `FUN_00e4a590` (§6.6's own already-documented 400-slot shader-LIBRARY registration routine — re-confirmed this pass by direct decompile, not just cross-reference) uses to check for an existing entry before creating one. If found, `FUN_00e4a6d0` converts the matched entry's pointer back into a dense array index (`(entryPtr − tableBase − 8) / 0x6C` — `0x6C` being the exact per-entry byte size §6.6 already documents) and returns it; otherwise `-1`.
- **The material record's own `+0x00` field is then overwritten in place** with this resolved dense index (or `-1` on a miss) — every later read of this field (including the one `rl_static_material`'s technique-select body performs, §22.4) sees the *already-resolved* small index, never the raw on-disk hash — which is exactly why §22.4's own table-index arithmetic checked out structurally while §22.6's original "not name-hash" conclusion did not: both observations were correct about different points in the same field's lifetime.

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly, `FUN_00e70d30`/`FUN_00e4a6d0` both fully decompiled; `FUN_00e4a480`'s own body not separately decompiled this pass but independently identified, by exact call-site match, as the one shared hash-lookup primitive both the registration side (§6.6) and this resolution side use.]**

### 22.10 Exhaustive real-data confirmation: 37/37 distinct on-disk `shaderHash` values match a real shipped `.fxo_pc` filename stem

Given §22.9's finding that the on-disk field is looked up as a hash key at all, the natural next check is direct: hash the STEM (post-suffix-stripping, per `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.6's own already-documented 13-suffix convention) of every one of the **844 real `.fxo_pc` filenames actually present in the shipped `shaders.vpp_pc` archive's own directory/name table** (read directly, `tools/scratchpad/shaders_vpp_names.txt` — a plain TOC/name-table read, not a payload decode, so unaffected by that archive's known mode-(a) payload-readability limits, `spec-fxo-format.md` §9) using this project's own already-established CRC-32 routine (reflected polynomial `0xEDB88320`, initial value `0`, no final XOR — `spec-save-format.md` §6, `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.4) and check it against all 37 distinct real on-disk `shaderHash` values pulled from the 17,026 real vehicle material records (§22.9).

**Result: 37 / 37 (100%) match, exactly, with zero ambiguity or near-misses.** `tools/harnesses/vehshader_dump2.py` (new this pass) produces the full table. **[CONFIRMED — empirical, exhaustive over every distinct value observed in real shipped vehicle data, cross-checked against every real shipped shader filename stem, zero exceptions.]** This closes the task's central question directly: **shader identity for vehicle-body materials IS resolved by a CRC-32 name-hash of the compiled shader's own filename stem** — the same general CRC-32 convention this project has documented repeatedly elsewhere (save data, customization bundle names, unlockable/cheat ids) — just applied at a different pipeline stage (once, at material-record load time, §22.9) than the one §20.4 checked (the shader-CONSTANT producers, a later and genuinely separate stage that §20.4 correctly found does NOT use a name-hash — that finding is unaffected and still stands).

**Why a peer-team parallel probe (relayed mid-pass) found a negative on this exact field:** testing "NameHash/NameHashCaseSensitive/vpp::hashFilename" against the *full* filename (with extension and/or suffix) would miss this — the hash is of the **suffix-stripped stem only** (e.g. `ir_sr3carpaint_gr`, not `ir_sr3carpaint_gr_bs.fxo_pc` and not `ir_sr3carpaint_gr_bs`), computed with this project's specific reflected/init-0/no-final-XOR CRC-32 variant. Both conditions have to match exactly; a generic or differently-parameterised hash routine, or hashing the un-stripped filename, silently fails without indicating why. Separately, a different hash this document already traced (§22.2's `FUN_00e5e450`/`FUN_00d9e6c0`, the `rl_static_material` registration-time DE-DUP hash over several of the record's *other* fields) is a genuinely different quantity from `shaderHash` and was correctly found NOT to be a name-hash — the two should not be conflated.

The following real, shipped `.fxo_pc` stems were matched (§22.10) to real `shaderHash` values actually present in vehicle material records, with the number of real (vehicle, material-record) pairs each one accounts for across all 393 `.ccar_pc` files (17,026 non-null records total) and representative bound texture references (via the already-validated blob/name resolution in `veh_pairing.py`):

| Rank | Stem (suffix-stripped; append `_s`/`_bs`/`_ms`/`_mv`/`_v`/`_c` per `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.6 for the actual per-stage file) | Records | Representative textures | Role (inferred from name + textures) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ir_bbsimple_1uv_decal` | 4,545 | `flat-normalmap_n.tga`, `al_veh_universal.tga` | generic flat decal/sticker element |
| 2 | `ir_sr3carlightcover` | 4,464 | `al_veh_universal.tga` | headlight/taillight lens cover |
| 3 | `ir_sr3carviewsphere_g` | 1,149 | `viewsphere_chrome.tga` | chrome/rubber trim environment-reflection sphere |
| 4 | `ir_sr3cardiffusespec` | 1,085 | `al_veh_universal.tga` | plain diffuse+specular vehicle part |
| 5 | `ir_sr3cardiffusespec_g` | 1,085 | `al_veh_universal.tga`, `burn_test.tga` | as above + damage/gloss variant |
| 6 | **`ir_sr3carpaint_gr`** | **796** | `shd_damagenormal_n.tga` | **the single most common car-BODY-PAINT shader** (gloss+reflection) |
| 7 | `ir_sr3carglass_g` | 673 | (none/`burn_test.tga`) | most common car-glass shader (gloss variant) |
| 8 | `ir_sr3carviewsphere_gdid1` | 498 | `viewsphere_chrome.tga` | chrome trim, detail-map variant |
| 9 | `ir_sr3carviewsphere` | 357 | `viewsphere_chrome.tga` | plain chrome/rubber trim reflection |
| 10 | `ir_sr3carpaint_g` | 299 | `shd_damagenormal_n.tga` | plain gloss-only car paint |
| — | `ir_sr3carglass` | 245 | (none) | plain car glass, no detail maps |
| — | 12 further `ir_sr3carpaint_*` variants (`_grdf1`, `_r`, `_gdidf1`, `_grdf1df2`, `_gdi`, `_rdf1`, `_grd1`, `_rdf1df2`, `_gdidf1df2`, `_gdird1d2`, `_rd1`, `_grd1d2`, `_gdid1`, `_rd1d2`) | 2–289 each | varies (damage/detail/decal overlays) | the rest of the car-paint permutation family — 16 of the library's 17 `ir_sr3carpaint_*` stems are used somewhere in the real vehicle roster |
