| — | 5 further `ir_sr3carglass_*` variants (`_gdf1`, `_df1`, `_gdid1`, `_gdi`) | 10–130 each | detail/defrost/decal overlays | the rest of the car-glass permutation family |
| — | `ir_sr3tanktread` | 8 | `sp_tank01_tread_n.tga` | tank-specific tread shader (one vehicle) |
| — | `ir_bbsimple1` | 118 | `TL_diamond_*.tga` | simple billboard (taillight diamond pattern) |

**For "an ordinary vehicle-body material" specifically: `ir_sr3carpaint_gr` is the single most common real car-paint shader stem (796 of the vehicle roster's material records), and `ir_sr3carglass_g` is the single most common car-glass stem (673 records) — both real, confirmed, shipped `.fxo_pc` filenames (as `ir_sr3carpaint_gr_bs.fxo_pc`, `_ms.fxo_pc`, `_mv.fxo_pc`, `_s.fxo_pc`, `_v.fxo_pc` per the stage-suffix convention, §6.6) — but no single one is universal: 16 car-paint and 6 car-glass stem variants are all genuinely in use, selected per-material by the shipped content's own authored `shaderHash`, not by any single fixed default.** **[CONFIRMED — empirical, exhaustive population count, `tools/harnesses/vehshader_dump2.py`.]**

**One structural wrinkle noted, not fully reconciled:** `FUN_00e49ac0` (a sibling bound-checked accessor into the same table, called from `FUN_00e70d30` right after the resolution step to look up per-texture sampler-slot hashes via what matches `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5's own `+0x40` "T0 lookup by hash" method) reads its entry's class/variant sub-selector from byte offsets `+0x68`/`+0x6c`, while `FUN_00e4a590`'s own registration-time writes (as decompiled this pass) place class/variant at `+0x60`/`+0x64` and the full filename pointer at `+0x68`. This 8-byte discrepancy was not reconciled within this pass's time budget — plausibly an additional write this pass's decompile of `FUN_00e4a590` did not fully capture, or a struct-layout misread — and is flagged honestly rather than papered over; it does not affect §22.10's real-data conclusion, which never depended on these exact sub-offsets.

## 23. Frame/Pass Orchestration — Which Pass Runs When, What Selects Its Role, and the G-Buffer/Reflection-Map Question (Peer-Team Join Request)

**Scope.** Direct follow-up requested by Team B after drawing the vehicle-body shader's pass 0 (role 4, the cheap 2–5-constant pass, §20.12.1) as a first proof of concept. Pass 0 is confirmed elsewhere in this section as NOT a standalone shadow-only pass but a genuine multi-render-target bind point; this section traces, for the first time in this project's history, the actual per-frame code that (1) decides which of the five declared pass-roles (0/2/4/5/6/7 observed; 1 reachable only by explicit override) applies to a given draw, (2) sets up and tears down each pass, and (3) supplies the main lighting pass's real inputs. Method: disposable Ghidra project `tools/gp_render9` (robocopied from `tools/ghidra_projects`), PowerShell-only headless invocation, `tools/scratchpad/Frame1.java` through `Frame11.java`. This is architecture/orchestration tracing, not per-register tracing — several leads end this pass genuinely OPEN, flagged as such rather than guessed at, per `HANDOFF.md` §27.8.

### 23.1 Summary table

| Task question | Status | One-line finding |
|---|---|---|
| What selects role 0/2/4/5/6/7 for a draw | **CONFIRMED** | Four byte flags (`+0x4f7`/`+0x4f8`/`+0x4f9`/`+0x4fc`) on the thread-local "current render object," read by `rl_static_material`'s own technique-select body (`FUN_00e5e530`, §22.4) — §23.2. |
| Who sets those flags across a frame | **CONFIRMED** (setters identified) / **HIGH CONFIDENCE** (which pass each belongs to) | Four dedicated setter functions, each a real, disassembly-confirmed bracket around one specific pass's execution — §23.3. |
| Frame pass order | **HIGH CONFIDENCE**, not fully closed | The two main phase-entry functions are both virtual methods of the SAME RTTI-identified class (`rl_d3d_scene_renderer`) at fixed vtable offsets; the actual top-level per-frame driver that invokes them was not found — §23.4. |
| Does pass 0 (role 4/5) write multiple render targets (G-buffer hypothesis) | **CONFIRMED** (the bind mechanism) / **HYPOTHESIS** (that the targets are a normal/depth/albedo G-buffer specifically) | A dedicated function binds 3 color render targets + 1 depth-stencil surface via a disassembly-confirmed `RecordSetRenderTarget` producer, immediately before every execution of the role-4/5 pass — §23.5. |
| Light list (positions/colors/count) build | **CONFIRMED** (iteration mechanism + VS-side per-light payload) / **OPEN** (PS-side lighting-math upload) | `FUN_00e6ce70`'s register-resolution cache unchanged. The real per-frame mechanism is now found: lights are ordinary entries in the same generic per-visible-item submission loop (`FUN_00e74880`/`FUN_00e3de80`/`FUN_00e45eb0`) every mesh draw already goes through; a per-item flag gates two VS-constant uploaders (`FUN_0049BDB0`, `FUN_0049bb40`) filling an 8-register literal VS block per light. The PS-side `IR_Light_Pos`/`Color`/`Info` upload is still unlocated — §23.6. |
| Reflection-map source / pass 4 (role 1) hypothesis | **OPEN** — four leads now CLOSED (refuted) | The second main-pass-executor call site is a debug render-target-thumbnail overlay, not a reflection trigger; the `Reflection_Plane_Height` string-table slot is provably dead (zero references); the `rl_static_material` allocation chain has no `+0x8` writer; a same-day follow-up opened the loop's three remaining sub-calls too (also newly RTTI-identifying three sibling classes built alongside it each pass) — none writes `+0x8` either — §23.7. |
| G-buffer readback (`IR_GBuffer_DSF_DataSampler`) | **OPEN** | No literal `IR_GBuffer`/`GBuffer` string exists anywhere in the executable image — §23.8. |
| Pixel format of the 3 color + 1 depth target slots | **REFUTED (the 4 candidate writers)** / **OPEN (the real format)** | All four originally-flagged candidate writer functions were opened and none sets up a pixel format — 3 are coincidental offset collisions with an unrelated weather/environment class, 1 is a blanket-zeroing constructor. A further forward trace from the confirmed `CreateTexture` wrapper's complete 5-call-site graph also does not resolve to these offsets — §23.11. |

### 23.2 The pass-role-selection mechanism, traced in full — CONFIRMED

`rl_static_material`'s technique-select body, `FUN_00e5e530` (already cited at a summary level in §22.4), was fully decompiled and instruction-stepped this pass. Its role-number resolution (the value ultimately passed to `FUN_00e4a180`'s `[materialCategory][tierA][tierB]` table lookup, §22.4) works as follows, evaluated in this exact order:

1. **Explicit per-material override.** If the `rl_static_material` instance's own `+0x8` field is not the sentinel `-1`, that value is used directly as the role number (bypassing every flag below), and the sibling `+0x4` field supplies a second lookup argument. If the resulting role is specifically `4` or `5`, an additional capability gate (`FUN_00e4a070`) is consulted and can suppress a dependent lighting flag if it fails. **This is the mechanism by which any caller can force a specific pass-role for a given material/draw**, independent of the ambient render-context state described below.
2. **Absent an override**, the code reads a byte at fixed offset `+0x4fc` on the thread-local "current render object" (reached via the standard `FS:[0x2c]`→TLS-index→`+0x674` pointer this document already uses throughout §3.7/§16–§21). **If set, the role is unconditionally `2`** — checked before any of the flags below, so a role-2 request always wins.
3. Otherwise, a byte at `+0x4f7` is checked: **if set, the role is `4` or `5`**, chosen by a second byte at `+0x4f9` (`4` if clear, `5` if set).
4. Otherwise, a byte at `+0x4f8` is checked: **if set, the role is `6` or `7`**, chosen by the return value of a helper call, `FUN_00e59eb0()` (`6` if it returns zero, `7` otherwise; not decompiled further this pass).
5. **If none of the above apply, the role defaults to `0`.** For the traced car-paint shader (`ir_sr3carpaint_gr_v.fxo_pc`, §20.12), role `0` is *not* one of the five declared passes (its own T8 role-index table maps role 0 to `-1`, unresolved, §20.12.1) — meaning this "no flag set" default state, if it were ever reached during ordinary vehicle rendering, would fail to resolve a technique for this shader entirely. The practical reading (**HYPOTHESIS**, not traced further) is that one of `+0x4f7`/`+0x4f8` is always set by the time any real per-object draw reaches this function during normal gameplay rendering — i.e. the engine's own per-frame driver always brackets ordinary scene draws inside one of the two dedicated pass-executor functions identified below (§23.3), so the "no pass active" default is reached only in some other, non-mainline context (an editor/tool build, a thumbnail-render path, or similar) that this pass did not identify.

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly, full decompile and instruction-level read of `FUN_00e5e530`'s role-resolution logic, `tools/scratchpad/Frame1.java`.]** This is, for the first time, a complete answer to the task's own question 2: the render-context "pass-role" state is not a single integer but four independent byte flags plus one explicit per-material override field, checked in a fixed priority order.

### 23.3 The flag setters — who puts the render context into "role 4/5" or "role 6/7" mode

A whole-binary scan (`tools/scratchpad/Frame2.java`) for every byte-sized store to offsets `0x4f5`–`0x4fe` on any register (the same idiom-search discipline this document has used throughout, e.g. §18.2/§20.2) returned only 53 hits, concentrated in a small number of functions — a genuinely selective result, not a coincidental-offset situation of the kind `WALLS.md` warns about, because (unlike the earlier refuted `SetRenderTarget`-offset cases) every hit here was individually traced to a real, substantive bracket around a real pass-execution call, not a bare offset match on an unrelated class. Four setters map directly onto §23.2's four flags:

- **`+0x4f7` (role 4/5) — `FUN_00e70380`** (892 bytes; the *same* function §12.1/§20.7 already fully examined and correctly refuted as a `SetRenderTarget` call — its own `+0x94` vtable slot coincidentally matched that offset, but the function's real job, established only this pass, is running the entire role-4/5 pass). Its sole non-virtual caller is `FUN_00490670` (`0x004906fe`); it is also referenced once more as DATA at `0x0125bf84` (its own class's vtable, a small "per-viewport/pass-setup" object distinct from the scene-renderer class of §23.4 — not further identified). Body, in order: a before/after `FUN_00dad740(1000000)` timer bracket (the identical microsecond-timer idiom §19.1 already used for `Present`); a skip check on a per-object flag (`+0x2d5`); when not skipped, three calls into `rl_renderer`'s own `+0xc0` sub-object (vtable `+0x14`, literal arguments `6`/`7`/`8` — texture-stage invalidation); several more virtual calls on its own vtable (`+0x94`, `+0x48`, `+0x44`, `+0x50`, `+0x4c`); then the pass itself: **set `+0x4f7 = 1`, make one virtual call on its own vtable `+0x54` (argument `0`), set `+0x4f7 = 0`** — i.e. the *entire* role-4/5 pass, as executed from this function, is a single virtual call bracketed by the flag. This call is the most direct candidate for "traverse the visible list and draw everything queued for this pass" (not itself opened this pass). The function closes with the same frame-time-smoothing math already documented for `Present`'s own timer (`DAT_0351f8f8+0x50` sub-object).
- **`+0x4f9` (role-4-vs-5 sub-select) — `FUN_00e70970`** (312 bytes; two callers, `FUN_00e70d10`@`0x00e70d25` and `FUN_00e70ab0`@`0x00e70ccb`, gated on a global byte `DAT_0132e6ff`). Sets `+0x4f9 = 1`, profiles via `DAT_0351f8f8`'s vtable `+0x48` (marker `0x45`), conditionally brackets `DAT_0351f8f8`'s own vtable `+0x24`/`+0x28` (the already-documented generic scope/profiling bracket, §16.1/§16.2), calls `FUN_00e70700` (the real per-item work, not opened this pass), then clears `+0x4f9 = 0`. Because §23.2 only consults `+0x4f9` when `+0x4f7` is *also* set, this function most plausibly runs *nested inside* the role-4/5 bracket above, selecting the CTAB-confirmed role-5 stipple/damage-crossfade variant (§20.12.1/§20.12.2) for a specific object sub-list rather than the whole pass — **HYPOTHESIS**, the nesting itself was not directly re-confirmed by instruction-level trace this pass.
- **`+0x4f8` (role 6/7, the main lighting pass) — `FUN_004942d0`** (358 bytes; sole caller `FUN_00494440`@`0x0049445d`, itself reached only virtually, §23.4). After post-process/depth-of-field setup (`FUN_004941b0`, `FUN_00494280` — the same `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` sub-chain §20.11 already traced to the permanently-sentinel `FUN_004A9420`), it **saves the current `+0x4f8` value, sets it to `1`, calls `FUN_00e686b0(1)`, then restores the saved value** (a proper nested save/restore, not a hardcoded clear — consistent with this pass genuinely being able to nest inside another).
- **`+0x4fc` (role 2) — `FUN_00e48200`** (32 bytes: `SetFlag(this, bool)` — if the value is actually changing, flushes `rl_static_material`'s global registry via `FUN_00e3f190`, §22.2, first). Unlike the three setters above, this one is called from **over twenty structurally unrelated sites** across the binary — the confirmed material-bind command-buffer producers (`FUN_00478490`), the lighting-pass driver itself (`FUN_00e686b0`, `FUN_00e68570`, `FUN_00e68380`), post-process setup (`FUN_00494620`, `FUN_00498070`), and at least two entirely separate, unidentified modules (`0xfcd610`, `0xb8afb0`/`0xb8b340`). This is too wide a caller set to be a single "enter role-2 pass" trigger; it reads far more like a generic, widely-shared per-draw-site override toggle (plausibly a wireframe/debug/two-sided-material flag) than a frame-level pass boundary — **flagged OPEN**, not claimed as a pass-orchestration finding in the same sense as the other three.

**Evidence anchors:** `FUN_00e5e530` (role resolution); setters `FUN_00e70380`, `FUN_00e70970`, `FUN_004942d0`, `FUN_00e48200`; callers `FUN_00490670`, `FUN_00e70d10`, `FUN_00e70ab0`, `FUN_00494440`; scan script `tools/scratchpad/Frame2.java`; per-function decompiles `tools/scratchpad/Frame3.java`.

### 23.4 Frame pass order — HIGH CONFIDENCE, not fully closed

`FUN_00490670` (the `+0x4f7`/role-4-5 phase driver, §23.3) and `FUN_00494440` (the top of the already-partially-documented `+0x4f8`/role-6-7 chain, §20.11) each have **exactly one reference anywhere in the binary, and it is a DATA reference, not a call** — `0x012A1444` and `0x012A146C` respectively (`tools/scratchpad/Frame10.java`). Both addresses land squarely inside `rl_d3d_scene_renderer`'s own vtable, whose base (`0x012A142C`) was already independently RTTI-identified in §20.11: `0x012A1444 − 0x012A142C = 0x18`, `0x012A146C − 0x012A142C = 0x40`. **Both phase-entry functions are virtual methods of the same RTTI-confirmed class, at slots `+0x18` and `+0x40` respectively** — not a coincidental offset match (the base is independently anchored by RTTI, per §20.11, and both slots hold large, substantive, previously-undecompiled function bodies, not shared stub addresses of the kind §18.10/§21 warn about). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + the same RTTI anchor already established in §20.11.]**

A full 48-slot dump of this vtable (`0x012A142C`–`0x012A14E7`, `tools/scratchpad/Frame11.java`; the class's own mangled-name string begins immediately after, at `+0xc0`, confirming the vtable's real extent) resolves several more slots to already-named functions from this pass and earlier ones:

| Slot | Function | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `+0x0` | `FUN_00490030` | Destructor (per §20.11's own citation of the `FUN_00490030`/`FUN_00490060` pair) |
| `+0x10` | `FUN_00490060` | Sole caller of `FUN_00e6ce70` (§23.6) — most likely this class's own constructor/init step, not the destructor previously guessed |
| `+0x18` | `FUN_00490670` | Role-4/5 phase driver (§23.3) |
| `+0x1c` | `FUN_004924d0` | Reads state-slot offsets `0x26d0`/`0x26dc` (§23.5); not decompiled |
| `+0x30` | `FUN_00493ab0` | Also calls `FUN_00490420` (§23.5's MRT-bind function) directly |
| `+0x34` | `FUN_00493c50` | Also calls `FUN_00490420` directly |
| `+0x40` | `FUN_00494440` | Top of the role-6/7 (`FUN_004942d0`→`FUN_00e686b0`) chain |
| `+0xa8` | `FUN_00e70ab0` | One of the two callers of `FUN_00e70970` (the `+0x4f9`/role-5 sub-selector, §23.3) |
| `+0xb8` | `FUN_00494620` | Also a caller of `FUN_00e48200` (role-2 toggle) and a reader of the same `0x2618`/`0x2624` state slot §23.5 uses |

**This establishes that essentially every pass-related function this document has traced this session — the role-4/5 driver, the role-5 sub-variant selector's own caller, the role-6/7 chain's top, and the MRT-bind helper's two other direct callers — are all virtual methods of one single class**, strongly supporting the idea of a single per-frame "run the scene" driver that owns every pass as one of its own phases. **What was NOT found this pass: the actual top-level function that calls through these vtable slots in sequence** (both `+0x18` and `+0x40` are reached ONLY via their own vtable slot, with no ordinary call site anywhere) — the same "reached only virtually, top-of-chain caller not identified" gap already on record for `FUN_00b17000`/`FUN_00b13850` (§18.6/§18.8/§21.1). **Vtable slot ORDER (`+0x18` before `+0x40` before `+0xa8`) is offered only as suggestive, not as confirmed execution order** — a hand-written "run the frame" method calling specific named phases by their declared slot is not guaranteed to call them in ascending offset order, and this pass did not find that outer driver to check directly. **[HIGH CONFIDENCE for the shared-class structure; OPEN for the actual per-frame call sequence and its own top-level driver.]**

### 23.5 The G-buffer/MRT hypothesis for the cheap pass (role 4/5) — CONFIRMED mechanism, HYPOTHESIS semantics

This directly answers the task's own suggested angle of attack. `FUN_00490670` (§23.3/§23.4), immediately before running the role-4/5 pass, calls **`FUN_00490420`** (582 bytes; 4 callers total: `FUN_00490670`, and directly from vtable slots `+0x30`/`+0x34` — `FUN_00493ab0`/`FUN_00493c50` — plus one more site inside the latter two). `FUN_00490420`'s body makes exactly four surface-bind calls, each sourced from a fixed, named offset of `rl_renderer`'s own `+0xc0` sub-object (the same sub-object §20.3(c) already used for shadow-cascade setup, at neighbouring offsets `0x26d0`/`0x26dc`/`0x2a68`/`0x2a74` — this pass's own finding, below, shows this sub-object is a general-purpose named-render-target registry, not shadow-specific as §20.3(c) had guessed):

1. `FUN_0047a680(0, targetSlot[0x2618].surface, targetSlot[0x2618].flag)` — bind stage/target index **0**.
2. `FUN_0047a680(1, targetSlot[0x2788].surface, targetSlot[0x2788].flag)` — bind stage/target index **1**.
3. `FUN_0047a680(2, targetSlot[0x2840].surface, targetSlot[0x2840].flag)` — bind stage/target index **2**.
4. `currentRenderObject+0x77c = targetSlot[0x26d0].surface`, then `FUN_004755b0()` — a **fourth surface, bound through a genuinely different opcode** (see below).
5. Two viewport-scale calls (`FUN_00ea4d60`/`FUN_00ea2560` pairs), deriving width/height from a camera/viewport sub-object's own fields — consistent with the bound targets having a different resolution than the primary back buffer.

**`FUN_0047a680`** (`bindStage(stage, surfaceObj, flag)`, 225 bytes, decompiled in full) is the single, shared, 119-call-site helper used everywhere in the renderer to attach a numbered stage's surface — and its own body **unconditionally** routes through `FUN_00474f20`→**`FUN_00475540`**. `FUN_00475540` writes the bare-register opcode literal **`9`**, takes exactly **2** arguments, and matches the exact reserve/write/advance/thread-notify idiom this document has used throughout (§18.2/§20.2) to identify every other confirmed `Record*` producer — i.e. **`FUN_00475540` is `RecordSetRenderTarget`**, and opcode `9` is independently already the confirmed replay-side `SetRenderTarget` handler (`0x0049CB00`, §7/§12). The fourth surface goes through **`FUN_004755b0`**, already identified in §18.2's own producer table as writing opcode `0xA` (10) with exactly 1 argument — the natural pairing for `IDirect3DDevice9::SetDepthStencilSurface`, a single-argument sibling call to `SetRenderTarget` in the real public API. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly: the opcode value, the argument count, and independent confirmation of opcode 9's own replay-side identity all agree, the same evidentiary bar `WALLS.md` requires to avoid the project's own repeated coincidental-offset failure mode — this is not a numeric coincidence.]**

**Conclusion for the task's own hypothesis: immediately before every execution of the cheap role-4/5 pass, this engine binds exactly 3 color render targets (stage indices 0, 1, 2) plus 1 depth-stencil surface, via a disassembly-confirmed `SetRenderTarget`/`SetDepthStencilSurface` producer chain — a genuine, real multi-render-target bind, not a guess.** Whether these 3 targets specifically hold normal/depth/albedo data (the classic deferred G-buffer layout) is **HYPOTHESIS, not confirmed**: this pass did not trace the *writers* of the four named target slots (`0x2618`/`0x2788`/`0x2840`/`0x26d0`) back to their own `CreateTexture`/`CreateRenderTarget` calls to read off pixel formats. Four candidate writer functions were found but not opened (`FUN_009dd7b0`, `FUN_009de970`, `FUN_009ed6f0`, `FUN_009ee0d0` — each touches one of these same offsets with a float/register store, consistent with format/size setup at device-creation or device-reset time, `tools/scratchpad/Frame8.java`). **This is the natural next step for a future pass**, and would settle the G-buffer semantics question definitively. **Update, 2026-09-29: this step was taken — see §23.11. The four candidates were opened and REFUTED as format writers (three are coincidental offset collisions with unrelated classes, one is a blanket-zeroing constructor); a further forward trace from the confirmed `CreateTexture` wrapper's complete call graph also did not resolve to these offsets. The pixel format of the three-colour+depth target set remains genuinely open on this project's own disassembly side.**

**Refinement, 2026-09-29 — corrected same day after a real, exhaustive, population-level data check contradicted this refinement's own first version (kept struck through, not deleted, per project practice).** ~~The most likely real content of the 3 bound color targets is Depth-adjacent/DSF-data and Normals — NOT Lighting, which the real evidence places in a separately-populated buffer.~~ **This overreached from a summary document without the population data to back it, and a peer team's exhaustive data-side CTAB scan (844/844 real `.fxo_pc` files, no gaps) found the opposite of what it predicted:** all 13 real `ir_light_*` shaders (§23.6) declare exactly three PS input samplers, at fixed registers — `IR_GBuffer_LightingSampler`@s12, `IR_GBuffer_NormalsSampler`@s13, `IR_GBuffer_DepthSampler`@s14 — i.e. Lighting IS one of the three named G-buffer concepts real light-drawing shaders read from, register-for-register, matching the plain "Lighting/Normals/Depth" reading this refinement had argued against. **Separately, `IR_GBuffer_DSF_DataSampler` is real, common (675/844 shaders, 80% of the whole library, 1,329/1,329 exact blob-level hits, always at sampler register s9, exactly matching the traced car-paint shader's own s9 usage, §20.12.2) — but it is declared by NONE of the 13 light shaders.** It is a forward-material-pass input (decals, foliage, glass, vehicle paint, `rl_ssao_*`/`rl_ground_reflect_*`), not a light-shader input — explaining why no engine-side string exists for it (§23.8): the name only ever lives inside the compiled shader's own CTAB.

**The genuine open reconciliation above — was `IR_GBuffer_LightingSampler` a prepass-written PARAMETER buffer or the final accumulated-light output — is now resolved in favour of the parameter-buffer reading, via real bytecode evidence (a peer team, disassembling all 3 structurally distinct `ir_light_*` variants — directional/point/local_ambient — 2026-09-29).** In every one of the 3 real light-shader blobs checked, the value sampled from `IR_GBuffer_LightingSampler` is never added or blended wholesale into the pixel's output the way reading back a final accumulated light color would be — instead its components are individually unpacked (scale+bias `MAD` against named constants) and fed through `POW` (specular-exponent-shaped), `NRM` (normalize — meaningful on a direction/parameter vector, meaningless on a light color), `RCP`, and `DP3`, before the actual lit output is built from further downstream math. This is real, consistent, cross-shader bytecode evidence for **`IR_GBuffer_LightingSampler` being a prepass-written material/lighting-PARAMETER buffer (baked specular/roughness/emissive-shaped terms), not the final light-accumulation target** — meaning this section's role-4/5 prepass writing "Lighting" alongside Depth/Normals is entirely consistent with its own real content, just not literally an accumulated-light buffer. **[CONFIRMED — real disassembled bytecode, 3 structurally distinct real shaders, consistent pattern across all 3; exact per-channel semantics (what x/y/z individually decode to) not traced, honestly left OPEN.]** The still-open writer-function check below (now further sharpened toward expecting a packed/compact parameter-shaped format, not a wide HDR accumulation format) remains the concrete next step for the disassembly side specifically.

Separately, `FUN_00490670`'s own OUTER structure — cache the current stage-0/1/2/depth bindings on entry, call `FUN_00490420` to bind the pass-specific targets, run the pass, then explicitly RESTORE the cached bindings via three more `FUN_0047a680` calls afterward — is itself strong, independent confirmation that this is a genuine nested sub-pass with its own private target set, distinct from whatever is bound before and after it (most plausibly the main back-buffer/primary scene targets used by the role-6/7 lighting pass). This is consistent with, and reinforces, the deferred-G-buffer-then-forward-lighting architecture the task asked about, even though the exact contents of the 3 targets remain unconfirmed.

**Evidence anchors:** `FUN_00490670`, `FUN_00490420`, `FUN_0047a680`, `FUN_00474f20`, `FUN_00475540` (`RecordSetRenderTarget`, opcode 9), `FUN_004755b0` (opcode 0xA); named target-registry offsets `0x2618`/`0x2624`, `0x2788`/`0x2794`, `0x2840`/`0x284c`, `0x26d0`/`0x26dc` on `rl_renderer`'s `+0xc0` sub-object; additional readers `FUN_00493ab0`, `FUN_00493c50`, `FUN_00494620`, `FUN_004ac1e0`, `FUN_00498900`, `FUN_004a9f10`, `FUN_004aa2d0`; unopened candidate writers `FUN_009dd7b0`, `FUN_009de970`, `FUN_009ed6f0`, `FUN_009ee0d0`. Scripts: `tools/scratchpad/Frame4.java`/`Frame7.java`/`Frame8.java`.

### 23.6 The light list — register resolution CONFIRMED once-at-init; the real per-frame iteration mechanism now CONFIRMED too, 2026-09-29; the PS-side lighting-math upload producer ALSO FOUND, 2026-09-29 (§23.6.2)

`FUN_00e6ce70` (2,837 bytes, fully decompiled; sole caller `FUN_00490060`@`0x00490064` — vtable slot `+0x10` of the same `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` class, §23.4, most plausibly this class's own construction-time init step) is **not** the per-frame light-list builder the task asked about — it is a **one-time CTAB register-resolution cache builder**, run once per `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` instance, for **13 distinct light-shader technique variants**: `ir_light_point`, `ir_light_point_tex`, `ir_light_spot`, `ir_light_spot_noshadows`, `ir_light_spot_tex`, `ir_light_spot_tex_noshadows`, `ir_light_directional`, `ir_light_directional_direct_only`, `ir_light_directional_amb_only`, `ir_light_tube`, `ir_light_tube_tex`, `ir_light_local_ambient`, `ir_light_overdraw_debug`. For each, obtained by name via `FUN_00e4a730` (the same class-handle-by-name lookup §20.12.4 already used for `"rl_vis_query"`), it resolves — via `FUN_00e49990`, the identical name-hash-to-register mechanism §20.12.4 traced for `World_xform`/`Scale`/`Offset`/`Camera_velocity` — the register numbers for `ir_light_pos`, `ir_light_color`, `ir_light_back_color` (all 13 variants), plus, per variant, `ir_shadow_map`/`ir_ambient_occlusion` (the point/spot-with-shadow variants) or `projection_texture` (the directional projector variant), caching all of it in a 13-entry, `0x58`-byte-stride array at this class's own `+0x4d09c` field. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, full decompile, `tools/scratchpad/Frame5.java`.]**

This precisely answers, and sharpens, §20.12.7's own looser phrasing ("dynamic per-light setup... registered from a function called `FUN_00e6ce70`") — the register RESOLUTION is fully traced and confirmed, but the function never touches per-light position/color VALUES, and does not run per-frame. A candidate worth checking in a future pass was `FUN_0049BDB0` (§20.3b, already confirmed to upload per-light data at fixed LITERAL registers `0x14`–`0x17`/`0x1b`, not a name-resolved register) — flagged then as possibly a *separate, simpler* direct-light path unconnected to this `ir_light_*` shader family entirely, given the register-source mismatch (literal vs. name-resolved). **This mismatch is resolved below, 2026-09-29.**

**Reframing, 2026-09-29, from real runtime evidence (an external runtime capture, dirty-side; findings summarized here, the source is not for clean-side consultation — §§2–3 of that source, verified directly against source).** Real disassembly of the `ir_light_*` shader family's own bytecode (a genuinely separate, independent method from anything on this project's own disassembly side) confirms these are deferred **light-volume** shaders — one draw call per light, each shader computing real per-light attenuation/cone-angle math from `IR_Light_Pos`/`IR_Light_Color`/`IR_Light_Info`/`IR_Spot_Info` and writing into the low-res light buffer §23.5 above discusses. Per that same document's own register table: `ir_light_point`/`ir_light_point_tex` declare `IR_Light_Pos` at pixel-shader `c0`, `IR_Light_Color` at `c3`, `IR_Light_Info` at `c4`; `ir_light_spot*` declares the same names at `c0`/`c1`(`IR_Light_Dir`)/`c13`/`c14`, plus `IR_Spot_Info` at `c15`; `ir_light_directional*` declares `IR_Light_Pos`(as a direction)/`IR_Light_Color` at `c0`/`c2`. **These are all confirmed real PIXEL-shader constants** (the disassembled ASM uses `_pp`-suffixed pixel-shader-only opcodes throughout, `mad_pp`/`rcp_pp`/`pow_pp`/`dp3_pp`) — matching exactly the shape `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own per-variant cache resolves register numbers for. **This means "the per-frame light-list builder" this section was searching for may not exist in the form assumed (a flat array of light data uploaded once and read by every object's shader) — the real per-frame mechanism is more likely a loop that iterates active lights and issues one `DrawPrimitive`-shaped draw call per light, using the matching `ir_light_*` variant, into the light buffer,** with `FUN_00e6ce70`'s already-confirmed per-technique register cache (this section, above) existing specifically to support that per-light draw loop efficiently. **[HIGH CONFIDENCE — inferred, at the time it was written. The follow-up below confirms the iteration-loop half directly.]**

### 23.6.1 Follow-up, 2026-09-29: the real per-frame iteration mechanism found — lights are ordinary entries in the SAME generic visible-item submission loop every mesh draw already uses

**Scope.** Direct follow-up requested specifically to (1) fully decompile `FUN_0049BDB0`, (2) find its real callers and trace upward to whatever loop iterates the scene's actual light data, and (3) reconcile the literal-vs-name-resolved register mismatch. Method: the same disposable Ghidra project already used for §23 (`tools/gp_render9`), scripts `tools/scratchpad/LightList1.java`–`LightList4.java`.

**`FUN_0049BDB0`, fully decompiled (440 bytes, confirming and completing §20.3(b)'s earlier summary-level citation).** Given a light-record pointer, it uploads, via the confirmed single-Vector4 `RecordSetVertexShaderConstantF` producer (`FUN_00475BE0`, §20.2 — the VERTEX-shader constant producer specifically, never the pixel-shader one) at four consecutive literal registers plus one more:
- register **`0x14`**: the light record's own position, read directly from its own `+0x14`/`+0x18`/`+0x1c` fields (w=0).
- registers **`0x15`/`0x16`/`0x17`**: a pre-stored 9-float block read verbatim from the light record's own `+0x2c` through `+0x50` fields, via a trivial 9-field copy helper (`FUN_00da4130`, fully decompiled this pass: it does no computation of its own, only a field-by-field copy — so whatever 3×3 orientation basis this represents is built and maintained elsewhere in the light object's own per-frame update, not inside this function), split into three w=0 Vector4s.
- register **`0x1b`**: the light record's own base colour (`+0x20`/`+0x24`/`+0x28`) multiplied component-wise by an intensity scalar at `+0x54`.

**A second, sibling upload function, `FUN_0049bb40` (623 bytes, not previously named or decompiled in this document), was found and fully decompiled this pass** — reached through the identical gating pattern described below, and filling out the *rest* of the same literal register block:
- register **`0x18`**: the reciprocals of three further light-record fields at `+0xbc`/`+0xc0`/`+0xc4` (most plausibly the light volume's own half-extents or radius terms — exactly the shape a vertex shader needs to un-scale a unit sphere/cone/tube mesh into the light's real size), plus a fourth component from an unrelated helper call (`FUN_00e784b0`, not traced further this pass).
- register **`0x19`**: a second colour-shaped quadruple (`+0x90`/`+0x94`/`+0x98`/`+0x9c`), each component scaled by the product of two further scalar fields (`+0x9c` again, and `+0xa0`) — structurally a second, independently-scaled tint value. This lines up suggestively with the `ir_light_back_color` name `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own cache resolves a register for (this section, above) — flagged as a plausible match, not confirmed as the same quantity.
- register **`0x1a`**: two independent linear scale-bias pairs, each of the classic `(1/(hi−lo), −lo/(hi−lo))` shape, computed from two further field pairs (`+0xc8`/`+0xcc` and `+0xd0`/`+0xd4`) — algebraically the same shape the real `ir_light_*` pixel shader's own `IR_Spot_Info`/`IR_Light_Info` falloff math uses (§23.6 above, citing `shader-map.md` §2's own disassembled ASM). Flagged as a suggestive structural echo, not confirmed to be the same value rather than an independently-computed vertex-shader-side quantity.

**Together, `FUN_0049BDB0` and `FUN_0049bb40` fully account for eight consecutive literal vertex-shader constant registers, `0x14` through `0x1b`**, all sourced from the same light-record layout — a single, tightly-packed, engine-reserved "current light" VS constant block, exactly as §20.3(b)'s original citation predicted, now with all eight registers' real field sources identified. **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, both functions fully decompiled.]**

**The gating mechanism, and why it is genuine per-frame execution rather than dead code.** Both upload functions sit behind trivial tail-call wrapper functions, each gated on the identical test — a light-record field at `+0x70` equal to zero:
- `FUN_00e78220` (24 bytes; raw disassembly confirms a genuine tail call — `MOV EAX,[ESP+4]` / `TEST` / `CMP [EAX+0x70],0` / `JMP FUN_0049BDB0`, the record pointer left in the same register the callee expects — not a decompiler artefact, which is why the pseudocode view alone appeared to call `FUN_0049BDB0` with no visible argument) → `FUN_0049BDB0`.
- `FUN_00e78200` (24 bytes, byte-for-byte the same shape) → `FUN_0049bb40`.

**Both wrapper calls happen, unconditionally for any item whose `+0x70` field is zero, inside the exact same real per-frame, per-visible-item submission loop this document already traced and closed for ordinary mesh draws in §16.2/§18.5/§18.6 — not a separate, light-specific loop.** Three structurally near-identical sibling functions were found and fully decompiled this pass, all sharing one calling convention (a `count`, a pointer to an array of per-item pointers, a pass-type flag, and a gate-flags byte) and one loop body shape:

| Function | Size | Callers found this pass |
|---|---|---|
| `FUN_00e74880` | 1,695 B | `FUN_00e58b70` (already documented, §16.2) + one vtable DATA reference (`0x0125c504`) |
| `FUN_00e3de80` | 1,797 B | two call sites, neither inside a named Ghidra function — reached only from unresolved/virtual code |
| `FUN_00e45eb0` | 1,597 B | **four** vtable DATA references (`0x01124e6c`, `0x01124fb4`, `0x0125ab9c`, `0x0125c45c`) — a shared vtable-slot override across at least four sibling classes; two of the four (`0x0125ab9c`/`0x0125c45c`) land near the already-known `rl_scene`/third-sibling vtable bases (§16.2), the other two (`0x01124e6c`/`0x01124fb4`) are a genuinely new, not-yet-named sibling-class pair, not investigated further this pass |

Each of the three walks its item array from index `0` to `count`, and for every item whose visibility gate passes: resolves the item's own technique via a virtual call on the item's own vtable `+0x10` (cached and compared to decide whether a technique rebind, `FUN_00e3f740`, is needed); calls `FUN_00e78200` then `FUN_00e78220`, **in that exact order, unconditionally, for every single item** — the light-constant uploads above fire purely on the item's own `+0x70` field, with no separate "is this a light" branch anywhere in the loop itself, the type check lives entirely inside the two thin wrapper functions; and then, for the main (non-shadow) pass specifically, loops over the item's own resolved draw-range count and issues, per range, the identical `(vtable+0x80)` / `DAT_0351f8f8` `vtable+0x10` call pair §18.5 already confirmed sits directly upstream of a real, disassembly-confirmed `DrawIndexedPrimitive` command-buffer write (§18.6). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, all three sibling functions fully decompiled, `tools/scratchpad/LightList2_out.txt`.]**

**This directly answers the task's central question: there is no separate "light list" data structure or dedicated light-iteration loop at all.** Lights are simply entries in the same generic per-frame visible-item array that every other renderable object in this engine is submitted through. A single per-item field (`+0x70`) distinguishes a light-type item from an ordinary mesh item; for a light item, the same submission loop that would otherwise (or additionally) bind a mesh's material and walk its draw ranges also uploads this fixed 8-register VS block of position/orientation/extent/colour data, immediately before falling through into the identical per-range draw-submission code that produces a real `DrawIndexedPrimitive` call for every other item kind — presumably drawing the light's own volume mesh (sphere/cone/tube) using whichever `ir_light_*` technique the item's own material/technique fields resolve to, via the same `rl_static_material`/`FUN_00e5e530` role-select machinery already documented in §23.2. This is now load-bearing, disassembly-confirmed evidence for the "one draw call per light, using the matching `ir_light_*` variant" reading this section already reached from the shader-bytecode side alone (§23.6's own reframing paragraph, above).

**This also resolves the specific register-source mismatch flagged as "noticed but not reconciled."** `FUN_0049BDB0`/`FUN_0049bb40` write literal, fixed VERTEX-shader constant registers (`0x14`–`0x1b`); `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own cache resolves, by name, PIXEL-shader constant registers for `ir_light_pos`/`ir_light_color`/`ir_light_back_color`. These are two non-overlapping D3D9 register files (VS constants and PS constants are entirely separate address spaces), and the two mechanisms have different reasons to vary at all: the PS register numbers genuinely differ per technique variant because of per-shader compiler register allocation (confirmed real CTAB data, §23.6 above — e.g. `ir_light_point`'s `IR_Light_Color` at PS `c3` vs. `ir_light_spot`'s at PS `c13`), while the VS block sits at the same eight literal registers for every variant, consistent with being consumed by a single, shared light-volume-mesh vertex shader rather than by the per-variant pixel shader's own lighting math at all. **The two were never expected to be the same registers, and are not — this is not a discrepancy needing reconciliation, but two different upload steps, for two different consumers (a shared VS vs. a per-variant PS), of the same underlying light record.**

~~**Honestly still open, narrower than before.** The actual producer that uploads the real per-light values into the PIXEL-shader registers `FUN_00e6ce70` resolves by name (`IR_Light_Pos`/`IR_Light_Color`/`IR_Light_Info`/`IR_Spot_Info`, confirmed real PS constants by independent shader-bytecode disassembly, an external runtime capture — dirty-side, findings summarized here, the source is not for clean-side consultation) was **not found this pass** — neither of the two PS-constant producer functions' (`FUN_00475D80`/`FUN_00475E10`, §20.2) already-enumerated callers (§20.9) nor the `FUN_00e78200`/`FUN_00e78220` gate pair leads to one, and no third sibling wrapper analogous to those two (testing the same `+0x70` field and tail-calling into a PS-constant producer) was found inside the three submission loops. Register `0x1a`'s scale-bias shape (above) is a suggestive structural echo of the real PS-side `IR_Spot_Info`/`IR_Light_Info` falloff math, but this pass could not confirm it is the same value rather than an independently-computed VS-side quantity feeding some other purpose (e.g. the volume mesh's own vertex placement along a cone axis). This is now a much narrower, well-anchored open item than the original task framing left it — the iteration mechanism, the gating, and the full 8-register VS-side payload are all CONFIRMED; only the PS-side lighting-math upload's own producer function remains unlocated. Reported honestly OPEN rather than guessed at, per this project's standing policy on not inventing an explanation once confirmable facts run out (`HANDOFF.md` §27.8).~~ **FOUND, same day, direct follow-up — see §23.6.2 immediately below.** The search inside the three submission loops themselves was correctly exhausted (see §23.6.2's own negative sweep, which independently re-confirms this paragraph's own checks and several more besides) — the real producer sits in a genuinely separate per-frame phase, not inside these loops at all, which is why it was not found here.

**Evidence anchors, this follow-up:** `FUN_0049BDB0` (440 B, per-light VS uploader, registers `0x14`–`0x17`/`0x1b`), `FUN_0049bb40` (623 B, sibling VS uploader, registers `0x18`/`0x19`/`0x1a`), `FUN_00da4130` (9-field copy helper, not a computation), `FUN_00e78220`/`FUN_00e78200` (the two `+0x70`-gated tail-call wrappers), `FUN_00e74880`/`FUN_00e3de80`/`FUN_00e45eb0` (the three sibling per-visible-item submission loops), `FUN_00e3f740` (technique-id rebind, confirms the item's own vtable`+0x10` return value is a real, cached technique selector), `FUN_00e79080` (the shared per-item bounds/frustum test all three loops call). New sibling-class vtable-DATA sites for `FUN_00e45eb0`: `0x01124e6c`, `0x01124fb4` (unidentified, new), `0x0125ab9c`, `0x0125c45c` (near the already-known §16.2 family). Scripts: `tools/scratchpad/LightList1.java`–`LightList4.java` and their `_out.txt` logs.

### 23.6.2 Direct follow-up, same day, 2026-09-29: the PS-side `IR_Light_*` value producer FOUND — a genuinely separate per-frame phase, not the generic submission loop

**Scope.** Direct follow-up on §23.6.1's own remaining open item, using the same disposable Ghidra project (`tools/gp_render9`), scripts `tools/scratchpad/LightList5.java`–`LightList16.java`. Per the task's own concrete suggestions: (1) the two `+0x70`-gated wrapper functions' full raw bodies were re-checked instruction-by-instruction for a third gated call; (2) every function that receives the light-record pointer inside the three submission loops was enumerated and checked; (3) the technique-rebind function was fully decompiled to check whether it also triggers a PS upload; and, when all three came back negative, a fourth angle — tracing the ONE OTHER reader anywhere in the binary of `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own `+0x4d09c` cache array — found the real answer.

**Negative sweep, all leads from the task brief, done first (each independently confirmed, none reopens anything already closed above):**
- **The wrapper bodies themselves.** `FUN_00e78200`'s full raw disassembly was pulled for the first time (previously only decompiled) and is, instruction-for-instruction, the byte-for-byte same 24-byte shape already confirmed for `FUN_00e78220` in §23.6.1: one null check, one `+0x70` comparison, one tail `JMP`. Neither wrapper has room for, or contains, a third gated call. **[CONFIRMED — raw disassembly, both wrappers.]**
- **The three loop-body calls sitting immediately around the two wrapper calls in `FUN_00e74880`** (`FUN_004770c0` right before, `FUN_00476fc0` and `FUN_004859f0` right after) — never individually decompiled before this pass. All three are REFUTED as light-value producers: `FUN_004770c0`/`FUN_00476fc0` are both thin, TLS-debug-object-gated wrappers around the exact same "current-render-object vtable`+0x20`" debug-overlay bracket call the other two submission loops (`FUN_00e3de80`/`FUN_00e45eb0`) already make inline — pure diagnostics, never touching a light record. `FUN_004859f0` dispatches two literal-tagged calls (`0x12`, `0xd`) through the SUBMISSION LOOP'S OWN holder object's vtable`+0x44` (not the light record, not a shader-constant producer), using two fixed field addresses on the holder that are identical on every loop iteration — reissued redundantly per item, unrelated to per-light data. **[CONFIRMED — full decompile of all three, `tools/scratchpad/LightList5_out.txt`.]**
- **The technique-rebind function, `FUN_00e3f740`, fully decompiled.** It caches a technique id and, only when that id actually changes, makes exactly ONE virtual call on a technique-registry-resolved object, always with its second argument hard-coded to `0` at every call site inside the three submission loops — no light-record pointer is ever passed through it. REFUTED as a PS-upload trigger. **[CONFIRMED — full decompile, `tools/scratchpad/LightList3_out.txt`.]**
- **The shared per-item bounds test, `FUN_00e79080`, fully decompiled.** It computes a screen-space bounding-rectangle clamp and a coverage ratio for LOD/culling purposes; it makes zero shader-constant calls of any kind. It is, together with the two known VS uploaders and the item's own vtable`+0x10` technique-id getter, one of only FOUR functions anywhere in the three loop bodies that ever receives the light-record pointer — and the only one of those four not already accounted for. REFUTED. **[CONFIRMED — full decompile, `tools/scratchpad/LightList4_out.txt`.]**
- **The strongest previously-flagged-but-unopened lead, `FUN_00487460`** (§20.6's own "plausible 'bind this light's full render state and constants' function," never before decompiled) — fully decompiled and traced to its own installation site, a vtable at `0x012a0d64` sitting in a literal-name-string-anchored run of sibling classes that read, byte for byte, `rl_d3d_starfield` and `rl_d3d_meteor_shower` (a third, adjacent name reads as an `rl_d3d_*_texture`-shaped class). Its own body loads a texture literally named `"misc-static.tga"` and issues two `RecordDrawPrimitive` calls (a 5,000-point list, then a 2,000-primitive triangle list) — the shape of a weather/particle effect, not a deferred light volume. Its VS/PS single-Vector4 uploads (this section's own reason for flagging it) read BOTH the register number and the value from fields of this same weather-effect object; several of those field offsets (`+0x1c`/`+0x20`/`+0x24`/`+0x28`/`+0x2c`/`+0x30`) numerically coincide with the light record's own known layout (§23.6.1) purely by chance — the exact coincidental-offset failure mode `WALLS.md`'s first entry documents, not a real connection. **REFUTED.** A structural sibling function in the same weather-effect family, `FUN_00487A90` (also flagged in the same producer's caller census, never before opened), was checked too and shows the identical "misc-static.tga"-style pattern on a differently-shaped, unrelated small object (`+0x25c`/`+0x10` fields, no overlap with the light layout at all) — REFUTED for the same reason. Five further previously-unopened callers from §20.9's own PSCF caller census were also opened and checked directly against the light-record layout: two (`FUN_004847C0`, `FUN_00484910`) operate on `rl_renderer`'s own texture-slot pool (§23.11's `0xB8`-stride mechanism), one (`FUN_00484E60`) uploads a 16-entry skinning/animation-palette-shaped array, and one (`FUN_004935B0`) is a whole-frame fog/view-setup pass reading `rl_d3d_scene_renderer`'s own persistent `+0x4d07x` sub-offsets (the same class and offset family as §20.11's `FUN_004A9420`) — none takes a light-record pointer of any kind. **All seven REFUTED. [CONFIRMED — full decompile of all seven, `tools/scratchpad/LightList5_out.txt`–`LightList7_out.txt`.]**

**The real producer, found via a different anchor: the ONE OTHER reader, anywhere in the binary, of `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own `+0x4d09c` cache array.** `FUN_00e6ce70` (§23.6) writes its 13-entry, `0x58`-stride register cache at a fixed absolute sub-offset, `+0x4d09c`, of the `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` instance. An exhaustive whole-binary scan for every instruction anywhere referencing that exact literal offset found precisely **five** hits, all accounted for: three are `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own writes/reads of its own array, and the other two are both inside a single, previously undocumented function, **`FUN_00e92010`** — a small (44-byte) accessor that takes a light-KIND selector, looks it up in a second small table (at the same instance's `+0x4d4bc` field) to get the matching technique's index into `FUN_00e6ce70`'s 13-entry cache, and returns a pointer directly into that cache entry (after also forwarding its very first cached field — the `ir_light_pos` register slot, on the read order `FUN_00e6ce70` itself populates it in — to a small helper, `FUN_00e91f90`, that binds either the shadow-map or a null sampler to it depending on a quality flag). **This is the missing link between the once-at-init register cache and a real per-frame consumer — `FUN_00e92010` is called from three brand-new functions never previously named in this document, `FUN_00491dd0`, `FUN_004920a0`, and `FUN_00491d70`, none of which is reachable from, or related to, the three generic submission loops §23.6.1 traced.** **[CONFIRMED — exhaustive whole-binary literal scan + full decompile of `FUN_00e92010`, `tools/scratchpad/LightList11_out.txt`–`LightList12_out.txt`.]**

**The real per-frame lighting-pass driver: `rl_d3d_scene_renderer`'s own vtable `+0x1c` slot, `FUN_004924d0` — a genuinely separate phase from the generic per-item submission loops.** `FUN_004924d0` (2,231 bytes) is reached only from one DATA reference, `0x012a1448` — which is exactly `0x012A142C + 0x1c`, i.e. a slot on the SAME RTTI-anchored `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` vtable §20.11/§23.9 already document (sibling of the already-known `+0x18` role-4/5 G-buffer driver and `+0x40` post-process chain). Its body binds the three named G-buffer sampler stages (matching `IR_GBuffer_LightingSampler`/`NormalsSampler`/`DepthSampler`, §23.5/§23.8), computes depth-linearization constants from the current camera's near/far planes, then calls a per-light-kind dispatcher, **`FUN_004922d0`**, exactly THREE times with kind selectors `0`, `2`, and `1` — most plausibly the point/spot, directional/ambient, and a third light-kind group. **This directly closes half of §23.10's own "top-level per-frame render driver not found" gap, specifically for the lighting pass: it is `rl_d3d_scene_renderer::+0x1c`, a real, RTTI-anchored virtual method, not a guess.** **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, full decompile, `tools/scratchpad/LightList16_out.txt`.]**

**`FUN_004922d0` walks up to nine fixed-offset "kind, count, pointer-array" buckets per light-kind group — the real, separate light-iteration structure the original task asked about, distinct from the generic visible-item array.** For one light-kind group (a `0x9c`-byte-stride block on the scene-renderer instance, indexed by the `0`/`1`/`2` selector `FUN_004924d0` passes), it calls a shared per-bucket walker, **`FUN_00491d70`**, against seven fixed sub-offsets (`+0x118` through `+0x160`), a second walker shape, **`FUN_00491dd0`**, against two more (`+0x16c`/`+0x178`, gated by a render-state bracket and paired with a shadow-map-render helper, `FUN_00491ed0`, that renders the light's own shadow map before its constants are set), and a third, differently-shaped walker, **`FUN_004920a0`** (a flag-array scan rather than a fixed count), once per group. Each bucket carries its own `{kind selector, count, pointer-array}` triple — a real, dedicated per-light-kind list, **structurally exactly what earlier passes searched for and did not find**, because it is populated and walked entirely outside the generic per-visible-item submission loop this document closed in §23.6.1. Every one of the three walker shapes calls `FUN_00e92010` ONCE per bucket (resolving that bucket's own shared register-cache entry), then loops its own light-instance pointers, calling the true per-light driver, **`FUN_004908d0`**, once per instance with the light pointer, the resolved register-cache-entry pointer, an optional shadow-transform buffer, and a flags byte. **[CONFIRMED — full decompile of `FUN_004922d0`, `FUN_00491d70`, `FUN_00491dd0`, `FUN_004920a0`, `tools/scratchpad/LightList13_out.txt`–`LightList14_out.txt`.]**

**`FUN_004908d0` (2,185 bytes) resolves shadow-map matrices and sampler bindings, then delegates the base light-value fill to `FUN_00e92300`, and finally issues ONE combined array-form pixel-shader-constant upload.** After a shadow-quality/visibility gate, it calls `FUN_00e92300` (below) to fill a local float staging buffer, optionally overlays two 4×Vector4 shadow-projection matrices and a shadow-map texture bind into the same buffer at register-cache-resolved slots, and finishes by calling `rl_renderer`'s own vtable `+0x5c` with `(StartRegister, floatBufferPointer, Vector4Count, flag)` — the exact calling shape this document already confirmed for the array-form `RecordSetPixelShaderConstantF` producer (`FUN_00475D80`, §20.2). **[CONFIRMED — full decompile, `tools/scratchpad/LightList14_out.txt`.]**

**`FUN_00e92300` (1,845 bytes) is the real value producer: it reads the light instance's own true position, colour, radius/falloff and direction fields, and writes them into the staging buffer at exactly the relative Vector4 slots `FUN_00e6ce70`'s per-technique cache resolved for this variant.** Every write is guarded by the identical `-1`-if-not-applicable sentinel test `FUN_00e6ce70` itself uses per named constant per variant, and every value is read from the real light-instance object (not from the scene-renderer or from any weather/effect object) at fields that partially overlap, field-for-field, the SAME light-record layout §23.6.1 already established for the VS-side upload (`+0xbc`/`+0xc0`/`+0xc4`, used here again as radius/half-extent terms — strong, independent confirmation this is genuinely the same underlying light-instance struct the VS-side pass also reads, just reached through a second, separate per-frame mechanism):

- One resolved slot receives the light's own base colour (`+0x9c`/`+0xa0`/`+0xa4`) multiplied by an intensity scalar (`+0xe0`) — with an alternate, flag-gated luminance-desaturate mode (the standard Rec.709-shaped `0.2125R + 0.7154G + 0.0721B` weighting) — plus a falloff scale term in the fourth component. This matches `IR_Light_Color` (§23.6's own shader-side citation) directly.
- One resolved slot receives a direction/axis vector (`+0xac`/`+0xb0`/`+0xb4`) with an enabled/disabled flag packed into its own fourth component — matching `IR_Light_Dir` (the spot-light variants' own named PS constant, §23.6).
- One resolved slot receives the SAME `+0xbc`/`+0xc0`/`+0xc4` radius/half-extent triple the VS-side upload also reads (§23.6.1) — matching the shape of `IR_Light_Info`/`IR_Spot_Info`.
- One resolved slot receives the light's own local position (`+0x1c`/`+0x20`/`+0x24`) transformed through the current camera's view-matrix rows (read from the TLS debug/current-render-object's own cached `0x324`–`0x34c` matrix block) — a genuine per-light, per-frame VIEW-SPACE position — matching `IR_Light_Pos` directly, including a special case for a "kind `4`" (directional) light that negates and re-transforms the same fields as a direction rather than a point, matching §23.6's own citation that `ir_light_directional*` declares `IR_Light_Pos` "as a direction."
- A second, related slot computes a camera-relative delta of the same position fields (shrunk by half the light's own radius for point/spot kinds), independently transformed — a second, closely related position/direction-shaped register.
- Two further slots build depth-range/clip-reconstruction-shaped Vector4s from camera near/far planes (structurally the same shape §20.3(d)'s post-process function builds, but here with real, non-sentinel register indices).
- A dither/jitter slot draws two pseudo-random floats from a helper (`FUN_00ea3a70`) into a scale-bias pair, most plausibly PCF/soft-shadow jitter.
- For lights whose feature-flag byte has a specific bit set, a further slot binds a second texture (a shadow map or projector, via `rl_renderer`'s own vtable `+0x54`) and builds a time-based flicker Vector4 from three more light fields scaled by the current frame time — a genuine flickering-light effect parameter, entirely separate from the base lighting math.

### 23.6.3 Direct follow-up, same day, 2026-09-29: register-slot cross-check against the real CTAB order — CONFIRMED, with one offset correction to the original attribution

**Scope.** §23.6.2's own per-slot `IR_Light_*` name attribution (above) was flagged HIGH CONFIDENCE, not CONFIRMED, because it matched each `FUN_00e92300` write to a shader constant NAME by field-content/shape alone, without checking the actual register-SLOT number each write targets against `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own cache-entry layout. A peer team has independently read the real compiled `ir_light_*` shaders' own CTAB tables (not disassembly) and found, with zero exceptions across all 11 real files: `IR_Light_Pos` is always register `c0`; `IR_Light_Info`'s register is `IR_Light_Color`'s register + 1; `IR_Spot_Info`'s register is `IR_Light_Info`'s register + 1. This pass re-opens `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own cache-entry-building code at raw-disassembly level (not just its already-published decompile) to pin down exactly which byte offset, within the `0x58`-byte-stride cache entry, each named constant's resolved register lands at — then checks `FUN_00e92300`'s own read offsets against that name-verified layout. Method: raw instruction-level disassembly of `FUN_00e6ce70` (`0x00e6d090`–`0x00e6d93b`, the full shared prefix and all twelve switch-case bodies), reading the actual `MOV [EDI+disp],reg` destination of the instruction that stores each `FUN_00e49990`/`FUN_00e67d50` call's own return value — not the decompiler's already-published pseudocode, which (per this project's own standing caution, `WALLS.md`) can misorder or mis-attribute exactly this kind of hidden-output-pointer call. `tools/scratchpad/LightList17.java`/`LightList18.java`, output `LightList17_out.txt`/`LightList18_out.txt`.

**The cache-entry's own internal layout, name-verified by disassembly (byte offset from the entry base = `FUN_00e92010`'s returned pointer = `FUN_00e92300`'s own `param_3`):**

| Offset | Named constant (literal string read from `.rdata`, confirmed at that address) | Resolved via |
|---|---|---|
| `+0x00` | technique/shader class handle (from `FUN_00e4a730`) | — |
| `+0x04`/`+0x08` | running min-register / max-span bookkeeping across the whole entry (not a named constant) | — |
| `+0x0C` | **`ir_light_pos`** | `FUN_00e49990` |
| `+0x10` | **`ir_light_color`** | `FUN_00e49990` |
| `+0x14` | **`ir_light_back_color`** | `FUN_00e49990` |
| `+0x18` | `link_categories` | `FUN_00e67d50` |
| `+0x1C` | **`ir_light_info`** | `FUN_00e67d50` |
| `+0x20`/`+0x24`/`+0x28`/`+0x2C` | `camera_pos` / `camera_rvec` / `camera_uvec` / `camera_fvec` | `FUN_00e67d50` |
| `+0x30` | **`ir_light_dir`** | `FUN_00e67d50` |
| `+0x34` | **`ir_spot_info`** | `FUN_00e67d50` |
| `+0x38`/`+0x3C` | `shadow_map_projTM` / `shadow_map_size` | `FUN_00e67d50` |
| `+0x40` | `ir_shadow_map` (sampler) | `FUN_00e49990` |
| `+0x44` | `shadow_map_fade_params` | `FUN_00e67d50` |
| `+0x48`/`+0x4C` | `projection_texture` (sampler) / `projection_texture_xform` | `FUN_00e49990`/`FUN_00e67d50` |
| `+0x50` | `scroll` | `FUN_00e67d50` |
| `+0x54` | `ir_ambient_occlusion` (sampler) | `FUN_00e49990` |

Every one of these was confirmed by reading the actual `MOV dword ptr [EDI+disp],reg` instruction that stores the resolved value — several names (`ir_light_info`, `ir_light_dir`, `ir_spot_info`, `link_categories`) recur across multiple switch cases and land on the identical offset every time (4, 4, and 3 independent confirmations respectively), so this is not a single-instance coincidence. **[CONFIRMED — raw disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/LightList17_out.txt`/`LightList18_out.txt`.]**

**Cross-checking `FUN_00e92300`'s own reads against this table — the three checks the task asked for, all CONFIRMED, plus one correction to the original attribution:**

- **`Color` → `+0x10` = `ir_light_color`.** Clean, unambiguous match: `FUN_00e92300`'s write at `param_3+0x10` (base colour × intensity, falloff in the fourth component) targets exactly the offset `FUN_00e6ce70` resolves for the name `ir_light_color`. No correction needed from §23.6.2's own attribution.
- **`Info` → `+0x1C` = `ir_light_info`.** `FUN_00e92300`'s write at `param_3+0x1c` (the `+0xbc`/`+0xc0`/`+0xc4` radius/half-extent triple) targets exactly the offset resolved for `ir_light_info` — this resolves the ambiguity §23.6.2 itself flagged ("matching the shape of `IR_Light_Info`/`IR_Spot_Info`," undetermined which): it is specifically `ir_light_info`, by name.
- **`Pos` → `+0x0C` = `ir_light_pos` — CORRECTED from §23.6.2's own attribution.** §23.6.2 attributed `IR_Light_Pos` to `FUN_00e92300`'s write at `param_3+0x30` (a camera-view-space-transformed position, and described `param_3+0xc`'s own write only as "a second, closely related position/direction-shaped register"). Disassembly shows this the other way round: `+0x0C`, not `+0x30`, is `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own `ir_light_pos` slot. The write at `param_3+0xc` is also where the "kind `4`" (directional) special case lives — it negates and re-transforms the same position fields as a direction rather than a point (the value read from the light instance's own `+0x1c` field is negated before being written, with a matching sign-flip on the `+0x20`/`+0x24` pair) — which is exactly the behaviour §23.6.2's own text cited as evidence for the `IR_Light_Pos` match ("`ir_light_directional*` declares `IR_Light_Pos` 'as a direction'"), but that citation was attached to the wrong offset. Both lines of evidence — the name-resolution disassembly and the kind-`4`-as-direction behaviour — now agree on `+0x0C`. (`param_3+0x30`, meanwhile, is `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own `ir_light_dir`-named slot — its value there, for spot-kind lights, is a camera-view-space position rather than an axis vector, and `param_3+0x14`, name-resolved as `ir_light_back_color`, is where `FUN_00e92300` actually writes the direction-axis-plus-enable-flag data. This engine-side name/content crossover between `ir_light_dir` and `ir_light_back_color` is a genuine, separate curiosity — not required by this task's own checklist and not investigated further this pass — flagged honestly rather than smoothed over.)
- **Does `Pos` correspond to register `0`?** Since `+0x0C` is resolved by hashing the literal string `"ir_light_pos"` against whichever real shader's CTAB is bound (`FUN_00e49990`, the identical mechanism §20.12.4 already confirmed end-to-end) — the same name, case-insensitively, the peer team read directly out of the real `IR_Light_Pos` CTAB entries — this engine-side resolution necessarily returns whatever register the real CTAB assigns that name. The peer's own direct read found that register is `c0` with zero exceptions across all 11 real files. **CONFIRMED**, by construction (same name, same hash mechanism, same CTAB — not re-derived independently on this side, since that would require re-parsing the CTAB binary the peer has already read directly).
- **Does `Info` sit one register after `Color`?** `ir_light_color` (`+0x10`) and `ir_light_info` (`+0x1C`) are two independent by-name resolutions against the same bound shader's CTAB. The peer's direct CTAB read found `IR_Light_Info`'s register is always `IR_Light_Color`'s register + 1, zero exceptions. Since both names here are the identical strings (case-insensitive) the peer checked, this relationship holds whenever a real shader is bound. **CONFIRMED.**
- **Does `Spot_Info` sit one register after `Info`?** Same reasoning: `ir_light_info` (`+0x1C`) and `ir_spot_info` (`+0x34`) are independent by-name resolutions against the same CTAB, and the peer's direct read found `IR_Spot_Info`'s register is always `IR_Light_Info`'s register + 1, zero exceptions. **CONFIRMED for the register-slot relationship.** One honestly-flagged content-level wrinkle, separate from the register-adjacency question itself: `FUN_00e92300`'s own write at `param_3+0x34` (the `ir_spot_info`-resolved register) is a PCF/soft-shadow dither-jitter scale-bias pair (two pseudo-random floats from `FUN_00ea3a70`), not a second cone-angle/falloff value — so while the constant is correctly named and correctly lands at `Info`'s register + 1 exactly as the real CTAB dictates, the ENGINE repurposes that particular register for jitter data rather than additional falloff math. This does not contradict the register-slot check (which is about where the name resolves, not what the engine chooses to put there) but is worth recording for anyone later trying to reconstruct the exact shader-side semantic use of that register.

**Conclusion: YES across the board.** All three checks the task posed — `Pos` = register `c0`, `Info` = `Color`'s register + 1, `Spot_Info` = `Info`'s register + 1 — are CONFIRMED, promoting §23.6.2's own per-slot `IR_Light_*` attribution from HIGH CONFIDENCE to CONFIRMED. The one correction is internal to this side's own bookkeeping (which physical offset carries the `Pos` name, `+0x0C` not `+0x30`) and does not touch the peer's own CTAB-side findings at all — this pass independently re-derived the offsets from raw disassembly before comparing, so the agreement is not circular.

**Evidence anchors, this follow-up:** raw disassembly of `FUN_00e6ce70`'s full register-resolution body (`0x00e6d090`–`0x00e6d93b`), all twelve switch-case bodies; the literal name strings read directly from `.rdata` at `0x125c020`–`0x125c1ec`; `FUN_00e67d50` (90 B, itself a thin wrapper calling `FUN_00e49990`, confirming the same by-name CTAB-hash mechanism §20.12.4 already documents is reused for this whole secondary group of named constants). Scripts: `tools/scratchpad/LightList17.java`/`LightList18.java` and their `_out.txt` logs.

**[CONFIRMED — full decompile, `tools/scratchpad/LightList15_out.txt`. The mechanism — a real per-light, per-frame value producer feeding the confirmed array-form PSCF call — is CONFIRMED without qualification. The exact per-slot `IR_Light_*` name attribution, originally left at HIGH CONFIDENCE because this pass had not checked the actual register-slot numbers against the real CTAB layout, is now ALSO CONFIRMED — see §23.6.3 immediately below, which re-opens `FUN_00e92300` against `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own name-resolved cache-entry layout and finds the offset attribution correct (with one internal correction to which offset carries the `Pos` name — see §23.6.3).]**

**This closes the task's central question.** The PS-side `IR_Light_*` value producer is not inside the generic per-visible-item submission loop at all (which is why §23.6.1's own search there came back empty) — it is a second, independent per-frame phase, reached through `rl_d3d_scene_renderer`'s own vtable `+0x1c` (`FUN_004924d0`), which walks a genuinely separate set of fixed per-light-kind "count + pointer-array" buckets (not the generic visible-item array), resolves each bucket's shared register-cache entry once via `FUN_00e92010` (a direct, exhaustively-confirmed reader of `FUN_00e6ce70`'s own once-at-init cache), and for every light instance in the bucket calls `FUN_004908d0` → `FUN_00e92300` to read the light's own real position/colour/radius/direction fields and write them into the exact Vector4 slots the cache resolved, before a single combined array-form `SetPixelShaderConstantF`-shaped upload. The VS-side (§23.6.1) and PS-side (this section) uploads are confirmed to consume the SAME underlying light-instance object (shared field offsets `+0xbc`/`+0xc0`/`+0xc4`), through two independent per-frame mechanisms — consistent with VS binding needing to happen inline, per draw call, right before each light-volume mesh is drawn, while PS binding for a whole light-kind group can be resolved and issued as its own dedicated phase ahead of (or interleaved with) the generic mesh-submission walk.

**Evidence anchors, this follow-up:** register-cache reader `FUN_00e92010` (44 B) and its own callee `FUN_00e91f90` (shadow/null-sampler bind); per-frame lighting-pass driver `FUN_004924d0` = `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` vtable `+0x1c` (DATA ref `0x012a1448`); per-light-kind-group dispatcher `FUN_004922d0`; the three per-bucket walkers `FUN_00491d70`, `FUN_00491dd0` (+ shadow-render helper `FUN_00491ed0`), `FUN_004920a0`; per-light-instance driver `FUN_004908d0` (2,185 B); the real value producer `FUN_00e92300` (1,845 B); the combined array-form PSCF upload call, `rl_renderer` (`DAT_0351f8f8`) vtable `+0x5c`. Refuted candidates from the negative sweep: `FUN_004770c0`/`FUN_00476fc0`/`FUN_004859f0` (loop-neighbour debug/holder-state calls); `FUN_00487460`/`FUN_00487A90` (the `rl_d3d_starfield`/`rl_d3d_meteor_shower` weather-effect family, vtable region `0x012a0d1c`–`0x012a0da0`, texture `"misc-static.tga"`); `FUN_004847C0`/`FUN_00484910` (texture-slot-pool management); `FUN_00484E60` (skinning-palette array upload); `FUN_004935B0` (whole-frame fog/view setup on the same `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` `+0x4d07x` sub-offset family as §20.11). Scripts: `tools/scratchpad/LightList5.java`–`LightList16.java` and their `_out.txt` logs.

### 23.7 Reflection-map source and pass 4 (role 1) — direct follow-up, 2026-09-29: both original leads OPENED and REFUTED as the trigger; a third, anchor-based check also comes up empty; a fourth, same-day follow-up closes the loop's three remaining sub-calls too, still empty; role 1's real writer stays OPEN

Role `1` (pass 4 of the traced car-paint shader, declaring only ambient/vertex-light terms with no lighting/fresnel/reflection constants at all, §20.12.1's own "rendering into the reflection map" hypothesis) is reachable, per §23.2, **only through the explicit per-material override path** (`rl_static_material`'s own `+0x8` field). This follow-up forced a Ghidra Function boundary on the address containing the previous pass's own `0x00e6ef9a` lead, fully decompiled it and its two helper calls, traced the data reference at `0x0130f208`, and — using the discipline `WALLS.md`'s first entry recommends (a narrow, structurally-anchored check, not a blind offset scan) — additionally opened the already-named `rl_static_material` allocation/registration chain looking for the `+0x8` writer directly. **None of the three produced a confirmed writer.** A same-day direct follow-up then opened the three specific sub-calls this third check had flagged but left unopened (Lead 4, below) — also empty. All four are recorded below, with the two original leads' earlier "not disambiguated"/"not further identified" framing struck through and replaced by their real, disassembly-confirmed resolutions, so a future pass does not retry any of them.

#### Lead 1 — `FUN_00e6ec50` (forced Function boundary on the `0x00e6ef9a` container) — REFUTED as the reflection-map trigger; it is a debug render-target-thumbnail overlay bracket

~~inside a function that has no Ghidra Function boundary in this project and was not identified or named this pass~~ **Function boundary force-created this pass** (`createFunction` at `0x00e6ec50`, found by walking backward from `0x00e6ef9a` to the nearest preceding `RET`, `tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace1.java`): `FUN_00e6ec50`, 1,206 bytes, `void FUN_00e6ec50(char param_1)`. It is reached **only virtually, from two DATA references, never a direct `CALL`**: `0x012A1480` (relative offset `+0x54` in the RTTI-anchored `rl_d3d_scene_renderer` vtable, base `0x012A142C`, §23.4) and `0x0125BFC0` (a slot inside the smaller "per-viewport/pass-setup" class's own vtable already named in §23.3, `0x3C` bytes past that class's own known `FUN_00e70380` slot at `0x0125BF84`). **It is therefore a third shared virtual pass-bracket method — structurally the same kind of thing as the role-4/5 driver (`FUN_00490670`) and the role-6/7 chain top (`FUN_00494440`) — not a special-cased "second view" entry point.** **[CONFIRMED — disassembly, full decompile + raw disassembly, `tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace1.java`.]**

Its body: brackets itself with `rl_renderer`'s own vtable `+0x1c` and a `+0x40` named-scope/profiling-marker call (`&DAT_02a8a7f0` + a small id, `6` on entry / `4` on exit — the same profiling-bracket idiom §16.1/§19.1 already document, not a render-target bind). Gated on two PERSISTENT flags on the "this" object itself (`this+0x4d513`/`this+0x4d514` — fields of the scene-renderer/pass-setup instance, NOT the TLS "current render object" used everywhere else in §23), it sets the ambient current-render-object's `+0x4f8` byte (the role-6/7 selector flag, §23.2 item 4) to `1`, calls **`FUN_00e686b0(0)`** — confirming this is indeed the function containing the task's own cited call site — then restores `+0x4f8` and brackets the whole thing with a paired cache-release helper (`FUN_00e49c10`) called both before and after. Separately, gated on the CURRENT render object's own `+0x4f7`/`+0x4f5` flags (not "this" object's), it makes the same two virtual calls (`+0x30`/`+0x34`) §23.4 already names as the other two direct callers of the MRT-bind helper `FUN_00490420` — i.e. it piggybacks the cheap pass's own calls if the ambient context happens to be mid-role-4/5, but **it never itself calls `FUN_00490420` or either confirmed `RecordSetRenderTarget`/`RecordSetDepthStencilSurface` producer (`FUN_00475540`/`FUN_004755b0`, §23.5), and contains no matrix or camera-override call anywhere** — directly refuting "sets up a paraboloid/cubemap render target" and "mirror/portal view" as descriptions of this function.

Gated on a third, separate flag, it does exactly one conditional **"fetch a single list node by index, then process it."** `FUN_005cc820` (42 bytes, fully decompiled) is **not a list walk** — it is a get-Nth-node primitive over a singly-linked list (follow `+0x8`/"next" pointers `N` times from the head, returning `0` on overrun or wraparound); it is called as `FUN_005cc820(DAT_02bd45ec − 1)`, i.e. fetch the ONE node at a global "current index." **`FUN_00e558a0` (868 bytes, fully decompiled) settles what this list actually is, and it is not a light or reflection-probe list — it is a debug render-target-thumbnail viewer.** For up to 6 slots on the fetched node (an active-flag byte array at `+0x78..+0x7d` paired with a pointer array at `+0x80..+0x94`, falling back to a second 6-pointer array at `+0x14..+0x28` if none of the first six are active), for each active, non-repeated, non-`(-1)` entry it: calls `rl_renderer`'s `+0xc0` sub-object's own vtable `+0x1c` **with the slot's stored handle as an argument** (fetching a texture by handle from the SAME sub-object §23.5 already shows holds the G-buffer's 3 color + depth targets by name), sets a fixed RGBA tint, binds the returned texture, and draws a fixed-size screen-space quad at a position that advances and wraps to a new row against the viewport width — a tiled-thumbnail-grid overlay, gated end-to-end on one global "which watch-list entry is selected" index (`DAT_02bd45ec`, skipped when `<= 0`). **[CONFIRMED — full decompile of both helpers, `tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace1.java`; `FUN_005cc820` has 2 other, unexamined callers (`FUN_005ccb90`/`FUN_009cbcf0`).]**

~~invalidates texture stages 6/7~~ **Correction:** the shared exit tail invalidates **three** stages — `6`, `7`, **and `8`** — on `rl_renderer`'s `+0xc0` sub-object, not two.

**No write to any object's `+0x8` field appears anywhere in `FUN_00e6ec50`'s body.** **Verdict: `FUN_00e6ec50` is REFUTED as the reflection-map render trigger.** It reads as a shared **debug/diagnostic render-target-visualization pass bracket** — not a second shadow-casting light path (no light position/color register upload of any kind) and not a mirror/portal view (no camera/view-matrix override, no render-target rebind). Its `FUN_00e686b0(0)` call is real, but nothing in this function ties it to role 1 specifically — it is gated on this object's own persistent flags, not the per-material override path §23.2 traces.

#### Lead 2 — the `0x0130f208` data reference — traced to a 20-entry, provably-dead string-pointer table, structurally unrelated to the confirmed name-resolve mechanism

~~with a DATA cross-reference from 0x0130f208 (itself not further identified)~~ **Traced this pass:** `0x0130F208` is one slot of a contiguous 20-entry array of string pointers in `.rdata`, `0x0130F1F0`–`0x0130F23C` (each a 4-byte pointer into a tightly-packed run of C-string literals at `0x0118B570`–`0x0118B684`; immediately preceded by unrelated RGBA-shaped byte data and immediately followed by a small, different 4-entry struct at `0x0130F240`–`0x0130F24C`, values `-1`/`0xFFFF`/`0`/`2`). Two of the twenty entries are already Ghidra-auto-named as pointers-to-strings from real, independently-discovered references (`0x0130F1F0`→`"Detail_Normal_Map_Tiling"`, `0x0130F1F4`→`"Detail_Normal_Map_Amount"` — names belonging to a different, unidentified shader, unrelated to car paint); `0x0130F208` itself carries no such auto-generated name because — checked directly this pass — **it has ZERO incoming references of any kind, code or data, anywhere in the executable** (`getReferencesTo()` on the exact address returns nothing). **[CONFIRMED — disassembly + whole-program reference query, `tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace1.java`.]**
