**Cross-checked against the mechanism the original lead assumed feeds this table (§20.12.4's by-name register-resolve, `FUN_00e49990`/`FUN_00e49d10`): it does not.** Both functions were decompiled in full this pass. `FUN_00e49990` dispatches on a small integer `kind` to one of three sibling resolvers, first hashing the caller-supplied name via `FUN_00d9e740`. `FUN_00e49d10` then indexes a per-technique array using **purely integer arithmetic** (bounded sub-indices, no string comparison) to reach an object pointer, which it calls through that object's own vtable `+0x1c` — **at no point does it read a string, or touch anything in the `0x0130F1F0`–`0x0130F23C` range.** This refutes the implicit premise that this string table feeds the standard by-name resolve mechanism; it is a structurally separate, and — per the reference count above — apparently dead, piece of static data. **[CONFIRMED — full decompile of both functions, `tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace1.java`.]**

**Net effect: Lead 2 does not identify a producer.** Unlike 6 of the 14 already-checked pass-2/3 constant names (§20.12.11), which have no string anywhere in the binary, `Reflection_Plane_Height`'s name string DOES exist — but the one table entry pointing to it is provably never read by anything, statically. `FUN_004A3360` remains REFUTED (§20.12.5, routes through the `rl_renderer` `_purecall` trap). **Best-motivated next step, not attempted this pass:** if `Reflection_Plane_Height` is ever populated for a real material, the mechanism is most plausibly the same generic per-material array-B/C name-hash remap already fully traced for `Specular_Power` and thirteen siblings (`FUN_00e70d30`, §20.12.6) — that mechanism resolves ANY name a shader declares and a material authors, is not pass-specific, and was never checked against this specific name.

#### Lead 3 (new this pass, narrow and anchor-based per `WALLS.md`'s own discipline, not a repeat of the flagged broad scan) — the `rl_static_material` allocation/registration chain — checked directly, does not write `+0x8` either

The already-named `rl_static_material` allocator and global-registry chain (§22.7's own anchors: `FUN_00e5e240`, `FUN_00e3fcb0`, `FUN_00e3faa0`, `FUN_00e3f8e0`, `FUN_00e3f830`, `FUN_00e3f190`, `FUN_00e3ece0`, `FUN_00e3edf0`, `FUN_00e7a520`), plus a real shader/technique-pass loader this pass found allocating one fresh `rl_static_material` per declared pass in a loop (`FUN_0074d260`, validated via a fixed header-signature check, together with `FUN_00e401d0`/`FUN_00e71090`), were all decompiled in full (`tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace2.java`). **The allocator `FUN_00e5e240` installs the `rl_static_material` vtable and zeroes exactly one field, `+0x14` — it never touches `+0x8`**, so the `-1` sentinel §23.2 describes must already be sitting in the recycled free-list memory from a prior construction, not freshly written by this allocator. `FUN_0074d260`'s per-pass loop calls exactly one setter on the freshly-allocated object afterward, **`FUN_00e5e220`** — decompiled (`tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace3.java`) as a **trivial one-line `this+0x14 = param_1` setter** (itself a shared, compiler-folded generic setter used by 50+ unrelated call sites/classes at the same relative offset), storing the loop's own pass-ordinal counter **at `+0x14`, not `+0x8`.** The remaining touch on the new object in this loop, `FUN_00e3fcb0`, is the already-documented content-dedup hash-registry insert (§22.2/§22.7), unrelated to role selection. **No write to `+0x8` was found anywhere in this chain. [CONFIRMED NEGATIVE — full decompile of 12 functions, `tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace2.java`/`ReflTrace3.java`.]** ~~The real `+0x8` writer — plausibly a separate step of this same per-pass construction loop reached through a call this pass did not open (`FUN_00e718b0`, `thunk_FUN_00482d20`, or three other unopened object constructions inside `FUN_0074d260` itself) — remains unfound.~~ **Update, same day: these three specific sub-calls were opened as a direct follow-up — see Lead 4, immediately below. None of them is the writer either.**

#### Lead 4 (direct follow-up, same day, 2026-09-29) — the loop's three remaining sub-calls, now opened in full: none writes `+0x8` either, and two previously-unnamed sibling classes are RTTI-identified along the way

The three specific, already-named sub-calls flagged as unopened at the end of Lead 3 above — the per-pass loop's own Mesh-block-resolving call, its object-pool call reached through a thunk, and the loop's other two fresh-object constructions alongside the already-refuted `rl_static_material` allocation — were opened and fully decompiled this pass (`tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace4.java`, `ReflTrace5.java`, `ReflTrace6.java`). Every function below was checked instruction-by-instruction, off the complete raw disassembly, for a literal `+0x8` byte-offset store on an object-pointer register — as distinct from an ESP-relative local-variable slot, which several of these functions genuinely do use at `+0x8` for unrelated float/register scratch space (the same coincidental-offset trap `WALLS.md` warns against; ruled out explicitly here by checking the base register on every hit, not assumed).

**Part A — the Mesh-block-resolving call: REFUTED, and independently identified as a pre-existing, unrelated generic function.** Fully decompiled (89 bytes; 8 callers project-wide, none render-pass-specific), it already carries its own annotation in this project's own analysis identifying it as a generic, format-agnostic Mesh-sub-block dispatcher: it hands off to a Mesh-block parser and, when that block's own first-byte flag has bit 0 set, to a second helper that reads matching geometry-buffer data via a threaded cursor — the same generic wrapper this project's tree/vehicle-geometry work already named and uses across all 8 of its call sites, unrelated to material role selection. Its own body performs no field store on its return value at all beyond two local stack temporaries; there is no `+0x8` write, and no write to any object field of any kind. **[CONFIRMED — full decompile + complete raw disassembly.]**

**Part B — the thunked object-pool call resolves to a fixed-size free-list pop for a class independently RTTI-identified as `rl_d3d_mesh`; REFUTED.** The thunk (a separate short unconditional-jump stub at a different address) forwards to a function that pops one node off a global free list and, on a successful pop, delegates the real per-object reinitialization to a second function. That function itself first calls a base-initializer that installs an intermediate vtable, derives a bounding-box/radius triple from three unit-scale floats, then re-installs a second, different vtable — before the outer function re-installs the FINAL vtable and links the object into a global doubly-linked registry (a counter-and-head-pointer pair at two fixed globals). The final vtable (`0x012A0418`) resolves cleanly via its RTTI Complete Object Locator to the mangled name `.?AVrl_d3d_mesh@@` — **this object's class is `rl_d3d_mesh`**, a previously-unnamed sibling class in this project's own RTTI inventory. Across all three functions in this chain, every touched byte offset was enumerated directly from the raw disassembly: `+0x0` (twice — base-then-derived vtable), `+0x10`, `+0x18`, `+0x1c`, `+0x20`, `+0x24`, `+0x28`, `+0x38`, `+0x3c`, `+0x40`, `+0x44`, `+0x48`. **`+0x8` is never touched.** **[CONFIRMED NEGATIVE — full decompile + complete raw disassembly of all three functions; RTTI Complete-Object-Locator/TypeDescriptor chain walked and the mangled name read directly from image memory.]**

**Part C — the loop's other two fresh-object constructions, alongside the already-refuted `rl_static_material` allocation: both opened, both RTTI-identified, neither writes `+0x8`.** One is a fully self-contained free-list pop with no delegate call — its entire body touches exactly two fields, installing its vtable at `+0x0` and zeroing one further field at `+0x4`, nothing else — and its vtable RTTI-resolves to `.?AVrl_material_map@@` (**class `rl_material_map`**). The other pops its own free-list node and delegates to a 391-byte real initializer before installing its own final vtable; that initializer sets several unit-scale floats, a visibility/registry flag pair (mirrored into a second lookup array only when a valid slot index is present), a handful of zeroed or word-sized state fields, a re-seeded per-instance random byte, and one field copied from a "current time" accessor, while the outer function then installs the final vtable and zeroes two more fields — RTTI-resolving to `.?AVrl_mesh_instance@@` (**class `rl_mesh_instance`**). Across both of this object's functions, the touched offsets are `+0x0`, `+0x34` (touched twice), `+0x38`, `+0x39`, the `+0x90`–`+0xac` and `+0xb0`–`+0xc8` float ranges, `+0xd8`, `+0xdc`, `+0xe0`, `+0xc4`, `+0xcc`, `+0xce`, `+0xf0`, `+0xf4`. **`+0x8` is never touched in either object's construction.** **[CONFIRMED NEGATIVE — full decompile + complete raw disassembly of all four functions involved (the two allocators and their two delegate initializers); RTTI resolved the same way as Part B.]**

**Net effect of Lead 4.** All three previously-flagged sub-calls are now open, and none is the `+0x8` writer. The per-pass construction loop's real shape is now fully mapped for the first time: for every declared technique pass it constructs FOUR sibling objects side by side — the already-known `rl_static_material` (Lead 3, refuted), plus one each of `rl_d3d_mesh`, `rl_material_map`, and `rl_mesh_instance` (this Lead, all three refuted) — and not one of the four construction chains ever stores a literal into any object's own `+0x8` field. This is a real, structurally-anchored exhaustion of every construction call this loop makes, not a partial check, and it surfaces three genuinely new RTTI-identified classes (`rl_d3d_mesh`, `rl_material_map`, `rl_mesh_instance`) for this project's own inventory even though none of them turned out to be the writer being searched for.

**Evidence anchors, Lead 4:** Mesh-block dispatcher `FUN_00e718b0` (→ `FUN_00e71410`/`FUN_00e71740`); thunk target `FUN_00482d20` → `FUN_00482e00` → `FUN_00e75cd0` (class `rl_d3d_mesh`, vtable `0x012A0418`); `FUN_00e3ffd0` (class `rl_material_map`, vtable `0x0125acd4`); `FUN_00e44680` → `FUN_00e46910` (class `rl_mesh_instance`, vtable `0x01124e0c`). Scripts: `tools/scratchpad/ReflTrace4.java`, `ReflTrace5.java`, `ReflTrace6.java`.

### 23.8 G-buffer readback (`IR_GBuffer_DSF_DataSampler`) — OPEN

A whole-image byte-pattern search for the literal strings `"IR_GBuffer"`, `"GBuffer"`, and `"Paraboloid"` found **zero hits anywhere in the main executable** (`tools/scratchpad/Frame8.java`) — in sharp contrast to every other named constant this document has successfully traced by name (`World_xform`, `ir_light_pos`, `Reflection_Plane_Height` above, etc.), all of which exist as literal strings in the `.exe`'s own data. This is consistent with `IR_GBuffer_DSF_DataSampler` being a name that only ever appears inside the compiled `.fxo_pc` shader bytecode's own CTAB (i.e. the engine-side code that binds it, if it exists, must either resolve it through a different, truncated/prefixed literal not searched for here, or through the sampler-specific "T0 lookup by hash" mechanism `spec-fxo-format.md` §6.5 documents as distinct from the float-register `FUN_00e49990` mechanism used for every constant traced so far) — **not chased further this pass.**

**Why no string exists — now confirmed, not just consistent-with, via a peer team's exhaustive data-side CTAB scan (844/844 real `.fxo_pc` files, 2026-09-29).** `IR_GBuffer_DSF_DataSampler` is a real, common sampler — 675/844 shaders (80% of the whole shader library), 1,329/1,329 blob-level hits exact, **always at sampler register s9**, precisely matching the already-traced car-paint shader's own s9 usage (§20.12.2) — but it is used exclusively by FORWARD MATERIAL shaders (decals, foliage, glass, vehicle paint, `rl_ssao_*`/`rl_ground_reflect_*`), never by any of the 13 `ir_light_*` shaders (§23.6). This confirms the engine-side binder this section was searching for, if one exists as a single mechanism, must be reached through the sampler-specific by-name-hash mechanism, not the float-register literal-string mechanism every other name in this document was traced through — a real, population-exact number (**pixel-shader sampler stage 9**) to search for the setter of, rather than the name string itself. **[CONFIRMED — real, exhaustive population data.]**

Consequently, whether the main lighting pass (role 6/7) actually samples back any of the three render targets §23.5 shows get bound for the cheap pass was **not confirmed or refuted** this pass. This is the single most direct unanswered piece of the task's own three-part question, and the most promising concrete next step for a future pass would be tracing the four target-registry writer functions named in §23.5 forward, rather than searching for the sampler name itself. **Update, 2026-09-29 (§23.11): that trace was run to exhaustion — the four candidates themselves and the confirmed `CreateTexture` wrapper's complete 5-call-site graph — without resolving to these offsets. This specific angle is now closed as inconclusive rather than merely untried.**

### 23.11 Opening the four candidate writer functions — CONFIRMED negative result, plus a deeper trace of the real `CreateTexture` call graph

Direct follow-up on §23.5's/§23.8's own "natural next step" (a peer-team join request, sharpened over the course of this pass by two real external corrections — first that the sampler-name population data favours the plain Lighting/Normals/Depth reading over an earlier Depth/DSF/Normals-only refinement, then that cross-shader bytecode evidence favours "Lighting" being a prepass-written material-parameter buffer rather than a final accumulation target, both already recorded in §23.5 above). All four previously-unopened candidates (`FUN_009dd7b0`, `FUN_009de970`, `FUN_009ed6f0`, `FUN_009ee0d0`) were fully decompiled this pass in an isolated Ghidra project (`tools/gp_render9`), scripts `tools/scratchpad/Frame12.java`–`Frame19.java`. **None of the four sets up a pixel format or texture-creation parameter for any of the four named target slots — the original "consistent with format/size setup" characterization (§23.5) does not survive actually opening the functions:**

- **`FUN_009dd7b0`** (186 bytes) is gated on an identity check against a *different* global (`DAT_0262edfc`), not `rl_renderer`'s `+0xc0` sub-object at all. Its body is a generic array-compaction routine — "find the entry whose two caller-supplied key fields match, remove it, shift the remainder down" — over an unrelated `0x18`-byte-stride array (base `param_1+0x2828`, count at `param_1+0x2820`). Its one touch on offset `0x2840` is simply array-element index 1 of *this different array*, not a write to the target-slot registry. **REFUTED as a target-slot writer.**
- **`FUN_009de970`** (1,887 bytes) genuinely is a constructor — it stores a vtable pointer at its own offset 0, then blanket-initializes on the order of 2,800+ fields to `0`/`-1`/cached-default-colour sentinels, including the byte ranges under `0x2618`/`0x2788`/`0x2840`/`0x26d0`/`0x26dc`. A constructor's blanket zero-fill cannot reveal a pixel format; it only nulls the surface pointers before first real use. Whether this is truly the constructor of the exact class embedded at `rl_renderer`'s `+0xc0` (as opposed to a same-shaped but different class elsewhere) was not resolved either way — its own caller chain (`FUN_009e85a0`←`FUN_009e85f0`, both single-caller) did not connect back to the confirmed device bring-up sequence (`FUN_0047c150`/`FUN_0047ba00`, §3.6) within the hops checked this pass.
- **`FUN_009ed6f0`** (165 bytes) and **`FUN_009ee0d0`** (4,173 bytes) both operate on a governing object that is a completely different, much larger class from an entirely different part of the binary — fields at `+0x2088`, `+0xd24`, `+0xa41`, `+0x1d97`, `+0x16c0`/`+0x16c4`, a circular-linked-list walk checking element type codes `0xc`/`0xd`/`0x13`/`0x16`, and heavy fog/ambient/time-of-day colour-blend arithmetic against a long, contiguous family of colour constants — consistent with a weather/time-of-day/environment-zone subsystem, not the renderer's target-slot registry. `FUN_009ed6f0` computes a four-float rectangle (via a shared helper, `FUN_009f5c00`) into that object's own offsets `0x2784`/`0x2788`/`0x278c`/`0x2790`; `FUN_009ee0d0` blends zone/fog colour data into offset `0x2794` and a large shader-constant block reached through a different helper's return value. **Both REFUTED as target-slot writers** — the touch on the `0x2788`/`0x2794` byte range is a coincidental match with this unrelated class's own field layout at the same numeric offset, the exact failure mode `WALLS.md`'s first entry documents.

This reproduces that documented failure mode three more times independently while trying to work around it. `FUN_00490420`'s own decompiled body shows the REAL fields it reads are not the four slots' bare base offsets, but `base+0x88` (the surface pointer) and `base+0x34`→`+8` (a companion flag byte) — and this exact `+0x30`/`+0x34`/`+0x88..`/`+0xb4` shape independently matches the field layout inside the confirmed `CreateTexture` wrapper `FUN_00472710` (§4.1: texture pointer, companion pointer, per-mip surface pointers, offscreen-plain-surface pointer respectively) — a real, reusable "texture-slot" struct, `0xB8` bytes long end to end. Re-running the search on these derived, function-confirmed offsets (`0x26a0`/`0x2810`/`0x28c8`/`0x2758`, `tools/scratchpad/Frame13.java`) still landed almost entirely on the same unrelated weather/environment-range functions, plus three more unrelated hits in entirely different subsystems: a Lua/string-table-shaped function (`FUN_00457370`); a further string/localization-cache class (`FUN_0070cfe0`/`FUN_0070d110`/`FUN_0070d270`); a video-settings mirror pair (`FUN_00b982b0`/`FUN_00b98570`); and one outright false positive (`FUN_00799850`), whose "hit" was inside an ~11 KB local stack frame, not a struct field at all. **None of these, once decompiled (`tools/scratchpad/Frame14.java`), contained a `CreateTexture`/`CreateRenderTarget`-shaped call or a format literal either.**

A second, independent angle was tried: `FUN_00472710` (the `CreateTexture` wrapper §4.1 already confirms against the public `IDirect3DDevice9::CreateTexture` vtable slot) has **exactly 5 call sites in the whole binary**, re-confirmed exhaustively this pass (`tools/scratchpad/Frame15.java`) and all decompiled in full (`Frame16.java`–`Frame18.java`):

- Three sites inside `FUN_00472f40`, plus one inside `FUN_00472e00`, all walk the *same* global linked list of texture-descriptor records (`DAT_03171b08`). Each record supplies its own width/height/levels/**format** — the format value comes from the descriptor node's own data field, genuinely data-driven rather than a literal in the consumer code — plus its own integer *index*, turned into a storage address via `index * 0xB8 + 0xC4 + <owning object>` (the default path) or `(index + 1) * 0xB8 + <per-thread override object>` (the override path). This is a real, generic, device-loss-safe texture pool: the successful-creation tail writes the cached width/height/levels/format back to the created slot's own `+0x10`/`+0x14`/`+0x18`/`+0x1c` fields (`+0x1c` = the cached format).
- The fifth site, `FUN_004739f0`, is a single hard-coded case for descriptor **index `10`** specifically, storing into a fixed field (`+0x7f4`) of *its own* parameter object — confirming the pattern (specific indices get dedicated, hard-coded storage spots on their owning object) but on a different object, not `rl_renderer`'s `+0xc0` sub-object.

**None of these 5 call sites resolves to our four named offsets.** The generic pool's own index arithmetic (`index*0xB8+0xC4`) does not land on `0x2618`, `0x2788`, `0x2840`, or `0x26d0` for any integer index, and no call site hard-codes any of these four offsets the way `FUN_004739f0` hard-codes index 10's own `+0x7f4`. **The specific `CreateTexture` call that ultimately supplies the surface pointer at each of the four named G-buffer target slots was not found this pass, via either the original offset-literal search or a forward trace from the one confirmed, exhaustively-enumerated `CreateTexture` wrapper in the whole binary.** Device reset (`FUN_0047c800`, §5) does call `FUN_00472f40` once (recreating the generic pool, plus a small handful of unrelated dynamic vertex/index buffers created directly against the device on the same path) and does explicitly rebind stage 0 to the primary back buffer while clearing stages 1–3 immediately afterward — behaviour fully consistent with (and not contradicting) the private role-4/5 target set being something *separate* from the primary swap-chain targets, but it does not itself create or touch the four named slots either.

**Cross-reference against real captured runtime data**, supplied and independently checked this pass (an external runtime capture, dirty-side — findings summarized here, the source is not for clean-side consultation — a real, verified single-frame `DrawIndexedPrimitive` capture; not derived from this project's own disassembly). The log's real render-target list includes a compact 2-channel `G16R16` (`D3DFMT` 34) target at the primary 1280×720 resolution, written by draws using a (non-`IR_GBuffer_`-prefixed) `Normal_MapSampler` pixel shader, and several low-resolution (roughly quarter-size, `400×288`–`512×512`) wide-float `A16B16G16R16F` (`D3DFMT` 113) targets — one of which is read by a fullscreen composite draw using `ps='IR_GBuffer_LightingSampler'` (address `14E90F00`, not itself captured as a written-to render target inside this particular frame, but adjacent in the allocator's address range to the other confirmed quarter-res `A16B16G16R16F` targets), and another read via `IR_GBuffer_DepthSampler` (bound through the depth-stencil surface directly rather than a regular texture slot — `tex0=00000000` in the log — consistent with genuine depth-buffer sampling). `IR_GBuffer_NormalsSampler` does not appear as a `ps=` value anywhere in this particular captured frame. **This is real, useful context for the overall G-buffer-semantics question, and is consistent with (does not refute) the peer team's "Lighting is a packed parameter buffer, not an accumulation buffer" bytecode finding above** — a 4×16-bit-float target is equally compatible with several independent packed parameters as with an accumulated colour — **but it cannot be tied to this section's own four specific static-side offsets, because no create-call or format literal was found on the disassembly side to match against it.** The `Normal_MapSampler`-written `G16R16` target is a suggestive, not confirmed, data point for a compact packed-normals format existing somewhere in this pipeline; it was not established that this specific real target corresponds to *this section's* role-4/5 stage-1/2 slots rather than some other normal-related buffer in the (heavily modified, Remix-intercepted) capture.

**[CONFIRMED — disassembly: all four originally-flagged candidate writers, fully decompiled and REFUTED as target-slot format writers; the confirmed `CreateTexture` wrapper's complete 5-call-site graph, exhaustively traced and also not resolving to these offsets. OPEN, unchanged in substance from §23.8/§23.10: the actual creation site and real pixel format of the four named G-buffer target slots. This upgrades the investigative state from "not yet opened" to "opened and exhausted, genuinely inconclusive" — a real, reported negative result, not an oversight.]**

**Addendum, same day — a peer team pinned the specific draws, and confirmed the negative more precisely.** Address `14E90F00` (the `IR_GBuffer_LightingSampler`-read target named above) is bound at real capture draw numbers **1483–1485** specifically (independently re-verified directly against the log by the orchestrator: draws 1483 and 1485 are `PASS`-type fullscreen composites reading it via `ps='IR_GBuffer_LightingSampler'`, draw 1484 a depth-only `MARK` prepass writing into it) — and, confirmed by direct search, `14E90F00` genuinely has **no** `=== RENDER TARGET ===` header of its own anywhere in this capture, meaning it was created or first written before this capture's own window began, so its real pixel format is not recoverable from this log at all, only from a wider capture or a live hook. **One concrete, cheap path to close this fully, requiring the user's own action (running the game under an instrumented capture — dynamic analysis, not something decided by a peer relay or proceeded with unilaterally): hooking `CreateTexture(D3DUSAGE_RENDERTARGET)` to log format+size per returned pointer, cross-referenced against per-draw bound-texture lists, would tie every G-buffer slot to its real format in one run.** This is recorded here as a real, available option for the user to consider — not pursued, and not something this project's own agents will run without the user's own direct authorization in this session, per this project's standing dynamic-analysis rule. **Not blocking:** neither this project's own disassembly side nor Team B's reimplementation needs the exact source format resolved to proceed — Team B can pick a reasonable clean-room format for their own targets regardless of what the real one turns out to be.

**Evidence anchors (this subsection):** `FUN_009dd7b0`, `FUN_009de970`, `FUN_009ed6f0`, `FUN_009ee0d0` (all fully decompiled and refuted, this pass); derived-offset re-scan hits `FUN_00457370`, `FUN_0070cfe0`, `FUN_0070d110`, `FUN_0070d270`, `FUN_00b982b0`, `FUN_00b98570`, `FUN_00799850` (all unrelated or false-positive); `FUN_00472710` (confirmed `CreateTexture` wrapper, §4.1) and its complete 5-caller graph, `FUN_00472f40`/`FUN_00472e00`/`FUN_004739f0`, plus the generic texture-descriptor-pool mechanism (`DAT_03171b08`, per-slot stride `0xB8`, format cached at slot`+0x1c`); `FUN_0047c800` (device reset, §5). Real runtime cross-reference: an external runtime capture (dirty-side; findings summarized here, the source is not for clean-side consultation). Scripts: `tools/scratchpad/Frame12.java`–`Frame19.java`.
